HK manifolds of Type K ​ 3 [ a 2 + 1 ] K3^{[a^{2}+1]} as moduli spaces of projective bundles on HK manifolds of Type K ​ 3 [ 2 ] K3^{[2]} Thanks: Partially supported by PRIN 2017YRA3LK
Kieran G. O’Grady

Abstract
. We prove results on moduli spaces of ω \omega slope stable bundles of projective spaces on a hyperkähler (HK) manifold X X of Type K ​ 3 [ 2 ] K3^{[2]} (a HK manifold is of Type K ​ 3 [ m ] K3^{[m]} if it deforms to the Hilbert scheme S [ m ] S^{[m]} parametrizing length- m m subschemes of a K ​ 3 K3 surface S S ) with Kähler class ω \omega . Let X X be projective and h h be a (generic) ample class of X X . We prove that the moduli space M 𝐰 ¯ a ​ ( X , h ) M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h) parametrizing h h slope stable bundles with a suitable mock Mukai vector 𝐰 ¯ a \overline{{\bf w}}_{a} contains an irreducible component M 𝐰 ¯ a ​ ( X , h ) ∙ M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h)^{\bullet} whose normalization M ~ 𝐰 ¯ a ​ ( X , h ) ∙ \widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h)^{\bullet} is a (projective) HK manifold of Type K ​ 3 [ a 2 + 1 ] K3^{[a^{2}+1]} , and that conversely every projective HK manifold W W of Type K ​ 3 [ a 2 + 1 ] K3^{[a^{2}+1]} is isomorphic to M ~ 𝐰 ¯ a ​ ( X , h ) ∙ \widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h)^{\bullet} for a suitable ( X , h ) (X,h) as above. Moreover the universal bundle of projective spaces on X × M ~ 𝐰 ¯ a ​ ( X , h ) ∙ X\times\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h)^{\bullet} defines a vector bundle whose 2 ​ n ​ d 2nd Chern class defines a rational Hodge isometry H 2 ​ ( X ) → H 2 ​ ( M ~ 𝐰 ¯ a ​ ( X , h ) ∙ ) H^{2}(X)\to H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h)^{\bullet}) . From this and a result of Markman one gets that the analogue of the Shafarevich conjecture (a special case of the Hodge conjecture) holds for rational Hodge isometries H 2 ​ ( W 1 ) ⟶ H 2 ​ ( W 2 ) H^{2}(W_{1})\longrightarrow H^{2}(W_{2}) between projective HK manifolds W 1 , W 2 W_{1},W_{2} of Types K ​ 3 [ a 1 2 + 1 ] K3^{[a_{1}^{2}+1]} and K ​ 3 [ a 2 2 + 1 ] K3^{[a_{2}^{2}+1]} respectively. We prove results also for ( X , ω ) (X,\omega) a general HK manifold of Type K ​ 3 [ 2 ] K3^{[2]} . In fact one ingredient in our proof is Verbistsky’s theory of projectively hyperhomolorphic vector bundles.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Main results

In order to present our results we briefly discuss
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundles over a complex variety
X
.
Such a bundle is a holomorphic map
\rho\colon{\mathscr{P}}\to X
of complex spaces with fiber
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
which is locally trivial in the
classical
topology. One associates (see
[
HS03
, Sect. 2]
) to
{\mathscr{P}}
a class
\eta_{r}({\mathscr{P}})\in H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})
as follows.
The exact sequence of sheaves (for the classical topology)

1\longrightarrow\mu_{r}\longrightarrow\SL_{r}({\mathscr{O}}_{X})\longrightarrow\PGL_{r}({\mathscr{O}}_{X})\longrightarrow 1

defines an exact sequence of cohomology groups

(1.1.1)

H^{1}(X,\mu_{r})\longrightarrow H^{1}(X,\SL_{r}({\mathscr{O}}_{X}))\longrightarrow H^{1}(X,\PGL_{r}({\mathscr{O}}_{X}))\overset{\partial}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(X,\mu_{r}).

A local trivialization of
{\mathscr{P}}
gives a
1
-cocycle with values in
\PGL_{r}({\mathscr{O}}_{X})
, whose cohomology class
\gamma({\mathscr{P}})\in H^{1}(X,\PGL_{r}({\mathscr{O}}_{X}))
is independent of the trivialization. Set

(1.1.2)

\eta_{r}({\mathscr{P}})\coloneq\partial(\gamma({\mathscr{P}})).

Next we define a class
\Delta({\mathscr{P}})\in H^{4}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
.
Let

(1.1.3)

\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}})\coloneq\rho_{*}\Theta_{{\mathscr{P}}/Y}

be the pushforward of the bundle of vertical tangent vectors. If
U\subset X
is an open subset such that

\rho^{-1}(U)\xrightarrow{\sim}U\times{\mathbb{P}}(F)
, where
F\to U
is a (holomorphic) vector bundle of rank
r
then

(1.1.4)

\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}})_{|U}\cong End^{0}(F),

where
End^{0}(F)
is the vector bundle of traceless endomorphisms of
F
. Thus
\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}})
is locally-free of rank
r^{2}-1
.
Set

(1.1.5)

\Delta({\mathscr{P}})\coloneq c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}})).

If
(X,\omega)
is a compact Kähler manifold there is the notion of
\omega
-slope stability for projective bundles on
X
, see Subsection
2.3
. Fix a
mock Mukai vector

(1.1.6)

\overline{{\bf w}}=(r,\eta,s)\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}\times H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})\times H^{2,2}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X).

There is a moduli space
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(X,\omega)
(an analytic space) of
\omega
-slope stable projective
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundles
{\mathscr{P}}\to X
with

\eta_{r}({\mathscr{P}})=\eta
and
\Delta({\mathscr{P}})=s
.

Next we define, for
a\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, a mock Mukai vector
\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}
for a HK manifold
X
of Type
K3^{[2]}
as follows. Let

q_{X}
be the Beauville-Bogomolov-Fujiki (BBF) quadratic form of
X
(
q_{X}
denotes both the BBF quadratic form and its polarization), and

(1.1.7)

\overline{\gamma}_{a}=[4a^{2}\widehat{\gamma}_{a}]\in H^{2}(X,\mu_{8a^{3}})\cong H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})/8a^{3}H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})

where
\widehat{\gamma}_{a}=[\gamma_{a}]\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})/2aH^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})
with
\gamma_{a}\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
such that

(1.1.8)

\divisore(\gamma_{a})=1,\qquad q_{X}(\gamma_{a})\equiv 2a^{2}-2\pmod{4a}.

Then

(1.1.9)

\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}\coloneq(8a^{3},\overline{\gamma}_{a},\frac{4a^{6}}{3}c_{2}(X)).

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
X
be hyperkähler (HK) manifold of Type
K3^{[2]}
. Let
\omega
be a Kähler class on
X
such that

(1.1.10)

\{\xi\in H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)\mid q_{X}(\omega,\xi)=0,\quad-2^{16}\cdot 5\cdot a^{18}\leq q_{X}(\xi)<0\}=\emptyset,

The following hold:

(1)

M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)
has an irreducible component
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
whose normalization is a holomorphic symplectic manifold
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
bimeromorphic to a HK manifold of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
(and deformation equivalent to such a manifold).

(2)

If
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)=\{0\}
, or
X
is projective and
\omega=c_{1}(L)
for an ample line bundle
L
, then

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
is a HK manifold of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
.

(3)

Let
\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
be the pull-back to
X\times\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
of the universal
{\mathbb{P}}^{8a^{3}-1}
-bundle on
X\times M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
, and let
\mathfrak{g}(\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\bullet}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}})
be the corresponding holomorphic vector bundle on

X\times\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
. Let

(1.1.11)

c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}(\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\bullet}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}))^{2,2}\in H^{2}(X)\otimes H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet})

be the Künneth
(2,2)
-component of
c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}(\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\bullet}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}))
, which we view as an element
of
H^{2}(X)^{\vee}\otimes H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet})
via the
isomorphism
H^{2}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(X)^{\vee}
defined by the (non degenerate) quadratic form
q_{X}
.
Then

(1.1.12)

32^{-1}\cdot a^{-4}\cdot c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}(\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\bullet}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}))^{2,2}\colon H^{2}(X)\longrightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet})

is a rational Hodge isometry, i.e. an isomorphism of rational Hodge structures matching the BBF quadratic forms (this make sense even if
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
is not Kähler because of Item (1)).

Remark 1.2
.

We do not know whether
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)
is irreducible. Independently of this open problem,

M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
can be described as follows: it is obtained by deformation of a well-defined irreducible component of a moduli space of vector bundles on a suitably polarized
S^{[2]}
where
S
is a
K3
surface (see Subsection
1.3
). In theory monodromy could “generate” more than one such irreducible component.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
W
be HK manifold of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
, where
a
is a positive integer. There exist a HK manifold
X
of Type
K3^{[2]}
and a Kähler class
\omega
on
X
for which (
1.1.10
) holds, such that

W
is bimeromorphic to

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
, where the latter is as in Item (1) of Theorem
1.1
. If
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(W)=\{0\}
,
then there exist
(X,\omega)
as above such that
W
is isomorphic to

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
. If
W
is projective then there exists a polarized HK manifold
(X,h)
of Type
K3^{[2]}

(for which (
1.1.10
) holds with
\omega
replaced by
h
) such that
W
is isomorphic to

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h)^{\bullet}
.

Remark 1.4
.

Let
m\geq 2
. If
X
is a HK manifold of Type
K3^{[m]}
then
H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})
equipped with the BBF quadratic form is isometric to

(1.1.13)

\Lambda_{m}\coloneq U^{\oplus 3}\oplus(-E_{8})^{\oplus 2}\oplus(-2(m-1)),

where direct sums are orthogonal,
U
is the hyperbolic lattice,
-E_{8}
is the unique rank-
8
unimodular even negative definite lattice, and

(-2(m-1))
is
{\mathbb{Z}}
with generator of square
-2(m-1)
. If
m,n\geq 2
and there is an isometry

\Lambda_{m}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
, then
(m-1)/(n-1)
is the square of a rational number because the discriminants of
\Lambda_{m},\Lambda_{n}
are equal to
2(m-1),2(n-1)
.
This shows the necessity of the “Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
” in the statements of
Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
. It suggests that it should be possible to generalize the statements above
with “Type
K3^{[2]}
” and “Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
” replaced by “Type
K3^{[m]}
” and “Type
K3^{[a^{2}(m-1)+1]}
” respectively.

Remark 1.5
.

In
[
BS07
]
the authors define a Kähler form on the moduli space of
\omega
slope stable principal bundles on a (compact) Kähler manifold
(X,\omega)
(see also
[
Ver96
, Thm. 6.3]
). If this gives a Kähler structure also on a singular moduli space (a statement which sounds plausible) then Item (2) of Theorem
1.1
holds for any
X
, and the isomorphism

W\cong\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
in Theorem
1.3
holds for any
HK manifold
W
of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
.

Remark 1.6
.

A natural question is the following. Given a HK manifold
W
of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
, how many are the isomorphism (or bimeromorphic equivalence) classes of
HK manifolds
X
of Type
K3^{[2]}
such that
W
is isomorphic to

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
? This brings to mind the degree of the maps
{\mathscr{F}}_{2n^{2}k}\to{\mathscr{F}}_{2k}
between moduli spaces of polarized
K3
surfaces of degrees
2n^{2}k
and
2k
, see
[
OG86
, Ch. 2]
.

Theorem 1.7
.

Let
W_{i}
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
, be projective HK manifolds of Types
K3^{[a_{i}^{2}+1]}
(
a_{i}
are integers), and suppose that there exists a rational Hodge isometry

f\colon H^{2}(W_{1})\longrightarrow H^{2}(W_{2}).

There exists an algebraic cycle class
Z\in\CH^{2a_{1}^{2}+2}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{1}\times W_{2})
whose cohomology class belongs to
H^{4a_{1}^{2}+2}(W_{1};{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes H^{2}(W_{2};{\mathbb{Q}})\cong H^{2}(W_{1};{\mathbb{Q}})^{\vee}\otimes H^{2}(W_{2};{\mathbb{Q}})
and equals
f
.

1.2.
Background and motivation

Every known HK manifold is, up to deformation, a moduli space (possibly desingularized) of semistable sheaves on a
K3
or abelian surface
S

(in the latter case we factor out
S
and
S^{\vee}
from the Beauville-Bogomolov decomposition of the moduli space). The key proviso in the last sentence is “up to deformation”. A smooth moduli space of semistable sheaves on a
K3
surface
S
is of Type
K3^{[m]}
. If
m\geq 2
such a HK manifold has
21
moduli (
20
moduli as projective manifold), while
S
has
20
moduli (respectively
19
moduli as projective manifold). Similar considerations apply to HK manifolds of Type OG10 (the difference between the number of deformations of OG10 and a
K3
surface is
2
), or to HK manifolds of Type
\Kum_{m}
(
m\geq 2
) or of Type OG6 (the difference between the number of deformations of
\Kum_{m}
or OG6 and those of a two-dimensional complex torus is
1
and
2
respectively).

The strength of our Theorem
1.3
is that it realizes an arbitrary projective HK manifold of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
as a moduli space of projective bundles on a (projective) HK manifold of Type
K3^{[2]}
. One may compare our result to
[
BL+21
, Cor. 29.5]
stating that, given coprime integers
a,b
, the
moduli spaces of Bridgeland stable objects in the Kuznetsov components of general cubic fourfolds with a suitable Mukai vector
v(a,b)

give a locally complete family of polarized HK manifolds
(Y,h)
of Type
K3^{[a^{2}-ab+b^{2}+1]}
. Since objects in the Kuznetsov component of a cubic fourfold
W
are objects in the derived category of
W
, we see a similarity with our results
on (twisted) vector bundles on a HK fourfold. On the other hand Kuznetsov components are
2
-dimensional CY categories: this accounts for the fact that their moduli spaces are smooth, and the irreducibility statement in their result.
To our advantage there is the possibility of considering moduli space of slope stable bundles of projective spaces on arbitrary HK manifolds, and hence using Verbitsky’s powerful theory of projectively hyperholomorphic (twisted) vector bundles.

The main effort in the proof of our results goes into studying moduli spaces of slope stable vector bundles with certain characteristic classes on the Hilbert scheme
S^{[2]}
, where
S
is a projective
K3
surface, see Subsection
1.3
. Recently there has been a surge of interest in moduli spaces of semistable sheaves on
HK
manifolds. The author realized
[
O’G22a
]
that if one deals with so-called modular (torsion-free) sheaves then variation of slope-stability behaves as if the HK manifold were a surface, and that stable modular sheaves on Lagrangian-fibered HK manifolds behave similarly to stable sheaves on elliptic
K3
surfaces (up to a point). This was used to prove results on slope stable rigid vector bundles on higher dimensional
HK
manifolds which are reminiscent of known results valid for
K3
surfaces, see loc. cit. and
[
OG24a
,
OG24b
]
. Markman
[
Mar24a
]
and Beckmann
[
Bec25
]
introduced the important notion of atomic sheaf, or complex of sheaves, on a
HK
manifold. Atomic sheaves are key ingredients in Markman’s proof of the Shafarevich
conjecture for couples of HK manifolds (both) of Type
K3^{[m]}

[
Mar24b
]
. Bottini
[
Bot24a
,
Bot24b
]
proved that certain moduli spaces of (twisted) atomic sheaves on HK manifolds of Type
K3^{[2]}
have an irreducible component which is a HK manifold of Type OG10. All of the sheaves (which are actually locally free) that appear in the works mentioned above are atomic. In the present work the sheaves are modular but
not
atomic. On the other hand, they are (again) locally free
1
1

1

Where have all the singular sheaves gone?
and projectively hyperholomorphic.

We feel that the results of the present paper (and those in
[
OG26
]
) give a glimpse of the theory of sheaves on HK manifolds of Type
K3^{[2]}
, and that it reinforces the impression that it resembles the theory of sheaves on
K3
surfaces. In fact we get moduli spaces which are of arbitrarily large dimension and which contain a HK component (up to normalization). On the other hand it is not clear (at least to me) what the
actual picture might be. We do not know the answer to the most basic questions: when does there exist a slope stable (twisted) vector bundle with assigned characteristic classes? Is there a reasonable expected dimension of the moduli space of stable sheaves with given mock Mukai vector (but see
[
Bot24a
, Subsect. 3.4]
)? Are HK (components of) moduli spaces of semistable sheaves on HK manifolds necessarily deformations of the known HK manifolds?

1.3.
Outline of the proofs

The starting point is the following. Let
S
be a
K3
surface, and suppose that
{\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
are vector bundles on
S
such that

(1.3.1)

r({\mathscr{E}}_{2})\cdot c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})=r({\mathscr{E}}_{1})\cdot c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{2}),\qquad\langle v({\mathscr{E}}_{1}),v({\mathscr{E}}_{2})\rangle=0,

where
r({\mathscr{E}}_{i}),v({\mathscr{E}}_{i})
are the rank and Mukai vector of
{\mathscr{E}}_{i}
respectively.
The involution of
S^{2}
exchanging the factors lifts to an involution of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
, and hence the Bridgeland-King-Reid (BKR) equivalence associates to
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
a vector bundle

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
on
S^{[2]}
.
In
[
OG26
, Prop. 2.7]
we proved that the discriminant of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is a multiple of
c_{2}(S^{[2]})
. Thus
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is modular, and if it is slope-stable then it is projectively hyperholomorphic.

Suppose that
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is spherical with
r({\mathscr{E}}_{1})=2a
. If

v({\mathscr{E}}_{2})=av_{1}-(0,0,1)
then the equalities in (
1.3.1
) hold. Let
h_{S}
be a polarization of
S
. Assume that
{\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
are (Giesker-Maruyama) stable.
Because of the second equality in (
1.3.1
) we expect

(1.3.2)

\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})=\{0\}

if
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is general. If that is the case then every small deformation of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is isomorphic to

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})
for a small deformation
{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}
of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
, and one identifies the deformation space of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
with that of

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
, see
[
OG26
, Prop. 2.6]
.
The upshot is that if for some
h_{S}
-stable
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
the equality in (
1.3.2
) holds and the vector bundle
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is stable for a polarization
h_{S^{[2]}}
of
S^{[2]}
, then we have a birational map

(1.3.3)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\coloneq{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}(S,h_{S})&\dashrightarrow&M_{{\bf w}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}\eqcolon M^{\bullet}_{{\bf w}_{a}}\\
\qquad\qquad[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]&\mapsto&[{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})]\qquad\quad\end{matrix}

where
v(a)\coloneq av_{1}-(0,0,1)
,
{\bf w}_{a}
is the mock Mukai vector (see
[
OG26
, Subsec. 1.2]
) associated to the vector bundles

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
, and
M_{{\bf w}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
is an irreducible component of the moduli space
M_{{\bf w}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
of
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable vector bundles on
S^{[2]}
with mock Mukai vector
{\bf w}_{a}
.
Note that
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
is a (projective) HK manifold of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
.
In loc. cit. we proved that if
a>1
and

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
is general, then
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is
h_{S^{[2]}}
(slope) stable for suitable
h_{S^{[2]}}
. In the present paper we determine for which
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
the sheaf
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is semistable, and what are the semistable replacements
of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
for the remaining

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. A key rôle is played by the (determinantal) locus
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
parametrizing sheaves
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
such that (
1.3.2
) is violated, or equivalently such that
\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\not=0
. This is an irreducible divisor containing all points parametrizing non locally free sheaves.
If
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)})
then
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is slope stable and locally free, and this implies that
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is

{\bm{\mu}}(h_{S})
slope stable, where
{\bm{\mu}}(h_{S})
is the pull-back of the symmetrization of
h_{S}
on
S^{(2)}
via the Hilbert-to-Chow map
S^{[2]}\to S^{(2)}
(here
h_{S}
is an
{\mathsf{a}}(v(a))
-generic polarization of
S
).
Note that
{\bm{\mu}}(h_{S})
is big and nef, but not ample. One of the virtues of modular sheaves such as
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is that variation of slope stability behaves as on surfaces. It follows that
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is slope stable for any polarization
h_{S^{[2]}}
close enough to
{\bm{\mu}}(h_{S})
, where “close enough” is determined by
a
.
This proves that we have a regular map

(1.3.4)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}&\xrightarrow{\psi}&M^{\bullet}_{{\bf w}_{a}}\\
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]&\mapsto&[{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})]\end{matrix}

defining an isomorphism between
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}
and an open
dense subset of
M_{{\bf w}_{a}}^{\bullet}
.

In the end we prove that
\psi
extends to a regular map

(1.3.5)

\overline{\psi}\colon{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\to M_{{\bf w}_{a}}^{\bullet}

although
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is slope unstable
for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}
. Thus
\overline{\psi}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])
represents the isomorphism class of a vector bundle which is
not
isomorphic to

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
. In order to explain what goes on we need to give more details on the divisor
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. Let

{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(a)}\subset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}
be the (closed) subset of points
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]
such that
\dim\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\geq k
. We have the (Brill-Noether) decreasing chain of closed subsets of
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
.

{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}={\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{1}\supset\ldots\supset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k}\supset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k+1}\supset\ldots\supset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{a}.

Let
1\leq k<a
and let

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k+1})
. The map
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}
is surjective, and it induces a surjection
\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{+}\colon{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{k}
, where

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}
is the rigid slope stable vector bundle studied in
[
O’G22a
]
, and
V_{k}\coloneq\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}
.
Let
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\coloneq\ker\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{+}
. The exact sequence

(1.3.6)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\overset{\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{-}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0,

is slope desemistabilizing for any polarization of
S^{[2]}
.
The map
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}
is no longer surjective if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{a}
, but there is an exact sequence similar to (
1.3.6
) which is slope desemistabiling for any polarization of
S^{[2]}
. Since
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}
is slope stable, the first step of semistable reduction for a one-parameter family of semistable sheaves specializing to
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
(given by a suitable elementary modification) replaces

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
by a sheaf
\widetilde{{\mathscr{G}}}
sitting into an exact sequence obtained by switching the two sides of (
1.3.6
):

(1.3.7)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathscr{G}}}\overset{\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{-}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow 0.

We prove that those extensions (
1.3.7
) which actually occur after elementary modification of a one-parameter family in a general direction normal to
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k+1}
(the latter space is smooth) are slope stable, provided that
h_{S}
is an
{\mathsf{a}}(v(a))
-generic polarization of
S
and the polarization of
S^{[2]}
is close enough to
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
. Moreover the isomorphism class of
\widetilde{{\mathscr{G}}}
depends on
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
but
not
on the direction normal to
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k+1}
.
Note that if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{a}
then
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is not locally free, but the sheaves that we get after the elementary modifications are locally free (pleasant surprise!).

The actual proof that
\psi
extends regularly to all of
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
goes as follows. Let
\widehat{f}\colon\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}_{v(a)}\to{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
be the birational map obtained by blowing up
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{a}
(which is smooth), blowing up the strict transform of
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{a-1}
(which is smooth) etc. Then
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}_{v(a)}
contains smooth prime divisors
\widehat{E}^{a},\ldots,\widehat{E}^{1}
such that

\widehat{f}(\widehat{E}^{k})={\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}^{k}
, the divisor

\widehat{E}^{a}+\ldots+\widehat{E}^{1}
has simple normal crossings, and

\widehat{f}
identifies
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}_{v(a)}\setminus(\widehat{E}^{a}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{1})
with
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}
. For simplicity we assume that there is a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
and hence also a sheaf
{\mathbb{G}}
on

S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
which is
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
-flat and such that
{\mathbb{G}}_{|S^{[2]}\times\{[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\}}\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
for every

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. Let
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}
be the pull-back of
{\mathbb{G}}
to
S^{[2]}\times\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}_{v(a)}
. One performs elementary modifications starting from
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}
, and the final result is a vector bundle
\widetilde{{\mathbb{G}}}
on
S^{[2]}\times\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}_{v(a)}

which restricts to a slope stable vector bundle on
S^{[2]}\times\{x\}
for every
x\in\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}_{v(a)}
, and
which is isomorphic to
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}
on
S^{[2]}\times({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)})
.
This proves that the map
\psi
in (
1.3.4
) extends to a regular map
\widehat{\psi}\colon\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}_{v(a)}\to M_{{\bf w}_{a}}^{\bullet}
.
If
x\in(\widehat{E}^{k}\setminus\widehat{E}^{k+1})
then the isomorphism class of
\widetilde{{\mathbb{G}}}_{S^{[2]}\times\{x\}}
depends only on
\widehat{f}(x)\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(a)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}_{v(a)})
, and is represented by a vector bundle fitting into (
1.3.7
). This proves that
\widehat{\psi}
descends to
a regular map
\overline{\psi}\colon{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\to M_{{\bf w}_{a}}^{\bullet}
extending
\psi
. The explicit description of the vector bundles parametrized by
\overline{\psi}({\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)})
gives that
\overline{\psi}
is a bijection. Thus
\overline{\psi}
identifies
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
with the normalization

\widetilde{M}_{{\bf w}_{a}}^{\bullet}
of

M_{{\bf w}_{a}}^{\bullet}
.

Items (1) and (2) of Theorem
1.1
follows from the existence of the extension
\overline{\psi}
in (
1.3.5
) together with Verbitsky’s fundamental results on projectively hyperholomorphic vector bundles. In fact, since

r({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}))=8a^{3},\quad\eta_{8a^{3}}({\mathbb{P}}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}))=[4a^{2}({\bm{\mu}}(D)-a\delta)]\pmod{8a^{3}H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})},

(see (
4.2.4
) and (
4.2.5
) for the notation in the second equation),

c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}({\mathbb{P}}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}))=\frac{4a^{6}}{3}c_{2}(S^{[2]}),

and
the projectivization of a slope stable vector bundle is a slope stable (with respect to the same polarization) bundle of projective spaces, we may view
M_{{\bf w}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
as a closed subset
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
of the moduli space
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
. By Verbitsky’s results every
{\mathbb{P}}^{8a^{3}-1}
-bundle
{\mathscr{P}}_{0}
parametrized by a point of

M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
extends to an
\omega_{t}
slope stable
{\mathbb{P}}^{8a^{3}-1}
-bundle
{\mathscr{P}}_{t}
on a(n arbitrary) twistor deformation
(X_{t},\omega_{t})
of
(S^{[2]},\omega_{0})
, where
\omega_{0}
is any Kähler class in an open
2^{16}\cdot 5\cdot a^{18}
-chamber containing
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
in its closure. Moreover the local (anaytic) structure of the moduli space

M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}(t)}(X_{t},\omega_{t})
at
{\mathscr{P}}_{t}
is isomorphic to that of
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\omega_{0})
at
{\mathscr{P}}_{0}
. This, together with some extra arguments, gives Item (1) and (2) of Theorem
1.1
because every deformation of
S^{[2]}
can be reached by a chain of twistor families.

In order to prove Item (3) of Theorem
1.1
it suffices to analyze the case of the pull-back of the universal
{\mathbb{P}}^{8a^{3}-1}
-bundle to
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. We may assume that there is a universal sheaf
{\mathbb{E}}
on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. The relevant computation is done via the Mukai map
v(a)^{\bot}\to H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)})
associated to
{\mathbb{E}}
.

Theorem
1.3
is proved as follows. Let
(X_{0},\omega_{0})
be a HK manifold of Type
K3^{[2]}
with Kähler class

\omega_{0}
such that
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{0},\omega_{0})^{\bullet}
is a HK of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
(e.eg.
X_{0}
projective and
\omega_{0}
an ample class).
There exists a chain of generic twistor families deforming

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{0},\omega_{0})^{\bullet}
to
W
.
Applying Item (3) of Theorem
1.1
one gets that there are corresponding (generic) twistor families deforming
(X_{0},\omega_{0})
to

(X,\omega)
in such a way that
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}

is bimeromorphic to
W
. If
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(W)=\{0\}
one gets that the bimeromorphic map is an isomorphism.
If
W
is projective a more refined argument is needed to prove isomorphism.

Theorem
1.7
is proved by combining Item (3) of Theorem
1.1
and (the case
m=2
of) Markman’s Theorem
[
Mar24b
, Thm. 1.1]
to the effect that the Shafarevich
conjecture for couples of HK manifolds (both) of Type
K3^{[m]}
holds.

1.4.
Organization of the paper

In Section
2
we discuss variation of slope stability for modular sheaves. This subject has been treated before for ample classes. Here we extend the discussion to arbitrary nef classes.
We also discuss twisted (locally free) sheaves and variation of slope stability for such sheaves.

Section
3
is devoted to the moduli spaces
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
(and also
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
for
1\leq t<a
) and the Brill-Noether stratification of the determinantal divisor
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a)}\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. The results are analogous to those in
[
Mar01
]
, but we cannot refer to Markman’s paper because the Mukai vector
v(a)
has divisible second entry (unless
a=1
).

Section
4
contains all the slope stability results for vector bundles on
S^{[2]}
that we will be using. Subsection
4.1
is a detailed road map for the section.

Section
5
contains the key result on moduli spaces of slope stable sheaves on
S^{[2]}
that are needed to prove Item (1) of Theorem
1.1
. A detailed road map is in Subsection
5.2
.

In Section
6
we carry out the computations needed to prove Item (2) of Theorem
1.1
(once Item (1) has been proved).

Section
7
contains the proofs of the main results. It consists of applying to the results of Sections
5
and
6
Verbitsky’s theory of projectively hyperholomorphic (twisted) vector bundles, coupled with the properties of twistor families.

1.5.
Notation

Schemes, varieties etc. are over
{\mathbb{C}}
. Sheaves are coherent sheaves unless we state differently.
If
{\mathscr{A}},{\mathscr{B}}
are sheaves on a variety
X
we let

\ext^{p}_{X}({\mathscr{A}},{\mathscr{B}})\coloneq\dim\Ext^{p}_{X}({\mathscr{A}},{\mathscr{B}}),\qquad\hom_{X}({\mathscr{A}},{\mathscr{B}})\coloneq\dim\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{A}},{\mathscr{B}}).

A HK variety (in this paper) is a projective HK manifold.
We denote complex vector spaces of dimension
d
by

V_{d}
,
W_{d}
etc.

1.6.
Acknowledgements

Many thanks go to Francesco Meazzini for several conversations on the subject of (projectively) hyperholomorphic vector bundles. Alessio Bottini and Emanuele Macrì have made helpful comments to a preliminary version.

2.
HK-slope stability of (twisted) sheaves

2.1.
Slope stability of sheaves

Let
X
be a smooth projective irreducible variety of dimension
m
. Let
h\in\NS(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\coloneq\NS(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
. The
h
slope
of a sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
on
X

of positive rank
r({\mathscr{F}})
is

(2.1.1)

\mu_{h}({\mathscr{F}})\coloneq\int_{X}\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{F}})\cdot h^{m-1}}{r({\mathscr{F}})}.

One may define
h
slope (semi)stability as in the case of ample
h
.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a torsion-free sheaf on
X
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h
slope semistable
if for all subsheaves
{\mathscr{E}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
with
0<r({\mathscr{E}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
we have

\mu_{h}({\mathscr{E}})\leq\mu_{h}({\mathscr{F}})
.
If strict inequality holds for all such
{\mathscr{E}}
then
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h
slope stable
.

Now assume that
X
is a HK manifold of dimension
2n
. Let
h\in\NS(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a sheaf on
X
of positive rank
r({\mathscr{F}})
. The
h
HK-slope
of
{\mathscr{F}}
is given by

(2.1.2)

\mu_{h}^{HK}({\mathscr{F}})\coloneq q_{X}\left(\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{F}})}{r({\mathscr{F}})},h\right).

Definition 2.2
.

A torsion-free (non-zero) sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
on
X
is
h
HK-slope semistable
if for all subsheaves
{\mathscr{E}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
with
0<r({\mathscr{E}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
we have
\mu_{h}^{HK}({\mathscr{E}})\leq\mu_{h}^{HK}({\mathscr{F}})
.
If strict inequality holds for all such
{\mathscr{E}}
then
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h
HK-slope stable
.

Let
q_{X}
be the Beauville-Bogomolov-Fujiki quadratic form of
X
, and let
c_{X}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}
be the
(small) Fujiki constant of
X
. Then for all
\alpha\in H^{2}(X)

(2.1.3)

\int_{X}\alpha^{2n}=(2n-1)!!c_{X}q_{X}(\alpha)^{n}.

Remark 2.3
.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a torsion-free (non-zero) sheaf on
X
. Let
h\in\NS(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
, and suppose that
q_{X}(h)>0
, e.g. if
h
is ample. By Fujiki’s formula (
2.1.3
)

\int_{X}c_{1}({\mathscr{F}})\cdot h^{2n-1}=(2n-1)!!c_{X}q_{X}(c_{1}({\mathscr{F}}),h)\cdot q_{X}(h)^{n-1}.

Thus
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h
HK-slope (semi)stable if and only if it is
h
slope (semi)stable.

Remark 2.4
.

Suppose that
X
has a Lagrangian fibration
\pi\colon X\to{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
. See
[
OG26
, Prop. 5.9]
for the meaning of
h
HK-slope (semi)stability
if
h\coloneq\pi^{*}c_{1}({\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{n}}(1))
.

2.2.
Variation of HK-slope (semi)stability for modular sheaves

The
discriminant
of a sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
on a manifold
X
is the characteristic class given by

(2.2.1)

\Delta({\mathscr{F}})\coloneq 2r({\mathscr{F}})c_{2}({\mathscr{F}})-(r({\mathscr{F}})-1)c_{1}({\mathscr{F}})^{2}=\ch_{1}({\mathscr{F}})^{2}-2r({\mathscr{F}})\ch_{2}({\mathscr{F}}).

Let
X
be a HK manifold of dimension
2n
. A torsion-free sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
on
X

is
modular
if
there exists
d({\mathscr{F}})\in{\mathbb{Q}}
such that for all
\alpha\in H^{2}(X)

(2.2.2)

\int_{X}\Delta({\mathscr{F}})\cdot\alpha^{2n-2}=d({\mathscr{F}})(2n-3)!!q_{X}(\alpha)^{n-1}.

If
{\mathscr{F}}
is modular we let

(2.2.3)

{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})\coloneq r({\mathscr{F}})^{2}\cdot d({\mathscr{F}})/4c_{X}

Remark 2.5
.

A torsion-free sheaf on a HK manifold
X
whose discriminant is a multiple of
c_{2}(X)
is modular. If
X
is of type

K3^{[n]}
and
\Delta({\mathscr{F}})=kc_{2}(X)
then

(2.2.4)

d({\mathscr{F}})=6(n+1)k,\qquad{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})=3k(n+1)r({\mathscr{F}})^{2}/2.

Let
S
be a
K3
surface and let
v\in\widetilde{H}^{1,1}(S)
be a Mukai vector with positive first entry (here
\widetilde{H}(S)
is the Mukai lattice of
S
). We let

{\mathsf{a}}(v)\coloneq{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})

where
{\mathscr{F}}
is any sheaf such that
v({\mathscr{F}})=v
. If
v=(r,l,s)
then

(2.2.5)

{\mathsf{a}}(v)=r^{2}(v^{2}+2r^{2})/4.

If
X
is a HK manifold of dimension at least
4
and
{\bf w}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}\times\NS(X)\times H^{2,2}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)
is a mock Mukai vector (see
[
OG26
, Sect. 1.2]
) we let
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w})\coloneq{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})

where
{\mathscr{F}}
is any sheaf such that
{\bf w}({\mathscr{F}})={\bf w}
.

Definition 2.6
.

Let
X
be a HK variety, and let
h_{0}\in\Nef(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
Let
{\mathsf{a}}>0
. An ample class
h\in\Amp(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
is
{\mathsf{a}}
-suitable for
h_{0}
if for all

\lambda\in\NS(X)
such that
-{\mathsf{a}}\leq q_{X}(\lambda)<0
one of the following holds:

(1)

q_{X}(\lambda,h)>0
and
q_{X}(\lambda,h_{0})\geq 0
.

(2)

q_{X}(\lambda,h)=0
and
q_{X}(\lambda,h_{0})=0
.

(3)

q_{X}(\lambda,h)<0
and
q_{X}(\lambda,h_{0})\leq 0
.

Remark 2.7
.

Let
{\mathsf{a}}
be a positive natural number. A codimension-
1
real subspace
W\subset\NS(X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\coloneq\NS(X)\otimes{\mathbb{R}}
is an
{\mathsf{a}}
-wall if
W=\xi^{\bot}
(orthogonality with respect to the BBF quadratic form
q_{X}
) where
\xi\in\NS(X)
is such that

-{\mathsf{a}}\leq q_{X}(\xi)<0
. The intersections of the
{\mathsf{a}}
-walls with the cone
{\mathscr{C}}(X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\subset\Nef(X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}
of classes with positive square form a locally finite collection of codimension-
1
submanifolds, and hence their union is closed in
{\mathscr{C}}(X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}
. A connected component of

(2.2.6)

{\mathscr{C}}(X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\setminus\bigcup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi\in\NS(X)}}{{-{\mathsf{a}}\leq q_{X}(\xi)<0}}}\xi^{\bot}

is an

open
{\mathsf{a}}
-chamber
. An ample class
h\in\Amp(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
is
{\mathsf{a}}
-generic
if it does not belong to any
{\mathsf{a}}
-wall, i.e. it belongs to a (unique) open
{\mathsf{a}}
-chamber. Let
h_{0},h_{1}
be
{\mathsf{a}}
-generic ample classes which belong to the same
open
{\mathsf{a}}
-chamber: then
h_{0}
is
{\mathsf{a}}
-suitable for
h_{1}
and viceversa.

For sheaves
{\mathscr{E}},{\mathscr{F}}
on
X
let

(2.2.7)

\lambda_{{\mathscr{E}},{\mathscr{F}}}\coloneq r({\mathscr{F}})\cdot c_{1}({\mathscr{E}})-r({\mathscr{E}})\cdot c_{1}({\mathscr{F}}).

Proposition 2.8
.

Let
X
be a HK variety, and let
h_{0}\in\Nef(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
Let
{\mathsf{a}}>0
. Suppose that
h\in\Amp(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
is
{\mathsf{a}}
-suitable for
h_{0}
.
Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a torsion free modular sheaf on
X
which is

h_{0}
HK-slope semistable but not
h
HK-slope stable, and
that
{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})\leq{\mathsf{a}}
.
Then there exists a subsheaf
{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
, with
0<r({\mathscr{H}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
, such that
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)\geq 0
and
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
.

Proof.

This has been proved for
X
with a Lagrangian fibration
\pi\colon X\to{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
and

h_{0}=\pi^{*}c_{1}({\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{n}}(1))
, see
[
OG26
, Prop. 5.9, Item (a)]
. A similar argument gives a proof of
Proposition
2.8
. We spell out the proof for the reader’s convenience.

First assume that
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h
HK-slope semistable. Since
{\mathscr{F}}
is not
h
HK-slope stable there exists a subsheaf
{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
such that
0<r({\mathscr{H}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
and

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)=0
. By
[
O’G22a
, Prop. 3.10]
we get that
-{\mathsf{a}}\leq-{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})\leq q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}})\leq 0
.
If
\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}}=0
then
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
trivially. If
\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}}\not=0
then

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}})<0
(the restriction of
q_{X}
to
h^{\bot}\cap\NS(X)
is negative definite). Thus

(2.2.8)

-{\mathsf{a}}\leq q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}})<0.

Since
h
is
{\mathsf{a}}
-suitable for
h_{0}
it follows that

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
.

Next assume that
{\mathscr{F}}
is not
h
HK-slope semistable. Thus there exists
{\mathscr{G}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
such that
0<r({\mathscr{G}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
and

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)>0
. If
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
we are done, hence we may assume that

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})\not=0
. Since
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h_{0}
HK-slope semistable we have
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{F}}},f)<0
.
Let
\mathsf{S}
be the set of rational numbers
s\in(0,1)
for which there exists a subsheaf
{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
, with
0<r({\mathscr{H}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
, such that

(2.2.9)

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},(1-s)h+sh_{0})=0.

Then
\mathsf{S}
is non empty because there exists a rational number
s\in(0,1)
for which (
2.2.9
) holds with
{\mathscr{H}}={\mathscr{G}}
.
We claim that
\mathsf{S}
is finite. In fact if (
2.2.9
) holds with
s\in(0,1)
then
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)\geq 0
, because

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})\leq 0
(
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h_{0}
HK-slope semistable). The inequality
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)\geq 0
holds if and only if

\mu_{h}({\mathscr{H}})\geq\mu_{h}({\mathscr{F}})
because
h
is ample (see Remark
2.3
).
Since the set of subsheaves
{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
such that

\mu_{h}({\mathscr{H}})\geq\mu_{h}({\mathscr{F}})
is bounded it follows that
\mathsf{S}
is finite. Hence
\mathsf{S}
has a maximum

s_{*}
. Let
h_{*}\coloneq(1-s_{*})h+s_{*}f
.

Suppose that
{\mathscr{F}}
is not
h_{*}
HK-slope semistable. Then there exists a subsheaf

{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
with
0<r({\mathscr{H}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
such that
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{*})>0
. If
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})<0
then there exists
s\in(s_{*},1)
such that (
2.2.9
) holds, and this is a contradiction because
s_{*}
is the maximum of
\mathsf{S}
. Thus
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
(
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h_{0}
HK-slope semistable).
Since
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{*})>0
and
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
, we get that
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)>0
and hence we are done.

Lastly suppose that
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h_{*}
HK-slope semistable. Then there exists a subsheaf

{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
with
0<r({\mathscr{H}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
such that
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{*})=0
. We claim that

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
. Granting this for the moment being, we get that
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)=0
because

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{*})=0
, and hence we are done. We finish by proving that
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
. Suppose that

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})\not=0
.
By
[
O’G22a
, Prop. 3.10]
we have
-{\mathsf{a}}\leq-{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})\leq q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}})\leq 0
, and since
\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}}\not=0

(because
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})\not=0
) we get that (
2.2.8
) holds.
Since
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})\not=0
we have

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})<0
(
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h_{0}
HK-slope semistable). Since (
2.2.8
) holds and
h
is
{\mathsf{a}}
-suitable for
h_{0}
, it follows that

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)<0
. The inequalities
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})<0

and
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)<0
contradict the equality

q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{*})=0
. Thus the hypothesis
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})\not=0
leads to a contradiction.
∎

Corollary 2.9
.

Let
X
be a HK variety, and let
h_{0}\in\Nef(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
Let
{\mathsf{a}}>0
. Suppose that
h\in\Amp(X)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
is
{\mathsf{a}}
-suitable for
h_{0}
.
Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a torsion free modular sheaf on
X
such that
{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}})\leq{\mathsf{a}}
. If
{\mathscr{F}}
is
h_{0}
HK-slope stable
then it is

h
HK-slope stable.

Proof.

Suppose that
{\mathscr{F}}
is not
h
HK-slope stable. By Proposition
2.8
there exists a subsheaf
{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
,
with
0<r({\mathscr{H}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
, such that
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h)\geq 0
and
q_{X}(\lambda_{{\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}}},h_{0})=0
. The last equality contradicts

h_{0}
HK-slope stability of
{\mathscr{F}}
.
∎

2.3.
Twisted sheaves and slope stability

2.3.1.
Twisted sheaves

Here
X
is a complex manifold with the classical topology, and sheaves are (analytic) coherent sheaves.
Let
\alpha\in H^{2}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
be a class represented by the
2
-cocycle
\{\alpha_{ijk}\}
for an open cover
X=\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
.
An
\alpha
-twisted coherent sheaf

{\mathscr{E}}
on
X
consists of a collection
\{{\mathscr{E}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of coherent sheaves
{\mathscr{E}}_{i}
on
U_{i}
and gluing (holomorphic) isomorphisms
\varphi_{ji}\colon{{\mathscr{E}}_{i}}_{|U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{{\mathscr{E}}_{j}}_{|U_{ij}}
such that
\varphi_{ii}=
,

\varphi_{ij}=\varphi_{ji}^{-1}
, and
on the triple intersection
U_{ijk}
we have

{\varphi_{kj}}_{|U_{ijk}}\circ{\varphi_{ji}}_{|U_{ijk}}=\alpha_{ijk}\cdot{\varphi_{ki}}_{|U_{ijk}}
. Morphisms of

\alpha
-twisted coherent sheaves are given by morphisms of sheaves on the
U_{i}
’s which are compatible with the gluings. Up to isomorphism, the abelian category of
\alpha
-twisted coherent sheaves depends only on the class
\alpha\in H^{2}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
, see
[
C00
]
.

Remark 2.10
.

An
\alpha
-twisted sheaf
{\mathscr{E}}
is
locally free sheaf of rank
r

if all the
{\mathscr{E}}_{i}
are locally free of rank
r
- we also say that it is an
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle
. If this is the case, the projectivized gluings

{\mathbb{P}}(\varphi_{ji})\colon{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}}_{i})}_{|U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}}_{j})}_{|U_{ij}}
give a projective
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}})\to X
. Note that

\alpha
is the image of
\eta_{r}({\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}}))
(see (
1.1.2
)) for the map
H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})\to H^{2}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
.
If
{\mathscr{P}}\to X
is a
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle there exists an
\alpha
-twisted coherent sheaf
{\mathscr{E}}
on
X
(unique up to isomorphism) such that
{\mathscr{P}}\cong{\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}})
.

Remark 2.11
.

Suppose that
{\mathscr{E}}
is an
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle of rank
r
. Then
\{\varphi_{ji}\}_{i,j\in I}
is a
1
-cocycle
c
with values in the sheaf
\PGL_{r}({\mathscr{O}}_{X})
. Since the coboundary class
\delta(c)\in H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})
(see (
1.1.1
)) is equal to
\alpha
, we get
that
\alpha^{r}
is the trivial class in

H^{2}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
, in particular
\alpha
is a torsion class.
We also get that we may replace
\varphi_{ij}
by
\varphi^{\prime}_{ij}=\lambda_{ij}\varphi_{ij}
, where
\lambda_{ij}
is a suitable 1-cochain with values in
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*}
, so that

\{\det\varphi^{\prime}_{ji}\}_{i,j\in I}
is an honest
1
-cocycle with values in
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*}
. We denote by
\det{\mathscr{E}}
the corresponding line bundle. We let
c_{1}({\mathscr{E}})\coloneq c_{1}(\det{\mathscr{E}})
. Now suppose that
{\mathscr{E}}
is an
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle of rank
r
. Then there exists an open subset
X^{0}\subset X

with complement of codimension at least
2
such that

{\mathscr{E}}
is locally free on
X^{0}
. Since the restriction map
H^{2}(X;R)\to H^{2}(X^{0};R)
is an isomorphism for any coefficient group
R
, it follows that

\alpha^{r}
is the trivial class (as above), and we may define
c_{1}({\mathscr{E}})
as the unique
c_{1}({\mathscr{E}})\in H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})
such that
c_{1}({\mathscr{E}})_{|X^{0}}=c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{|X^{0}})
.

Remark 2.12
.

Suppose that
{\mathscr{E}}
is an
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle of rank
r
. Then the gluings of the
vector bundles
\{{\mathscr{E}}_{i}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
induced by the
\varphi_{ji}
’s match on triple intersections (not only up to homothety), and hence
{\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}
is an honest vector bundle, isomorphic to
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\oplus\mathfrak{g}({\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}})
.
If
\dim X\geq 2
we let

(2.3.1)

\Delta({\mathscr{E}})\coloneq c_{2}({\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}})=c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}({\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}})).

Definition 2.13
.

Let
X
be a HK manifold of dimension
2n
. An
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
on
X
is
modular
if
there exists
d({\mathscr{E}})\in{\mathbb{Q}}
such that for all
\alpha\in H^{2}(X)

(2.3.2)

\int_{X}\Delta({\mathscr{E}})\cdot\alpha^{2n-2}=d({\mathscr{E}})(2n-3)!!q_{X}(\alpha)^{n-1}.

Remark 2.14
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be an
\alpha
-twisted modular vector bundle on
a HK manifold
X
of dimension
2n
. Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}
is a(n honest) modular vector bundle because

(2.3.3)

\int_{X}\Delta({\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}})\cdot\alpha^{2n-2}=2\cdot r({\mathscr{E}})^{2}\cdot d({\mathscr{E}})(2n-3)!!q_{X}(\alpha)^{n-1}.

2.3.2.
Slope stability of twisted sheaves

In the present subsubsection
X
is a compact Kähler manifold (of dimension
m
) with a Kähler class
\omega\in H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(X)
.
Let
\alpha\in H^{2}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
. If
{\mathscr{E}}
is an

\alpha
-twisted torsion-free sheaf on
X
the

\omega

slope
of
{\mathscr{E}}
is defined as usual (recall that by Remark
2.11

c_{1}({\mathscr{E}})
is defined):

(2.3.4)

\mu_{\omega}({\mathscr{E}})\coloneq\int_{X}\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{E}})\cdot\omega^{m-1}}{r({\mathscr{E}})}.

Definition 2.15
.

An
\alpha
-twisted torsion-free sheaf
{\mathscr{E}}
on
X
is
\omega

slope
semistable if for all
\alpha
-twisted
subsheaves
{\mathscr{G}}\subset{\mathscr{E}}
with
0<r({\mathscr{G}})<r({\mathscr{E}})
we have
\mu_{\omega}({\mathscr{G}})\leq\mu_{\omega}({\mathscr{E}})
.
The sheaf
{\mathscr{E}}
is
{\omega}
slope stable
if strict inequality holds for all such
{\mathscr{G}}
, and it is
\omega

slope
polystable if it is a direct sum of
\omega
slope stable
torsion-free sheaves with equal
\omega
-slopes.

Remark 2.16
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be an
\alpha
-twisted locally-free sheaf of rank
r
on
X
. There is a notion of
\omega

slope
(semi)stability for the associated principal
\PGL_{r}
-bundle
\Princ({\mathscr{E}})\to X
, see for example
[
AB01
]
. These notions are equivalent, i.e.
{\mathscr{E}}
is
\omega

slope
semistable if and only if

\Princ({\mathscr{E}})
is.

Theorem 2.17

(Anchouche - Biswas)
.

An
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
on
X
is
\omega
slope polystable (respectively semistable) if and only if
{\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}
is

\omega
slope polystable (respectively semistable).

Proof.

The statement about polystability is
[
AB01
, Cor. 3.8]
(for
\PGL
-bundles), the statement about semistability is Prop. 2.10 in loc. cit.
∎

Let
\overline{{\bf w}}
be as in (
1.1.6
).
The moduli space
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(X,\omega)
of
\omega
-slope stable projective
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundles
{\mathscr{P}}\to X
with

\eta_{r}({\mathscr{P}})=\eta
and
\Delta({\mathscr{P}})=s
is an analytic space, see for example
[
AB01
]
. By
Remarks
2.10
and
2.16

we may, and will, view it as the moduli space of
\omega
-slope stable twisted locally-free sheaves
{\mathscr{E}}
on
X
with
r({\mathscr{E}})=r
,

\eta_{r}({\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}}))=\eta
,
\Delta({\mathscr{E}})=s
.

2.3.3.
Variation of slope stability for modular twisted sheaves

In the present subsubsection
X
is a HK manifold. We let
c_{X}
be the (small) Fujiki constant of
X
, see
[
OG26
, (2.6.3)]
.
Let
{\mathscr{K}}(X)\subset H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(X)
be the Kähler cone
(whose elements are the cohomology classes of Kähler metrics).
Let
{\mathsf{a}}
be a positive real number. An
{\mathsf{a}}
-wall
of
{\mathscr{K}}(X)
is the intersection

\lambda^{\bot}\cap{\mathscr{K}}(X)
, where

\lambda\in H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)
is a class such that

-{\mathsf{a}}\leq q_{X}(\lambda)<0

(orthogonality is with respect to the BBF quadratic form
q_{X}
).
The set of
{\mathsf{a}}
-walls is locally finite, in particular the union of all the
{\mathsf{a}}
-walls is closed in
{\mathscr{K}}(X)
. An

open
{\mathsf{a}}
-chamber
of
{\mathscr{K}}(X)
is a connected component of the complement of the union of all the

{\mathsf{a}}
-walls of
{\mathscr{K}}(X)
. A Kähler class is
{\mathsf{a}}
-generic if it belongs to an open
{\mathsf{a}}
-chamber.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be an
\alpha
-twisted modular vector bundle on
X
, where
\alpha\in H^{2}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
. Set

(2.3.5)

{\mathsf{a}}_{\rm tw}({\mathscr{E}})\coloneq{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}})=\frac{r({\mathscr{E}})^{4}\cdot d({\mathscr{E}})}{2c_{X}},

where
d({\mathscr{E}})
is as in (
2.3.2
).

Proposition 2.18
.

Let
X
be a HK manifold,
\omega_{0}\in{\mathscr{K}}(X)
an
{\mathsf{a}}
-generic Kähler class on
X
, and
\mathscr{C\mkern-4.0muH}\subset{\mathscr{K}}(X)
be the open

{\mathsf{a}}
-chamber containing
\omega_{0}
.
Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be an
\alpha
-twisted modular vector bundle on
X
, where
\alpha\in H^{2}(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
, such that

{\mathsf{a}}_{\rm tw}({\mathscr{E}})\leq{\mathsf{a}}
. If
{\mathscr{E}}
is

\omega_{0}
slope stable then it is
\omega
slope stable for every
\omega\in\mathscr{C\mkern-4.0muH}
.

Proof.

First we note that if
\omega\in{\mathscr{C}}
then
{\mathscr{E}}
is

\omega
slope semistable. In fact suppose that
{\mathscr{E}}
is
not
\omega
slope semistable.
By Theorem
2.17

the (honest) vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}
is
\omega_{0}
slope polystable, but not
\omega
slope semistable. Since

{\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}
is modular, and
{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}})\leq{\mathsf{a}}
, this contradicts the hypothesis that

\omega_{0},\omega
belong to the same open
{\mathsf{a}}
-chamber. In fact the proof is the same as the proof of
[
OG26
, Prop. 5.4]
.

Now suppose that there exists
\omega\in{\mathscr{C}}
such that
{\mathscr{E}}
is

\omega
slope-semistable but not stable, i.e. properly
\omega
slope-semistable. Then there exists an
\alpha
-twisted subsheaf

{\mathscr{G}}\subset{\mathscr{E}}
with
0<r({\mathscr{G}})<r({\mathscr{E}})
and (see
[
OG26
, Lemma 5.1]
)

(2.3.6)

q_{X}(r({\mathscr{E}})c_{1}({\mathscr{G}})-r({\mathscr{G}})c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}),\omega)=0.

Let
\omega_{t}\coloneq(1-t)\omega_{0}+t\omega
.
Let
\epsilon>0
such that
\omega_{t}\in{\mathscr{K}}(X)
for all
t\in(1,1+\epsilon)
. For such
t
we have

q_{X}(r({\mathscr{E}})c_{1}({\mathscr{G}})-r({\mathscr{G}})c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}),\omega_{t})=\\
=(1-t)q_{X}(r({\mathscr{E}})c_{1}({\mathscr{G}})-r({\mathscr{G}})c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}),\omega_{0})+tq_{X}(r({\mathscr{E}})c_{1}({\mathscr{G}})-r({\mathscr{G}})c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}),\omega)>0

because
{\mathscr{E}}
is

\omega_{0}
slope stable (see
[
OG26
, Lemma 5.1]
). Thus for all
t\in(1,1+\epsilon)
the
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
is not
\omega_{t}
slope-semistable. By what we have just proved it follows that
\{\omega_{t}\mid t\in(1,1+\epsilon)\}\cap{\mathscr{C}}=\emptyset
: contradiction because

{\mathscr{C}}
is open.
∎

3.
A series of moduli spaces of sheaves on K3 surfaces

3.1.
Set up

Throughout the present section
S
is a projective
K3
surface and
h_{S}
is an ample class on
S
. If
v=(r,l,s)\in H^{0}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})\oplus\NS(S)\oplus H^{4}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})
is a vector with
r>0
we let
{\mathscr{M}}_{v}={\mathscr{M}}_{v}(S,h_{S})
be the moduli space of
h_{S}
semistable (meaning
Gieseker-Maruyama
semistable) torsion-free sheaves on
S
with Mukai vector
v({\mathscr{F}})\coloneq\ch({\mathscr{F}})\cdot\sqrt{\Td_{S}}
equal to
v
.
If
v
is primitive and
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v)
-generic then
{\mathscr{M}}_{v}
is a HK variety of Type
K3^{[n]}
where
2n=2+\langle v,v\rangle
and
\langle,\rangle
is the Mukai pairing.
Whenever there is no ambiguity we drop reference to
h_{S}
when dealing with slope (semi)stability, or (semi)stability.
Let

(3.1.1)

v_{1}\coloneq(r_{1},l_{1},s_{1})\in H^{0}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})\oplus\NS(S)\oplus H^{4}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})

of square
-2
, with
r_{1}>0
. Throughout the section we assume that
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{1})
-generic.
Since
v_{1}
is primitive
{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{1}}(S,h_{S})
is a singleton. We let
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
be the unque (up to isomorphism)
h_{S}
slope stable spherical vector bundle with

(3.1.2)

v({\mathscr{E}}_{1})=v_{1}=(r_{1},l_{1},s_{1}).

Our notation ignores the dependency of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
on
h_{S}
.
For
t
an integer we let

(3.1.3)

v(t)\coloneq tv_{1}-(0,0,1).

Remark 3.1
.

The vector
v(t)
is primitive. In fact suppose that a prime
p
divides
v(t)
. By (
3.1.3
)
p
does not divide
t
and hence it divides both
r_{1}
and
l_{1}
. On
the other hand
p
does not divide both
r_{1}
and
l_{1}
because

v_{1}^{2}=-2
. Contradiction.

3.2.
The boundary of
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}

Definition 3.2
.

Let
{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}={\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
be the closed subset parametrizing sheaves which are not locally free.

Proposition 3.3
.

Suppose that
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{1})
-generic. Let
0<t\leq r_{1}
.

(1)

If
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})
then
{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus t}
and
{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}/{\mathscr{F}}\cong{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
for some
p\in S
.

(2)

By associating to
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})
the support of
{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}/{\mathscr{F}}
one gets a fibration
\pi^{a}_{v(t)}\colon{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})\to S
with fibers isomorphic to the Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}(r_{1}-t,r_{1})
, locally trivial in the classical topology.

Before proving Proposition
3.3
we go through some preliminary results.

Lemma 3.4
.

Suppose that
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{1})
-generic. Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be an
h_{S}
slope semistable torsion free sheaf on
(S,h_{S})
such that

(3.2.1)

v({\mathscr{F}})=kv_{1}+q(0,0,1)

for some
q\geq 0
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus k}
. In particular
{\mathscr{F}}
is locally free and
q=0
.

Proof.

We have

(3.2.2)

\chi(S,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{F}})=-\langle v_{1},v({\mathscr{F}})\rangle=-\langle v_{1},kv_{1}+q(0,0,1)\rangle=2k+q\cdot r_{1}.

By Serre duality it follows that either there exists a non zero map

(3.2.3)

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\overset{\phi\not=0}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{F}}

or
a non zero map

(3.2.4)

{\mathscr{F}}\overset{\psi\not=0}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}.

We argue by induction on
k
. Let
k=1
. If a map
\phi
as in (
3.2.3
) exists, then it has maximal rank in codimension
1
because

\mu_{h_{S}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\mu_{h_{S}}({\mathscr{F}})
. Composing
\phi
with the inclusion

{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}
we get
a map of vector bundles
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\to{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}
which is an isomorphism in codimension
1
, hence an isomorphism. It follows that

{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
, and hence

{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
.

If a map
\psi
as in (
3.2.4
) exists, arguing as above we get that

{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
. The exact sequence

(3.2.5)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}\longrightarrow 0,

with
{\mathscr{A}}
an Artinian sheaf gives that
v({\mathscr{F}})=v({\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee})-l({\mathscr{A}})(0,0,1)=v_{1}-l({\mathscr{A}})(0,0,1)
. It follows that

{\mathscr{A}}=0
and hence
{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
.

Next we prove the inductive step. Thus
k\geq 2
. If a map
\phi
as in (
3.2.3
) exists, then it has maximal rank in codimension
1
, and hence we get an exact sequence

(3.2.6)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Q}}\longrightarrow 0,

where
v({\mathscr{Q}})=(k-1)v_{1}+q(0,0,1)
.

We claim that
{\mathscr{Q}}
is torsion free. In fact the two step locally free resolution of
{\mathscr{Q}}
in (
3.2.6
) gives that

\Ext^{2}({\mathscr{Q}},{\mathbb{C}}_{P})=0
for any
P\in S
. But if
{\mathscr{Q}}
has torsion, then
\Tors({\mathscr{Q}})
has zero dimensional support
because
{\mathscr{Q}}
is locally free in codimension
1
(
\phi
has maximal rank in codimension
1
), and hence

\Ext^{2}({\mathscr{Q}},{\mathbb{C}}_{P})\not=0
for any
P
in the support of
\Tors({\mathscr{Q}})
.

We have
\mu_{h_{S}}({\mathscr{Q}})=\mu_{h_{S}}({\mathscr{F}})
because
\mu_{h_{S}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\mu_{h_{S}}({\mathscr{F}})
. Since every quotient of
{\mathscr{Q}}
is a quotient of
{\mathscr{F}}
it follows that
{\mathscr{Q}}
is slope semistable.

Since
{\mathscr{Q}}
is torsion free, slope semistable, and
v({\mathscr{Q}})=(k-1)v_{1}+q(0,0,1)
it follows from the inductive hypothesis that

{\mathscr{Q}}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus(k-1)}
.
Since
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is spherical we have
\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus(k-1)},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=0
, and hence

{\mathscr{F}}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus k}
. This finishes the proof if a map
\phi
as in (
3.2.3
) exists.

If a map
\psi
as in (
3.2.4
) exists one argues similarly.

∎

Let
V_{t}
be a complex vector space of dimension
t>0
, and let
f\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}\to{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
be a surjection, where
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
is the skyscraper sheaf at
p\in S
. Of course
f
is determined by the non zero linear map

f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes V_{t}\to{\mathbb{C}}
. We view
f(p)
as a map

(3.2.7)

f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\longrightarrow V^{\vee}_{t}.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}\coloneq\ker(f)
. Thus
{\mathscr{F}}
fits into the exact sequence

(3.2.8)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\longrightarrow 0.

Lemma 3.5
.

Suppose that
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{1})
-generic. The sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
appearing in (
3.2.8
) is semistable if and only if

f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{t}
is surjective, and then it is actually stable.

Proof.

Assume that
f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{t}
is not surjective. Then there exists
0\not=v\in V_{t}
such that
\langle f(p)(s),v\rangle=0
for every

s\in{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)
. Let
\Lambda\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}
be given by

\Lambda(s)\coloneq s\otimes v
. Then
\Lambda
is an injection, and since
f\circ\Lambda=0
, it factors through an injection

\overline{\Lambda}\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\to{\mathscr{F}}
. Since
r({\mathscr{F}})\cdot\chi(S,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(n))>r({\mathscr{E}}_{1})\cdot\chi(S,{\mathscr{F}}(n))
for

n\gg 0
it follows that
{\mathscr{F}}
is not semistable.

Assume that
f(p)
is surjective. Suppose that
{\mathscr{H}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
is a destabilizing subsheaf, i.e. that
0<r({\mathscr{H}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
and

(3.2.9)

r({\mathscr{F}})\cdot\chi(S,{\mathscr{H}}(n))\geq r({\mathscr{H}})\cdot\chi(S,{\mathscr{F}}(n)),\quad n\gg 0.

Then we get an inclusion

{\mathscr{H}}^{\vee\vee}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}
, and
\mu({\mathscr{H}})\geq\mu({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t})
. Since
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is slope stable, it follows that
{\mathscr{H}}^{\vee\vee}={\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes U
where
U\subset V_{t}
is a subspace such that
0<\dim U<\dim V_{t}
,
and the
inclusion
{\mathscr{H}}^{\vee\vee}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}
is induced by
U\subset V_{t}
. Since
f(p)
has maximal rank, it follows that we have an exact sequence

(3.2.10)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes U\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\longrightarrow 0.

This contradicts the inequality in (
3.2.9
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.3
.

(1): Let
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}
is slope semistable and
v({\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee})=tv_{1}+q(0,0,1)
for some
q\geq 0
. By
Lemma
3.4
it follows that
{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus t}
. Since
v({\mathscr{F}})=tv({\mathscr{E}}_{1})-(0,0,1)
it follows that
{\mathscr{F}}
fits into an exact sequence

(3.2.11)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\longrightarrow 0

where
V_{t}
is a complex vector space of dimension
t
, and
p\in S
.

(2): Suppose that the sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
fits into the exact sequence in (
3.2.11
). By Lemma
3.5

{\mathscr{F}}
is semistable if and only if
f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{t}
is surjective, and then

{\mathscr{F}}
is stable. The group
\GL(V_{t})
acts on the set of maps
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{t}
of maximal rank, and

\ker(f)
is isomorphic to
\ker(g)
(for
f,g\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{t}
of maximal rank) if and only if the maps
f
and
g
are in the same
\GL(V_{t})
-orbit. By associating to a map
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{t}

of maximal rank its kernel (an element of
\mathrm{Gr}(r_{1}-t,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p))
) we identify
\mathrm{Gr}(r_{1}-t,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p))
with
the quotient of the set of maps
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{t}
of maximal rank modulo the action of
\GL(V_{t})
.
∎

3.3.
Brill-Noether loci

From now on we assume that
r_{1}=2a
in (
3.1.1
). Thus

(3.3.1)

v_{1}=(2a,l_{1},s_{1}).

We are mainly interested in the moduli space
{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
, where

(3.3.2)

v_{2}=av_{1}-(0,0,1)=v(a).

More precisely we are interested in the determinantal loci
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v_{2}}\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
defined below, see Definition
3.8
. This leads to consider also the moduli spaces
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}

for
1\leq t<a
and the analogous determinantal loci
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(t)}\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
. The collection of the closed subsets
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(t)}
form a dualizable collection in the sense of
[
Mar01
, Def. 2.3]
(the maps
f_{\bullet,\bullet}
are essentially given by the maps
\pi_{v(t)}^{k}
appearing in (
3.3.22
)).

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
1\leq t\leq a
. If the polarization
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic then

(A)

the polarization
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v(t))
-generic for every
1\leq t\leq a
,

(B)

there is no strictly semistable sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
on
S
with
v({\mathscr{F}})=v(t)
, and

(C)

all sheaves parametrized by
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})
are stable.

(D)

{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})
is smooth (and projective).

Proof.

(A): Equation (
2.2.5
) and the equality

(3.3.3)

\langle v(t),v(t)\rangle=2t(2a-t)

give that
{\mathsf{a}}(v(t))\leq{\mathsf{a}}(v(a))={\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
.

(B): Since
l_{1}^{2}-4as_{1}=-2
, we have

\gcd\{\divisore(l_{1}),2a\}=1
. It follows
that
v(t)
is primitive. Item (B) follows because
h_{S}
is
v(t)
-generic.

(C): follows from (B).

(D): follows from (C) and the Mukai-Artamkin criterion for smoothness.
∎

Notation 3.7
.

Throughout the present subsection
h_{S}
is an
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic polarization of
S
, and

{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}={\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})
.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
0<t\leq a
. For
k
a natural number let

{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\coloneq\{[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}\mid\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\geq k\}.

We let
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}={\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{1}
.

We have the chain of closed subsets

(3.3.4)

{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}={\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{0}\supset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{1}\supset\ldots\supset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\supset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1}\ldots

The next remark gives each
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
a structure of closed subscheme of
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
.

Remark 3.9
.

Let
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
. Since
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})
vanishes by stability of
{\mathscr{F}}
, we have
\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\{0\}
by Serre duality.
Since

(3.3.5)

\chi_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=-\langle v(t),v_{1}\rangle=-2(a-t),

it follows that

(3.3.6)

\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})+2(a-t).

The vanishing of
\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
gives also that

{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
can be locally (in the analytic or étale topology) described as the degeneracy locus of a map of vector bundles, i.e. as a determinantal variety. More precisely, locally we have a map
\varphi\colon V^{m}\longrightarrow V^{m+2(a-t)}
of vector bundles of ranks
m
and
m+2(a-t)
respectively such that
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
is the set of points
x
such that
\ker\varphi(x)
has dimension at least
k
. Form this we get that

{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
has a structure of
closed subscheme of
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
, of expected codimension
k(2a-2t+k)
. The last assertion means that if
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
is non empty then every one of its irreducible components has codimension at most
k(2a-2t+k)
.

From now on we view
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
as a
closed subscheme of
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
.
Note that (
3.3.4
) is a chain of inclusions of closed subchemes.

Proposition 3.10
.

Suppose that
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic. Let
2\leq t\leq a
and let

[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})
. If
{\mathscr{F}}
is locally free then the following are equivalent:

(1)

{\mathscr{F}}
is not slope stable.

(2)

[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}
, i.e.
\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})>0
, and there is an exact sequence

(3.3.7)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0

where
{\mathscr{H}}
is slope stable (in particular
r({\mathscr{H}})>0
), and
\psi
corresponds to
\Id\in\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
.

Proof.

We prove that (1) implies (2).
Since
{\mathscr{F}}
is not slope stable there exists an exact sequence of torsion free sheaves

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Q}}\longrightarrow 0

where
0<r({\mathscr{Q}})<r({\mathscr{F}})
,
\mu({\mathscr{F}})=\mu({\mathscr{Q}})
and
{\mathscr{H}}
is slope stable. Since
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic it is also

{\mathsf{a}}(v(t))
-generic (see Lemma
3.6
).
It follows (see
[
O’G22a
, Pro. 3.10]
) that
r({\mathscr{Q}})c_{1}({\mathscr{F}})-r({\mathscr{F}})c_{1}({\mathscr{Q}})=0
.
This gives that
r({\mathscr{Q}})=2ka
and
c_{1}({\mathscr{Q}})=kl_{1}
where
k
is an integer such that
1\leq k\leq t-1
, and hence we may write
v({\mathscr{Q}})=(2ka,kl_{1},m)
for some integer
m
.
By stability of
{\mathscr{F}}
(and the equality
\mu({\mathscr{F}})=\mu({\mathscr{Q}})
) it follows that

1+\frac{s_{1}}{2a}-\frac{1}{2at}=\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{F}})}{r({\mathscr{F}})}<\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{Q}})}{r({\mathscr{Q}})}=1+\frac{m}{2ka}.

This gives that
m\geq ks_{1}
(recall that
1\leq k\leq t-1
). Hence
v({\mathscr{Q}})=kv_{1}+q(0,0,1)
where
q\geq 0
.
Since
{\mathscr{F}}
is slope semistable and
\mu({\mathscr{F}})=\mu({\mathscr{Q}})
the sheaf
{\mathscr{Q}}
is slope semistable. By
Lemma
3.4

we get that
{\mathscr{Q}}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus k}
. Since
{\mathscr{H}}
is slope stable we have

\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=0
and hence
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\cong\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\oplus k},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
. Since
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is stable it follows that
\dim\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=k
. This proves that (1) implies (2).

(2) implies (1) because
c_{1}({\mathscr{F}})/r({\mathscr{F}})=l_{1}/2a=c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})/r({\mathscr{E}}_{1})
.
∎

Remark 3.11
.

Let
{\mathscr{H}}
be the sheaf appearing in (
3.3.7
). Then
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=0
. In fact this follows by applying the functor

\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
to the exact sequence in (
3.3.7
) and noting that
\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=0
because
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is spherical.

Remark 3.12
.

Applying the functor

\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},-)
to the exact sequence in (
3.3.7
) we get the coboundary map

(3.3.8)

{\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\overset{\partial}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})

The map is injective because by stability of
{\mathscr{F}}
we have

\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})=0
.

The result below gives a converse of Proposition
3.10
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Suppose that
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic.
Let
0<k<t\leq a
. Let

[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(t-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t-k)})
, and let
V_{k}\subset\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
be a
k
-dimensional vector subspace (by (
3.3.6
) we have
2k\leq\ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
). Let

(3.3.9)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0

be the extension with extension class in
\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k},{\mathscr{H}})=V_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
corresponding to the inclusion map
V_{k}\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}
is a stable sheaf,
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}
, and

\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=k
.

Proof.

Suppose that
{\mathscr{F}}
is not stable. Then
{\mathscr{F}}
is unstable by Lemma
3.6
, i.e. there exists a non zero subsheaf

{\mathscr{G}}\subset{\mathscr{F}}
such that

(3.3.10)

\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{G}}(n))}{r({\mathscr{G}})}>\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{F}}(n))}{r({\mathscr{F}})},\quad n\gg 0.

Consider the exact sequence

(3.3.11)

0\longrightarrow\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}\overset{\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}}}{\longrightarrow}\varphi({\mathscr{G}})\longrightarrow 0

Since
\mu({\mathscr{H}})=\mu({\mathscr{F}})=\mu(V_{k}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
, it follows from the inequality in (
3.3.10
) that

(3.3.12)

\mu({\mathscr{G}})=\mu({\mathscr{F}})=\mu(\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}}))=\mu(\varphi({\mathscr{G}})).

(Of course
\mu(\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}}))
make sense only if
\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}})\not=0
and similarly for
\mu(\varphi({\mathscr{G}}))
.)
Hence the inequality in (
3.3.10
) implies that

(3.3.13)

\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{G}})}{r({\mathscr{G}})}>\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{F}})}{r({\mathscr{F}})}.

Suppose that
\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}})
is non zero. Then since it is a subsheaf of the stable sheaf
{\mathscr{H}}
we have

\frac{\chi(S,\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}})}{r(\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}}))}<\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{H}})}{r({\mathscr{H}})}=1+\frac{s_{1}}{2a}-\frac{1}{2a(t-k)}<1+\frac{s_{1}}{2a}-\frac{1}{2at}=\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{F}})}{r({\mathscr{F}})}.

Similarly, if
\varphi({\mathscr{G}})
is non zero then
\chi(S,\varphi({\mathscr{G}}))/r(\varphi({\mathscr{G}}))\leq\chi(S,V_{k}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})/r(V_{k}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
, with equality if and only if
\varphi({\mathscr{G}})=U_{j}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
for some vector subspace
U_{j}\subset V_{k}
of non zero dimension
j
(recall the equalities in (
3.3.12
)).
The equality

\frac{\chi(S,{\mathscr{G}})}{r({\mathscr{G}})}=\frac{r(\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}}))}{r({\mathscr{G}})}\frac{\chi(S,\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}})}{r(\ker(\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}}))}+\frac{r(\varphi({\mathscr{G}}))}{r({\mathscr{G}})}\frac{\chi(S,\varphi({\mathscr{G}}))}{r(\varphi({\mathscr{G}}))}

gives that
\varphi_{|{\mathscr{G}}}=0
and
\varphi({\mathscr{G}})=U_{j}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
. This is absurd: by our choice of extension class the inclusion

U_{j}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\subset V_{k}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
does not lift to
{\mathscr{F}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
0<t\leq a
. Suppose that
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(t)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}_{v(t)})
. Then

(3.3.14)

\dim{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}-\dim\Theta_{[{\mathscr{F}}]}{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(t)}=k(2a-2t+k).

Proof.

By hypothesis
\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=k
. Let

(3.3.15)

V_{k}\coloneq\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee},

and let
f\colon{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k}
be the tautological map. We have the linear map

(3.3.16)

\begin{matrix}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{F}})&\overset{\Phi_{\mathscr{F}}}{\longrightarrow}&\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k})\\
e&\mapsto&e\cup f\end{matrix}

The Zariski tangent space
\Theta_{[{\mathscr{F}}]}{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(t)}\subset\Theta_{[{\mathscr{F}}]}{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}=\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{F}})
is given by

(3.3.17)

\Theta_{[{\mathscr{F}}]}{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(t)}=\ker\Phi_{\mathscr{F}}.

Assume that
{\mathscr{F}}
is locally free. By Proposition
3.10
we have the exact sequence

(3.3.18)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0,

where
{\mathscr{H}}
is slope stable with
v({\mathscr{H}})=v(t-k)
. Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},-)
to the exact sequence in (
3.3.18
) we get the exact sequence

\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{F}})\overset{\Phi_{\mathscr{F}}}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{H}})\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{F}}).

We claim that
\alpha
is an isomorphism, i.e. that the transpose
\alpha^{t}
is an isomorphism. In fact
\alpha^{t}
is equal (via Serre duality) to the map
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{F}})\to\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{F}})
obtained by composing with the inclusion
{\mathscr{H}}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}
. Since
{\mathscr{F}}
and
{\mathscr{H}}
are stable (and hence simple), in order to show that
\alpha^{t}
is an isomorphism it suffices to prove that there are no non-zero maps
{\mathscr{H}}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k}
. This follows from slope stability of
{\mathscr{H}}
.
Since
\alpha
is an isomorphism the map
\Phi_{\mathscr{F}}
is surjective, and hence

\dim{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}-\dim\Theta_{[{\mathscr{F}}]}{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v(t)}=\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k}).

Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
to the exact sequence in (
3.3.18
) we get the exact sequence

(3.3.19)

0\to\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\to\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\to\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{k}^{\vee}\to 0.

We have
\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=2(a-t+k)
by Remark
3.11
and Equation (
3.3.6
) (for
{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{H}}
). Hence

\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=2a-2t+k

and the equality in (
3.3.14
) follows (for
{\mathscr{F}}
locally free).

Next assume that
{\mathscr{F}}
is not locally free. By Proposition
3.3
we have an exact sequence

(3.3.20)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\longrightarrow 0.

In particular
\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=t
, i.e.
k=t
.
Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},-)
to the above exact sequence one gets the exact sequence

\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{F}})\overset{\Phi_{\mathscr{F}}}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{t}\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\overset{\partial}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{F}})\longrightarrow 0.

We have
\chi_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})=-\langle v({\mathscr{F}}),v({\mathbb{C}}_{p})\rangle=2at
. Since
\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})=2at+1
(the stalk
{\mathscr{F}}_{p}
is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{O}}_{S,p}^{\oplus(2at-1)}\oplus\mathfrak{m}_{p}
