) and
\ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})=\hom_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{F}})=0
, it follows that
\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})=1
. Hence the exact sequence above gives that
\Phi_{\mathscr{F}}
is surjective. Thus

\dim{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}-\dim\Theta_{[{\mathscr{F}}]}{\mathscr{D}}^{t}_{v(t)}=\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{t}).

Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
to the exact sequence in (
3.3.20
) we get the exact sequence

(3.3.21)

0\to\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\to\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\to\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{t}^{\vee}\to 0.

Hence
\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=2a-t
and the equality in (
3.3.14
) follows.
∎

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
0<t\leq a
. Let
k
be a natural number.

(1)

If
k>t
then
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
is empty.

(2)

Let
0\leq k\leq t
. Then
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1}
is non empty and smooth of the expected dimension.

(3)

We have
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{t}={\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}
.

(4)

Let
0\leq k<t
. There is a fibration (locally trivial in the classical topology)

(3.3.22)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}(k,2(a-t+k))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{v(t)}^{k}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t-k)}}

defined by
\pi_{v(t)}^{k}([{\mathscr{F}}])\coloneq[{\mathscr{H}}]
where
{\mathscr{H}}
is the sheaf appearing in (
3.3.7
) (
{\mathscr{F}}
is locally free by Item (3) and
[{\mathscr{H}}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t-k)}
by Remark
3.11
), and
(\pi_{v(t)}^{k})^{-1}([{\mathscr{H}}])=\mathrm{Gr}(k,\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))
.

Proof.

(1): Let
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
. Suppose that
{\mathscr{F}}
is locally free. Then
{\mathscr{F}}
fits into the exact sequence in (
3.3.7
) with

\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\geq k
. Since
r({\mathscr{H}})>0
we have
2at=r({\mathscr{F}})>\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})r({\mathscr{E}}_{1})\geq kr({\mathscr{E}}_{1})=2ak
. Thus
k<t
if
{\mathscr{F}}
is locally free. If
{\mathscr{F}}
is not locally free
i.e.
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}
, then
k\leq t
by Item (1) of Proposition
3.3
.

(2): We have
{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}\subset{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{t}
by Item (1) of Proposition
3.3
. Thus
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{t}
is non empty. Now let
0<k<t
. Then
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t-k)}
is non empty because
\langle v(t-k),v(t-k)\rangle=2(t-k)(2a-t+k)>0
. Moreover
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}\not={\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
by Lemma
3.14
. Let
[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(t-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)})
, let
V_{k}\subset\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
be a
k
-dimensional vector subspace, and let
{\mathscr{F}}
be the sheaf fitting into the exact sequence in (
3.3.9
). By Proposition
3.13
the sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
is
stable and
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in({\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1})
. This proves that
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1}
is non empty for
0<k\leq t
.
It is smooth of the expected dimension by Lemma
3.14
, because the right-hand side of (
3.3.14
) is equal to the expected codimension of
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
.

(3): In the proof of Item (1) we have shown that we have the inclusion of sets

{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{t}\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{v(t)}
. The reverse inclusion follows from Item (1) of Proposition
3.3
.

(4): This follows from Propositions
3.10
and
3.13
.
∎

Remark 3.16
.

Let
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in({\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1})
, and let
{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}}([{\mathscr{F}}])
be the fiber at
[{\mathscr{F}}]
of the normal bundle of

{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
in
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
(recall that
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}
is smooth away from
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1}
). The proof of Lemma
3.14
gives an identification

(3.3.23)

{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}}([{\mathscr{F}}])=\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

Remark 3.17
.

Let
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in({\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k+1})
. Without going through Lemma
3.14
one defines an (a priori) inclusion

(3.3.24)

{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}}([{\mathscr{F}}])\subset\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})

as follows. Locally on
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
(in the Zariski topology if there exists a universal sheaf on

S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}
, in the étale topology if a universal sheaf does not exist) we have a morphism of vector bundles

V_{d}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{U}\xrightarrow{\phi}W_{d+2(a-t)}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{U},

where
V_{d},W_{d+2(a-t)}
are complex vector spaces of dimensions
d
and
d+2(a-t)
respectively such that

{\mathscr{D}}^{l}\cap U
(we work in the Zariski topology) for all
l
is identified with the determinantal scheme

D^{l}(\phi)
. Put differently,
\phi
defines a morphism
\alpha\colon U\to V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)}

and
{\mathscr{D}}^{l}\cap U=\alpha^{*}D^{l}(V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)})
where

(3.3.25)

D^{l}(V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)})\subset V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)}

is the determinantal subscheme parametrizes linear maps

V_{d}\to W_{d+2(a-t)}
whose kernel has dimension at least
l
. Let
0=[{\mathscr{F}}]\in U
,
and let
K_{0}\coloneq\ker\phi(0)
,
J_{0}\coloneq\coker\phi(0)
. Thus we have identifications

(3.3.26)

K_{0}=\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}),\qquad J_{0}=\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

Of course
K_{0}=\ker\alpha(0)
and
J_{0}=\coker\alpha(0)
. Let
\mu_{0}\colon V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)}\longrightarrow K_{0}^{\vee}\otimes J_{0}
be the obvious map.
An elementary computation shows that

\Theta_{D^{k}(V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)})}(\alpha(0))=\ker\mu_{0}\subset V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)}=\Theta_{V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)}}

The above equality, together with the identifications in (
3.3.26
), defines the inclusion in (
3.3.24
).

3.4.
An iterated blow up

Throughout the present subsection we adopt the notation of Subsection
3.3
, in particular Notation
3.7
is in force. In addition we let
{\mathscr{M}}\coloneq{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
and
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\coloneq{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v_{2}}
.

Definition 3.18
.

Let
varieties
{\mathscr{M}}(a),\ldots,{\mathscr{M}}(0)
, birational maps

{\mathscr{M}}(0)\xrightarrow{f_{0}}{\mathscr{M}}(1)\xrightarrow{f_{1}}\ldots{\mathscr{M}}(a)\xrightarrow{f_{a}}{\mathscr{M}},

and for
j\in\{a,a-1,\ldots,0\}
closed subsets
E(j)^{s},{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{t}\subset{\mathscr{M}}(j)
for
s>j\geq t\geq 0

be as follows.

(1)

{\mathscr{M}}(a)={\mathscr{M}}
,
f_{a}\colon{\mathscr{M}}(a)\to{\mathscr{M}}
is the identity,
E(a)^{s}=\emptyset
and
{\mathscr{D}}(a)^{t}={\mathscr{D}}^{t}
.

(2)

f_{j-1}\colon{\mathscr{M}}(j-1)\to{\mathscr{M}}(j)
and
E(j-1)^{s},{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{t}
are defined inductively:

(2a)

f_{j-1}
is the blow up of
{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}

(2b)

E(j-1)^{s}
is the strict transform of
E(j)^{s}
if
s>j
, and

E(j-1)^{j}
is the exceptional divisor of
f_{j-1}
.

(2c)

If
j-1\geq t\geq 0
then

{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{t}
is the strict transform of
{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{t}
.

Note that
E(j)^{s}
is non empty if and only if
a\geq s>j
.
We let

(3.4.1)

{\mathscr{D}}(j)={\mathscr{D}}(j)^{1},\quad\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}={\mathscr{M}}(0),\quad\widehat{E}^{s}=E(0)^{s},\ a\geq s\geq 1.

Note that
{\mathscr{D}}(0)=\emptyset
. Let

(3.4.2)

\widehat{f}\coloneq f_{a}\circ\ldots\circ f_{0}\colon\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}}.

We have
\widehat{f}^{-1}({\mathscr{D}})=\widehat{E}^{a}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{1}
.

Remark 3.19
.

Formally
f_{a}
is the blow up of
{\mathscr{D}}^{a+1}=\emptyset
. The last blow up is an isomorphism because
{\mathscr{D}}(1)
(as any other
{\mathscr{D}}(j)
) is a Cartier divisor.
The reason to define the last blow up is the following. According to Proposition
4.2
, by associating to

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}
the sheaf
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
(notation as in loc. cit.) we get a rational map to a moduli space
M_{{\bf w}}
of semistable sheaves on
S^{[2]}
. The sheaves
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
are non stable precisely when
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}
. The sequence of blow-ups
f_{j}

is dictated by Langton’s algorithm for semistable reduction. The last blow up
f_{0}\colon{\mathscr{M}}(0)\to{\mathscr{M}}(1)
is an isomorphism but it does change the sheaves parametrized by
{\mathscr{D}}(1)
(making them stable).

Proposition 3.20
.

\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
is smooth and the divisor on
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
given by
\widehat{E}^{a}+\ldots+\widehat{E}^{1}
has simple normal crossings.

Before proving Proposition
3.20
we describe the normal cone of
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}
in
{\mathscr{D}}^{h}
(for
0\leq h\leq k
) away from
{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
.
Let

[{\mathscr{F}}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1})
, and let
{\mathscr{C}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{D}}^{h}}([{\mathscr{F}}])
be the fiber at
[{\mathscr{F}}]
of the normal cone of

{\mathscr{D}}^{k}
in
{\mathscr{D}}^{h}
. By the identification in (
3.3.23
) we have an embedding

(3.4.3)

{\mathscr{C}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{D}}^{h}}([{\mathscr{F}}])\subset\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

For an integer
l
we let

(3.4.4)

D_{l}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}))\subset\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})

be the determinantal subscheme parametrizing maps of rank at most
l
- note the difference between the meaning of
l
in the above definition and in (
3.3.25
).

Lemma 3.21
.

Let

[{\mathscr{F}}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1})
. Then, referring to the
embedding in (
3.4.3
), we have

(3.4.5)

{\mathscr{C}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{D}}^{h}}([{\mathscr{F}}])=D_{k-h}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})).

Proof.

We adopt the notation of Remark
3.17
. An elementary computation gives the following description of the fiber of the normal cone of
D^{k}=D^{k}(V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)})
in
D^{h}=D^{h}(V_{d}^{\vee}\otimes W_{d+2(a-t)})
at
\alpha(0)
:

{\mathscr{C}}_{D^{k}/D^{h}}(\alpha(0))=D_{k-h}(K_{0}^{\vee}\otimes J_{0}).

The lemma follows from the above equality, the identifications in (
3.3.26
), and the equality in (
3.3.23
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.20
.

Starting from
j=a
we prove that for
j\in\{a,\ldots,0\}
the following hold:

(
R_{j}
)

{\mathscr{M}}(j)
is smooth.

(
S_{j}
)

{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}
is smooth.

(
T_{j}
)

The divisor
E(j)^{a}+\ldots+E^{j+1}(j)
on
{\mathscr{M}}(j)
has simple normal crossings.

We have
{\mathscr{M}}(a)={\mathscr{M}}
,
{\mathscr{D}}(a)^{a}={\mathscr{D}}^{a}
. Hence
{\mathscr{M}}(a)
is smooth by Item (D) of Lemma
3.6
, and
{\mathscr{D}}(a)^{a}
is smooth by Items (1) and (2) of Proposition
3.15
.
Since the divisor in Item (
T_{a}
) is
0
, we have shown that Items (
R_{a}
), (
S_{a}
) and (
T_{a}
) hold. Assume that Items (
R_{j}
), (
S_{j}
) and (
T_{j}
) hold. Since
f_{j-1}\colon{\mathscr{M}}(j-1)\to{\mathscr{M}}(j)
is the blow up of

{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}
, and
{\mathscr{M}}(j),{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}
are smooth by the inductive hypothesis, it follows that Item (
R_{j}
) holds.
Let us prove that Item (
S_{j-1}
) holds. The composition

f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j-1}\colon{\mathscr{M}}(j-1)\to{\mathscr{M}}
restricts to an isomorphism

({\mathscr{M}}(j-1)\setminus E(j-1)^{a}\setminus\ldots\setminus E(j-1)^{j})\xrightarrow{\sim}({\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{j})
, and it maps

{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{j-1}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{j}
to

{\mathscr{D}}^{j-1}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{j}
. By Proposition
3.15
it follows that
{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{j-1}
is smooth away from
the intersection with
E(j-1)^{a}\cup\ldots\cup E(j-1)^{j}
. Let

x\in{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{j-1}\cap(E(j-1)^{a}\cup\ldots\cup E(j-1)^{j})
. There exists a maximum
k
with
a\geq k\geq j
such that
x\in E(j-1)^{k}
. The restriction of
f_{k-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j-1}
to
E(j-1)^{k}\setminus E(j-1)^{k+1}
defines a map

\lambda\colon E(j-1)^{k}\setminus E(j-1)^{k+1}\longrightarrow E(k-1)^{k}\setminus E(k-1)^{k+1}.

By (
3.3.23
)
the restriction of
f_{a}\circ\ldots\circ f_{k-1}
to
E(k-1)^{k}\setminus E(k-1)^{k+1}
is a fibration

(3.4.6)

\begin{matrix}{\mathbb{P}}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}))&\subset&E(k-1)^{k}\setminus E(k-1)^{k+1}\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow\theta\\
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]&\in&{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}\end{matrix}

Composing with
\lambda
we get the map

(3.4.7)

\theta\circ\lambda\colon E(j-1)^{k}\setminus E(j-1)^{k+1}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}.

The fiber of
\theta\circ\lambda
over
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]
is mapped by
f_{a}\circ\ldots\circ f_{k-1}
to

{\mathbb{P}}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}))
, and by Lemma
3.21
this map is the blow-up of the projectivized determinantal scheme

{\mathbb{P}}(D_{1}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})))
, followed by the blow-up of the strict transform of

{\mathbb{P}}(D_{2}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})))
, etc.
Moreover by Lemma
3.21

{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{j-1}\cap(E(j-1)^{k}\setminus E(j-1)^{k+1})
intersects such fiber in the strict transform of

{\mathbb{P}}(D_{k-j}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})))
. Said strict transform is smooth.
This proves that
{\mathscr{D}}(j-1)^{j-1}
is smooth at
x
.
∎

4.
Families of slope stable vector bundles on
S^{[2]}

4.1.
Road map

In Subsection
4.2
we prove that
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is slope stable for all

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S}))
, provided the polarization of
S^{[2]}
is close enough to
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})

(here
h_{S}
is an
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic polarization of
S
), see Proposition
4.2
.
In Subsection
4.3
we prove that if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
then
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is slope unstable for any choice of polarization of
S^{[2]}
, see Proposition
4.9
.
The remaining subsections are dedicated to the construction of the semistable replacements of the sheaves
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
- but note that the proof that they are the semistable replacements is given later, in Section
5
.
Specifically, in Subsection
4.4
we define, for

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S}))
with
k<a
, the semistable replacement of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
as a double extension of sheaves

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}
,
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}
and
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
, where
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}
are the rigid slope stable vector bundles studied in
[
O’G22a
]
, and
[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a-k)}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a-k)}(S,h_{S}))
, and we give a similar construction for the semistable replacement of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
when
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
, see Definitions
4.18
and
4.30
. Subsection
4.5
contains the proof that the extensions defined in the previous subsection are slope stable, see Propositions
4.33
and
4.38
. We also prove that the extensions are locally free, see Corollary
4.43
- this is obvious for the semistable replacements of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
if

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\notin{\mathscr{D}}^{a}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
, but if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
it is a pleasant surprise.

4.2.
Stability of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}})

Hypothesis-Definition 4.1
.

S
is a projective
K3
surface,
a,k
are positive integers, and
D
is a divisor on
S
such that

(4.2.1)

D\cdot D=2\cdot(2ak-1).

The Mukai vectors
v_{1},v_{2}
on
S
are given by

(4.2.2)

v_{1}\coloneq\left(2a,D,k\right),\quad v_{2}\coloneq av_{1}-(0,0,1)=\left(2a^{2},aD,ak-1\right).

Note that
v_{1}^{2}=-2
,
v_{2}^{2}=2a^{2}
(here squares are with respect to Mukai’s pairing). Moreover
v_{2}
is primitive by Remark
3.1
,
and of course also
v_{1}
.
Let
h_{S}
be an
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic polarization of
S
. The moduli space
{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{1}}(S,h_{S})
is a reduced point
because
h_{S}
is also
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{1})
-generic. Throughout the section we let

(4.2.3)

{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{1}}(S,h_{S})=\{[{\mathscr{E}}_{1}]\}.

Thus
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is a spherical vector bundle. The moduli space
{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
is a HK variety of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
. Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
, and let
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
be the sheaf on
S^{[2]}
defined in
[
OG26
, Def. 2.1]
.
By Lemma 3.4 loc. cit.
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is a torsion free simple sheaf on
S^{[2]}
, and its mock Mukai vector is given by

(4.2.4)

{\bf w}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}))={\bf w}(D,a)\coloneq 4a^{2}\left(2a,{\bm{\mu}}(D)-a\delta,\frac{a^{4}}{3}c_{2}(S^{[2]})\right),

where
{\bm{\mu}}(D)
is represented by the divisor in
S^{[2]}
parametrizing subschemes
intersecting
D
non trivially (assume that
D
is reduced), see also (
6.1.6
), and

(4.2.5)

\Delta\coloneq\{[Z]\in S^{[2]}\mid\text{$Z$ is non reduced}\},\qquad 2\delta=\cl(\Delta).

Below is the main result of the present subsection.

Proposition 4.2
.

Keep hypotheses and notation as above, in particular
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic. Let
h_{S^{[2]}}
be a polarization of
S^{[2]}
which is
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w}(D,a))
-suitable for
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
.
Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S}))
. Then

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is an
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable vector bundle.

Below is a result which follows from Proposition
4.2
.

Corollary 4.3
.

With hypotheses as in Proposition
4.2
, and
{\bf w}\coloneq{\bf w}(D,a)
, the map

(4.2.6)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})&\longrightarrow&M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})\\
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]&\mapsto&[{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})]\end{matrix}

defines an isomorphism

(4.2.7)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})&\overset{\psi}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}&M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}\\
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]&\mapsto&[{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})]\end{matrix}

where
M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
is an open dense subset of an
irreducible component of
M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
.

Proof.

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S}))
. The map in (
4.2.6
) identifies the deformation space of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
with the deformation space of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
, see Item (4) of
[
OG26
, Prop. 2.6]
. This proves that
M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
is smooth at each point of the image and that the image is
an open dense subset of an
irreducible component of
M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
. Hence in order to finish the proof it suffices to show that the map
\psi
in (
4.2.7
) is injective. This holds because if

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\not=[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S}))
then
\Hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}),{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}))=0
by the BKR correspondence.
∎

We prove Proposition
4.2
after a series of preliminary results.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
X_{1},X_{2}
be irreducible smooth projective varieties. For
i\in\{1,2\}
let
h_{i}
be an ample class on
X_{i}
and
{\mathscr{V}}_{i}
be an
h_{i}
slope-stable vector bundle on
X_{i}
.
Let
\pr_{i}\colon X_{1}\times X_{2}\to X_{i}
be the projection and let
h\coloneq\pr_{1}^{*}h_{1}+\pr_{2}^{*}h_{2}
.
Then
{\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2}
is
h
slope-stable.

Proof.

If
{\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2}
is not
h
slope-stable there exists an exact sequence

(4.2.8)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow 0

of torsion free sheaves such that
0<r({\mathscr{A}})<r({\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2})
and

(4.2.9)

\mu_{h}({\mathscr{A}})\geq\mu_{h}({\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2}).

For
i\in\{1,2\}
let
n_{i}\coloneq\dim X_{i}
, and let
d_{i}\coloneq\int_{X_{i}}h_{i}^{n_{i}}
be the degree of
X_{i}
. We have

(4.2.10)

\mu_{h}({\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2})=d_{2}{m\choose n_{2}}\mu_{h_{1}}({\mathscr{V}}_{1})+d_{1}{m\choose n_{1}}\mu_{h_{2}}({\mathscr{V}}_{2}).

where
m\coloneq n_{1}+n_{2}-1
.
Let
p_{i}\in X_{i}
be general points.
Since

{\mathscr{A}}
is locally free in codimension
1
we have

(4.2.11)

\mu_{h}({\mathscr{A}})=d_{2}{m\choose n_{2}}\mu_{h_{1}}({\mathscr{A}}_{|{X_{1}}\times\{p_{2}\}})+d_{1}{m\choose n_{1}}\mu_{h_{2}}({\mathscr{A}}_{|\{p_{1}\}\times X_{2}}).

Let
r_{i}\coloneq r({\mathscr{V}}_{i})
. Since the restrictions of
{\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2}
to
X_{1}\times\{p_{2}\}
and to
\{p_{1}\}\times X_{2}
are isomorphic to the polystable vector bundles
{\mathscr{V}}_{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathbb{C}}^{r_{2}}
and
{\mathbb{C}}^{r_{1}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathscr{V}}_{2}
respectively, it follows from (
4.2.9
), (
4.2.10
), (
4.2.11
) that
\mu_{h_{1}}({\mathscr{A}}_{|X_{1}\times\{p_{2}\}})=\mu_{h_{1}}({\mathscr{V}}_{1})
and

\mu_{h_{2}}({\mathscr{A}}_{|\{p_{1}\}\times X_{2}})=\mu_{h_{2}}({\mathscr{V}}_{2})
. These equalities give that there exist vector subspaces

0\not=U\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{r_{2}}
and
0\not=W\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{r_{1}}
such that in codimension
1
we have

{\mathscr{A}}_{|X_{1}\times\{p_{2}\}}={\mathscr{V}}_{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}U
and
{\mathscr{A}}_{|\{p_{1}\}\times X_{2}}=W\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathscr{V}}_{2}
respectively. It follows that

r({\mathscr{A}})=r({\mathscr{V}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{V}}_{2})
. This is a contradiction.
∎

Let
\tau\colon X(S)\to S^{2}
be the the blow up of the diagonal
D(S)
. We have a commutative diagram

(4.2.12)

\textstyle{X(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{S^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{S^{[2]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{S^{(2)}}

where
\rho,\pi
are the quotient maps for the natural
{\mathbb{Z}}/(2)
actions, and
\gamma
is the cycle (or Hilbert-to-Chow) map.
For
i\in\{1,2\}
let
\pr_{i}\colon S^{2}\to S
be the
i
-th projection, and
let
\tau_{i}\colon X(S)\to S
be the composition
\tau_{i}\coloneq\pr_{i}\circ\tau
.
Recall that
v(t)\coloneq tv_{1}-(0,0,1)
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
0<t\leq a
. Let
h_{S}
be an
{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic polarization of
S
.
Let

(4.2.13)

[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})).

Then

\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{H}}
and
\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{H}}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}

are
\rho^{*}\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stable vector bundles with equal
\rho^{*}\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
-slopes.

Proof.

Since
{\mathscr{H}}
is not in
{\mathscr{D}}_{v(t)}(S,h_{S})
it is locally free by Item (3) of Proposition
3.15
. The covering involution of

\rho
maps

\rho^{*}\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
to itself and it lifts to an isomorphism between

\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{H}}
and

\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{H}}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
. Hence the latter have equal
\rho^{*}\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
-slopes.

It remains to prove that
\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{H}}
and
\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{H}}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}

are
\rho^{*}\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stable. By symmetry it suffices to prove it for
\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{H}}=\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}})
.
Let
P\coloneq\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
and
h_{S^{2}}\coloneq\pr_{1}^{*}h_{S}+\pr_{2}^{*}h_{S}
. Let

{\mathscr{F}}\subset\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}})
be a subsheaf with
0<r({\mathscr{F}})<r_{1}r_{2}=r(\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}))
.
We claim that

(4.2.14)

\mu_{\rho^{*}P}({\mathscr{F}})=\mu_{h_{S^{2}}}(\tau_{*}{\mathscr{F}})<\mu_{h_{S^{2}}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}})=\mu_{\rho^{*}P}(\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}})).

In fact the intersection of three general divisors in
|Nh_{S^{2}}|
(for
N>0
such that
Nh_{S^{2}}
is very ample) is contained in the open

S^{2}\setminus D(S)
over which
\tau
is an isomorphism. Since
\rho^{*}P=\tau^{*}h_{S^{2}}
this implies
the first equality.

The vector bundle
{\mathscr{H}}
is
h_{S}
slope stable (because
c_{1}({\mathscr{H}})=D
is primitive and
h_{S}
is
{\mathsf{a}}(v(1))
-generic if
t=1
, and by Proposition
3.10
if
t\geq 2
) and hence

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}
is
h_{S^{2}}
slope stable by Proposition
4.4
. Since
\tau_{*}{\mathscr{F}}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}
we get that the inequality in (
4.2.14
) holds.

The second equality in (
4.2.14
) holds because

\rho^{*}P=\tau^{*}h_{S^{2}}
.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
0<t\leq a
. Let
h_{S}
and
{\mathscr{H}}
be as in Lemma
4.5
.
Then

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
is
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stable.

Proof.

Let
{\mathscr{G}}={\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
and
P\coloneq\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
. Let
{\mathscr{A}}\subset{\mathscr{G}}
with
0<r({\mathscr{A}})<r({\mathscr{G}})
.
By
[
OG26
, Subsect. 2.4]
we have an exact sequence

(4.2.15)

0\longrightarrow\rho^{*}{\mathscr{G}}\longrightarrow\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{R}}\longrightarrow 0

where
{\mathscr{R}}
is zero away from
E\coloneq\tau^{-1}(D(S))
is the exceptional divisor of
\tau
. Let us prove that

(4.2.16)

\mu_{P}({\mathscr{A}})=\mu_{\rho^{*}P}(\rho^{*}{\mathscr{A}})\leq\mu_{\rho^{*}P}(\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}))=\\
=\mu_{\rho^{*}P}(\rho^{*}{\mathscr{G}})=\mu_{P}({\mathscr{G}}).

In fact the inequality above holds because
\rho^{*}{\mathscr{A}}\subset\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})

by the exact sequence in (
4.2.15
),
and because
\tau^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}
,
\tau^{*}{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
are
\rho^{*}\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stable (of the same slope)
by Lemma
4.5
.
In addition the second equality in (
4.2.16
) holds because
E\cdot\rho^{*}P^{3}=0
. This finishes the proof of the validity of (
4.2.16
). Since the vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}
is not isomorphic to the vector bundle

{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
, the inequality above is an equality only if in codimension
1
we have

\rho^{*}{\mathscr{A}}={\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}
or
\rho^{*}{\mathscr{A}}={\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
. This is absurd because
\rho^{*}{\mathscr{A}}
is a subsheaf invariant under the action of the covering involution.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

Let
a=1
. Then all sheaves parametrized by
{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
are slope stable, but if
[{\mathscr{H}}]\in{\mathscr{B}}_{v_{2}}
then
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
is
not
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stable, see Subsection
4.3
. For the validity of the proof of Proposition
4.6
it is crucial that
{\mathscr{H}}
is locally free.

Remark 4.8
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}
be the rank-
4a^{2}
vector bundles defined in
[
O’G22a
, Def. 5.1]
,
i.e.
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}=\rho_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\Sigma_{2}}
is the sheaf of invariants for the “natural” lift to

\tau_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\tau_{2}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
of the action of the symmetric group on two elements
\Sigma_{2}
on
X(S)
, and
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}
is defined multiplying the natural action by the sign function. Arguing as above one proves that
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}
is
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stable.

4.2.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.2

The sheaf
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is locally free (it is a spherical vector bundle), and
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is locally free by Item (3) of Proposition
3.15
. It follows that

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is locally free, see
[
OG26
, Prop. 2.4]
.

Lastly we prove that
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable. Since

h_{S^{[2]}}
and
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
have positive BBF square,

h_{S^{[2]}}
and
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stability are equivalent respectively to

h_{S^{[2]}}
and
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
HK-slope stability, see Remark
2.3
.
By Proposition
4.6

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is

\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
slope stable. Since
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is modular and
h_{S^{[2]}}
is
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w}(D,a))
-suitable for
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
,
it follows from Corollary
2.9
that

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable.

4.3.
Instability of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}

Throughout the present subsection Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
holds, and the polarization
h_{S}
is

{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic. Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}
, i.e.
k\coloneq\hom_{S}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})>0
. By Proposition
3.15
we have
k\leq a
, and
k=a
if and only if

{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is not locally free. We treat separately the cases
k<a
and
k=a
.

4.3.1.
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is not slope semistable if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{a})

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{k+1})
where
0<k<a
. In particular

{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is locally free.
Let

(4.3.1)

V_{k}\coloneq\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}.

By Proposition
3.10

there is an exact sequence

(4.3.2)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0

where
\psi
is the tautological map, and

{\mathscr{H}}
is a slope stable vector bundle with
v({\mathscr{H}})=v(a-k)=(a-k)v_{1}-(0,0,1)
. Pulling back the exact sequence in (
4.3.2
) to
S^{2}
via the projection
\pr_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, tensorizing by
\pr_{3-i}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and taking the direct sum, we get the
exact sequence

(4.3.3)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\overset{\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}
be as in Remark
4.8
. In
[
OG26
, Prop. 2.3]
we have defined the direct sum decomposition

(4.3.4)

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})={\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}

as follows.
We have injections

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}&\overset{\iota^{+}}{\hookrightarrow}&{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\\
\xi&\mapsto&(\xi,\xi)\end{matrix}\qquad\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}&\overset{\iota^{-}}{\hookrightarrow}&{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\\
\xi&\mapsto&(\xi,-\xi)\end{matrix}

The morphism
(\iota^{+},\iota^{-})
is an isomorphism:

(4.3.5)

(\iota^{+},\iota^{-})\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}.

Moreover
(\iota^{+},\iota^{-})
is
\Sigma_{2}
-equivariant if the action on the first
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
addend (respectively the second one) is the first one
(respectively the second one) in Remark
4.8
. Thus (
4.3.5
) gives a direct sum decomposition in the category of
\Sigma_{2}
-equivariant bounded complexes on
S^{2}
. Applying the BKR correspondence one gets the decomposition in (
4.3.4
) - more precisely an isomorphism between the right-hand side and the left-hand side.
Let
\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{\pm}
be the composition

(4.3.6)

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\xrightarrow{\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{k}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes V_{k},

where the second map is the projection associated to the decomposition in (
4.3.4
). Let

(4.3.7)

{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{\pm}\coloneq\ker\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{\pm}.

Note that
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{\pm}
fits into the exact sequence

(4.3.8)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{\pm}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0.

The exact sequence in (
4.3.3
) gives the exact sequences

(4.3.9)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{-}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\overset{\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{+}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0

and

(4.3.10)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\overset{\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{-}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{k}\longrightarrow 0.

Proposition 4.9
.

If
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{k+1})
where
0<k<a
then
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is unstable (i.e. non semistable) for all polarizations
of
S^{[2]}
.

Proof.

By
[
O’G22a
, Prop. 5.8]
we have (recall (
4.2.5
))

(4.3.11)

\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})}{r({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})}=\frac{\bm{\mu}(D)}{2a}-\frac{\delta}{2}\pm\frac{\delta}{4a},

and by
[
OG26
, Prop. 2.7]
we have

(4.3.12)

\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}))}{r({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}))}=\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))}{r({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))}=\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}))}{r({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}))}=\frac{\bm{\mu}(D)}{2a}-\frac{\delta}{2}.

Hence the proposition follows from the exact sequence in (
4.3.10
).
∎

4.3.2.
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is not slope semistable if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{a}

Suppose that
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}_{v_{2}}
, i.e. that
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is not locally free.
By Proposition
3.3
there exist a point
p\in S
and an exact sequence

(4.3.13)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{a}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\longrightarrow 0,

where
f
is identified with a linear map
f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes V_{a}\to{\mathbb{C}}
, which viewed as a map
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{a}
is surjective by Lemma
3.5
. Note that
p
is the unique singular point of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
(the localization of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
at
p
is not free).
From (
4.3.13
) we get the exact sequence

(4.3.14)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{a}\overset{\Phi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\longrightarrow 0.

By the decomposition in (
4.3.4
) the above exact sequence reads

(4.3.15)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\longrightarrow({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\otimes V_{a}\overset{\Phi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\longrightarrow 0.

Let
\Phi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{\pm}
be the restriction of
\Phi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
to
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes V_{a}
.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{a}
, and let
p
be the singular point of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
. The map
\Phi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{+}\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes V_{a}\to{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})
is surjective.

Granting the validity of Proposition
4.10
, we proceed to show that
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is unstable if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{a}
.
Let

{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}
be the kernel of
\Phi^{+}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
. We have the exact sequence

(4.3.16)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes V_{a}\overset{\Phi^{+}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\longrightarrow 0.

Note that
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}
is a subsheaf of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
, see (
4.3.15
). Since
\Phi^{+}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
is surjective the quotient

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})/{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}
is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{a}
(see the exact sequence in (
4.3.15
)). Hence we have
an exact sequence

(4.3.17)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\overset{\Psi^{-}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{a}\longrightarrow 0.

Proposition 4.11
.

If
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{a}
then
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is unstable for all polarizations
of
S^{[2]}
.

Proof.

Since
r({\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+})=r({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})
and
c_{1}({\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+})=c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})
the proposition follows from the exact sequence in (
4.3.17
) and the equalities in (
4.3.11
) and (
4.3.12
).
∎

It remains to prove Proposition
4.10
.

Definition 4.12
.

If
g\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
the morphism
\Phi_{g}^{+}\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\to{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{P})
is the one corresponding via the BKR correspondence to

(4.3.18)

(\Id\boxtimes g,g\boxtimes\Id)\in\Hom_{S^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\Sigma_{2}}.

By the BKR correspondence and simplicity of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
we have an isomorphism

(4.3.19)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}&\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}&\Hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{P}))\\
g&\mapsto&\Phi_{g}^{+}\end{matrix}

Proposition 4.13
.

If
g\in{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
is non zero then
\Phi_{g}^{+}
is a surjection.

Proof.

Let
b\colon S_{p}\to S
be the blow up of
S
with center
p
, and let
i_{p}\colon R_{p}\hookrightarrow S_{p}
be the inclusion of the exceptional divisor. We view
S_{p}
as the closed subset of
S^{[2]}
given by

(4.3.20)

S_{p}=\{[Z]\in S^{[2]}\mid p\in\supp Z\},

and we let
j\colon S_{p}\hookrightarrow S^{[2]}
be the inclusion map.
Note that
R_{p}
parametrizes non reduced subschemes of
S
parametrized by
S_{p}
, i.e. those which are supported at
p
.
We have

(4.3.21)

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\cong j_{*}(b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

We also have an exact sequence of sheaves on
S_{p}
.

(4.3.22)

0\longrightarrow j^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}\bigwedge^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes i_{p,*}{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}\longrightarrow 0,

where
g
is defined via the projection of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)=\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\oplus\bigwedge^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)
onto the second direct summand.
Tensorizing the exact sequence in (
4.3.22
) with
{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}
we get an exact sequence

(4.3.23)

0\longrightarrow\bigwedge^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}(1)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}_{|R_{p}}\longrightarrow\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}\longrightarrow 0.

We are ready to prove the proposition. The sheaf
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})
is supported on
S_{p}
.
On
S_{p}\setminus R_{p}
the map
\Phi^{+}_{g}
is given by

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}_{|(\widetilde{S}_{p}\setminus R_{p})}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1|(S\setminus\{p\})}&\xrightarrow{\Phi^{+}_{g|(S_{p}\setminus R_{p})}}&b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1|(S\setminus\{p\})}\\
s_{1}\otimes s_{2}&\mapsto&g(s_{1})s_{2}\end{matrix}

and hence is surjective. Next we examine
\Phi^{+}_{g}
over
R_{p}
. The exact sequence in (
4.3.23
) gives that the restriction of
\Phi^{+}_{g}
to
R_{p}
factors through the quotient
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}_{|R_{P}}\to\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}
. Abusing notation we may write

(4.3.24)

\begin{matrix}\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}&\xrightarrow{\Phi^{+}_{g|R_{p}}}&{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}\\
s_{1}\otimes s_{2}+s_{2}\otimes s_{1}&\mapsto&2(g(s_{1}(p))s_{2}(p)+g(s_{2}(p))s_{1}(p))\end{matrix}

It follows that
\Phi_{g}^{+}
is surjective over
R_{p}
.
∎

By the BKR correspondence and simplicity of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
we also have an isomorphism

(4.3.25)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}&\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}&\Hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}),{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{P}))\\
g&\mapsto&\Phi_{g}\end{matrix}

A straightforward argument gives the following.

Claim 4.14
.

Let
g\in{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
. Let
i\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\to{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
be the inclusion given by the direct-sum decomposition
in (
4.3.4
).
The composition

(4.3.26)

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\xrightarrow{i}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\xrightarrow{\Phi_{g}}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{P})

is equal to
2\Phi^{+}_{g}
.

Proof of Proposition
4.10
.

By Lemma
3.5
the morphism
f
in (
4.3.13
) defines an injection
V_{a}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
. Since
V_{a}
is non zero the proposition follows from Claim
4.14
and Proposition
4.13
.
∎

4.4.
Cohomology computations, and extensions

Throughout the present subsection Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
holds, and the polarization
h_{S}
is

{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic.

4.4.1.
Cohomology computations and extensions, I

The BKR equivalence gives the following isomorphisms. First

(4.4.1)

\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})\overset{\BKR}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\Ext^{p}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}),

where
\pm
means that either both are
+
or both are
-
, and secondly

(4.4.2)

\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\overset{\BKR}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\begin{cases}\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})&\text{if $p=2$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
0\leq k<a
and let
[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a-k)})
. Then the BKR equivalence defines isomorphisms

(4.4.3)

\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})\overset{\BKR}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\begin{cases}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})&\text{if $p\in\{1,3\}$},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

and

(4.4.4)

\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm},{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))\overset{\BKR}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\begin{cases}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})&\text{if $p\in\{1,3\}$},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

The isomorphisms in (
4.4.3
) follow from the vanishings

\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=0
. The first group vanishes because
[{\mathscr{H}}]\notin{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a-k)}
.
The second group vanishes because

\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\cong\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})^{\vee}
(Serre duality) and because

\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})=0
by semistability of
{\mathscr{H}}
. The isomorphisms in (
4.4.4
) are obtained by a similar argument, or
from the isomorphisms in (
4.4.3
) and Serre duality.
∎

Definition 4.16
.

Assume that
1\leq k<a
and
[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a-k)})
(hence
\ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})=2k
by Remark
3.9
).
If
U_{k}\subset\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
is a
k
dimensional subspace, we let
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
be the (locally free) sheaf on
S^{[2]}
fitting into the exact sequence

(4.4.5)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}\overset{\lambda^{+}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes U_{k}\longrightarrow 0

with extension class in
\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))\otimes U_{k}^{\vee}=\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\otimes U_{k}^{\vee}
given by the inclusion
U_{k}\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
.

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
and
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
be as in Definition
4.16
. Let
*
be either
+
or
-
. Then the BKR equivalence defines isomorphisms

\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})\overset{\BKR}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\begin{cases}U_{k}^{\vee}&\text{if $p=0$ and $*=+$},\\
\Ann(U_{k})&\text{if $p=1$, or $p=3$ and $*=+$},\\
\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})&\text{if $p=3$ and $*=-$},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

where
\Ann(U_{k})\subset\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})^{\vee}

is the annihilator of
U_{k}
.

Proof.

To simplify notation we omit the subscript
S^{[2]}
from the
\Ext
-groups, and we let
{\mathscr{G}}={\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
,
{\mathscr{B}}^{+}={\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
. Applying the functor
\Hom(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})
to the exact sequence in (
4.4.5
) we get the long exact sequence

(4.4.6)

\ldots\overset{\partial_{p-1}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{p}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})\otimes U^{\vee}_{k}\longrightarrow\Ext^{p}({\mathscr{B}}^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})\longrightarrow\\
\longrightarrow\Ext^{p}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})\overset{\partial_{p}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{p+1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})\otimes U_{k}^{\vee}\longrightarrow\ldots

We claim that the coboundary maps

\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})\overset{\partial_{1}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{2}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{*})\otimes U_{k}^{\vee}=U_{k}^{\vee}

and

\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\Ext^{3}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})\overset{\partial_{3}^{+}}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{4}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})\otimes U_{k}^{\vee}=U_{k}^{\vee}

are identified with the transpose of the inclusion
U_{k}\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
. In fact the coboundary maps are given by Yoneda product with the extension class of (
4.4.5
), and our assertion follows because Yoneda products match under the BKR equivalence.
The Proposition is a straightforward consequence of the long exact sequence in (
4.4.6
), equations (
4.4.1
),
(
4.4.2
), Lemma
4.15
, and the identification of the coboundaries

\partial_{1}^{*}
,
\partial_{3}^{+}
given above.
∎

Definition 4.18
.

If
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
and
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
are as in Definition
4.16
,

{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
is the (locally free) sheaf on
S^{[2]}
fitting into the exact sequence

(4.4.7)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})\overset{\lambda}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}\longrightarrow 0

with extension class (by Proposition
4.17
) in
\Ann(U_{k})\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}

given by
the identity map.

Claim 4.19
.

Let
1\leq k<a
,

[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a-k)})
, and
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
.
Then

(4.4.8)

\ch({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k}))=\ch({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})).

Proof.

We start by noting that
\ch({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}))
is independent of which
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
we choose.
By Proposition
3.13

there exists
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
such that
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
fits into the exact sequence (
4.3.2
) (
{\mathscr{H}}

is as in the claim). Then both

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
and
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
have filtrations with associate graded pieces
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
,
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{k}
. In fact for
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
this holds by construction, while for
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
it was shown in Subsection
4.3
.
The equality in (
4.4.8
) follows.
∎

The vector bundle
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
is a quotient of
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
. An analogous subsheaf of
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
is also relevant for what follows.

Definition 4.20
.

Assume that
1\leq k<a
,
[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a-k)}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a-k)})
and
U_{k}\subset\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
is a
k
dimensional subspace. We let

{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}\subset{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
be defined by

{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}\coloneq\ker(\lambda^{+}\circ\lambda),

where
\lambda^{+},\lambda
are the morphisms in (
4.4.5
) and (
4.4.7
) respectively.

From the exact sequences in (
4.4.5
) and (
4.4.7
) we get that
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}
sits in the exact sequence

(4.4.9)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}\overset{\lambda^{-}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow 0

By (
4.4.3
) the extension class of the above exact sequence is an element of

(4.4.10)

\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}.

Claim 4.21
.

The extension class of (
4.4.9
), which is an element of the group in (
4.4.10
), is given by the inclusion

\Ann(U_{k})\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
.

Proof.

Let
e
be the extension class of the exact sequence in (
4.4.7
), i.e. the identity

\Ann(U_{k})\xrightarrow{\Id}\Ann(U_{k})
. The extension class of (
4.4.9
) is the image of
e
via the map

\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}.

(We let
{\mathscr{B}}^{+}={\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
and
{\mathscr{G}}={\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
.)
The map

\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\overset{\BKR}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})

is injective with image

\Ann(U_{k})
(see the proof of Proposition
4.17
). The claim follows.
∎

The remainder of the present subsubsection contains different
constructions of
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{\pm}
.
Let
{\mathscr{H}}
and
U_{k}
be as in Definition
4.16
.
Let

(4.4.11)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes U_{k}\longrightarrow 0

be the exact sequence with extension class in
U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
given by the inclusion map. Proceeding as in Subsubsection
4.3.1
we associate to the exact sequence in (
4.4.11
) an exact sequence

(4.4.12)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})\overset{\Psi_{{\mathscr{F}}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes U_{k}\longrightarrow 0.

The decomposition in (
4.3.4
) gives the decomposition
\Psi_{{\mathscr{F}}}=\Psi^{+}_{{\mathscr{F}}}\oplus\Psi^{-}_{{\mathscr{F}}}
, where

\Psi^{\pm}_{{\mathscr{F}}}\colon{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes U_{k}
.

Definition 4.22
.

Assume that
{\mathscr{H}}
and
U_{k}
are as in Definition
4.16
, and
{\mathscr{F}}
is the sheaf in (
4.4.11
) defined above. We let

(4.4.13)

{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{\pm}\coloneq\ker\Psi_{{\mathscr{F}}}^{\mp}.

The sheaf

{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{\pm}
fits into the exact sequence

(4.4.14)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{\pm}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes U_{k}\longrightarrow 0.

Proposition 4.23
.

The extension class of the exact sequence in (
4.4.14
), which by Lemma
4.15
is an element of
U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
, is given by the inclusion. In particular
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
.

Proof.

We have a direct sum decomposition

(4.4.15)

U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\\
=\bigoplus_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}),

where the indices
i,j
correspond to first/second addends of the decompositions of the two sheaves. The sheaves above are
\Sigma_{2}
equivariant sheaves.
The non trivial element of
\Sigma_{2}
permutes the
(1,1)
,
(2,2)
addends and the
(1,2)
,
(2,1)
ones.
We also have a direct sum decomposition

(4.4.16)

U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})=\bigoplus_{l=1}^{2}U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}),

where the index
l
corresponds to first/second addend of the decomposition of the second sheaf.
Let

\begin{matrix}\bigoplus_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})&\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{\pm}}&\bigoplus_{l=1}^{2}U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\\
(e_{1,1},e_{1,2},e_{2,1},e_{2,2})&\mapsto&(e_{1,1}\pm e_{2,1},e_{1,2}\pm e_{2,2})\end{matrix}

By the definition of the decomposition in (
4.3.4
) the map

\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}),{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))\xrightarrow{\Psi^{\pm}_{{\mathscr{F}}}}\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm},{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))

corresponds, via the BKR equivalence, to the restriction of
\Lambda^{\pm}
to the
\Sigma_{2}
-invariant subspaces.
Let
\overline{e}\in U_{k}^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}),{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))
be the extension class of (
4.4.12
). By the BKR equivalence

\overline{e}
corresponds to a
\Sigma_{2}
-invariant element
e
of the Ext-group in (
4.4.15
). By definition of (
4.4.12
) we have
e=(f,0,0,f)
where
f
is the extension class of the exact sequence in (
4.4.11
), i.e. given by the inclusion
U_{k}\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
.
Since

(4.4.17)

\Lambda^{+}(f,0,0,f)=(f,f),\qquad\Lambda^{-}(f,0,0,f)=(f,-f)

the proposition follows.
∎

Next we give an analogue of Proposition
4.23
with
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
replaced by
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}
. Of course here
{\mathscr{H}}
and
U_{k}
are as in Definition
4.16
. Let

(4.4.18)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow 0

be the exact sequence with extension class in

\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}
given by the inclusion
\Ann(U_{k})\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
.
The exact sequence in (
4.4.18
) gives rise to the exact sequence

(4.4.19)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow 0.

By the decomposition

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})={\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\oplus{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}
and the above exact sequence we may view
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}
as a subsheaf of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})
.

Definition 4.24
.

Assume that
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
are as in Definition
4.16
, and
{\mathscr{F}}
is the vector bundle in (
4.4.18
) defined above. We let

{\mathscr{C}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{\pm}\coloneq{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})/{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\mp}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}.

The inclusion
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\mp}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
gives the exact sequence

(4.4.20)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{\pm}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow 0.

Proposition 4.25
.

The extension class of the exact sequence in (
4.4.20
), which by
Lemma
4.15
is an element of
\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
, is given by the inclusion map. In particular

{\mathscr{C}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}
.

Proof.

The first statement is proved by an argument analogous to the proof of the first statement of Proposition
4.23
.
The isomorphism
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}
follows from the first statement and Claim
4.21
.
∎

4.4.2.
Cohomology computations and extensions, II

Proposition 4.26
.

Let
p\in S
. Then we have

\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})\cong\begin{cases}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)&\text{if $p\in\{2,4\}$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

By the BKR correspondence,
\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})
is the subspace of
\Sigma_{2}
invariants of

(4.4.21)

\bigoplus_{q+r=p}\left(\Ext^{q}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes\Ext^{r}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\oplus\Ext^{q}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes\Ext^{r}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\right).

The proposition follows because
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is a spherical vector bundle,
\Ext^{r}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p_{0}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
vanishes except for
r=2
, and

\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\cong\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})^{\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)
.
∎

Definition 4.27
.

Let
p\in S
and
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be an
a
-dimensional vector subspace. By the isomorphism in (
4.3.19
) we get
a morphism

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes W_{a}\xrightarrow{\Phi_{W_{a}}^{+}}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}).

Note that
\Phi_{W_{a}}^{+}
is a surjection by Proposition
4.13
.

Definition 4.28
.

Given
p\in S
and
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
an
a
-dimensional vector subspace,
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}
is the sheaf fitting into the exact sequence

(4.4.22)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes W_{a}\xrightarrow{\Phi_{W_{a}}^{+}}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\longrightarrow 0.

Proposition 4.29
.

Let
p\in S
and
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be an
a
-dimensional subspace.

\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\cong\begin{cases}\Ann W_{a}&\text{if $p=1$,}\\
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)&\text{if $p=3$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where
\Ann W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)
is the annihilator of
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
.

Proof.

Applying the functor
\Hom(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})
to the exact sequence in (
4.4.22
) we get the long exact sequence

(4.4.23)

\ldots\longrightarrow\Ext^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\longrightarrow\Ext^{p+1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\longrightarrow\\
\longrightarrow\Ext^{p+1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\otimes W_{a}^{\vee}\longrightarrow\ldots

By (
4.4.2
) the group
\Ext^{q}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})
vanishes unless
q=2
, and in that case it is canonically identified with
{\mathbb{C}}
.
We claim that the homomorphism

(4.4.24)

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)=\Ext^{2}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})\longrightarrow\Ext^{2}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})\otimes W_{a}^{\vee}=W_{a}^{\vee}

is identified with the transpose of the inclusion map
W_{a}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
. Before proving the claim we note that the proposition follows from the claim and the exact sequence in (
4.4.23
). The BKR correspondence identifies the morphism
\Phi_{W_{a}}^{+}
with the
\Sigma_{2}
-equivariant morphism

(4.4.25)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes W_{a}&\longrightarrow&{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\\
\sigma\boxtimes\tau\otimes g&\mapsto&(\sigma\boxtimes g(\tau),g(\sigma)\boxtimes\tau)\end{matrix}

and the homomorphism in (
4.4.24
) with the homomorphism

\Ext^{2}_{S^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1},({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\rm tw})^{\Sigma_{2}}\longrightarrow\Ext^{2}_{S^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes W_{a},({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\rm tw})^{\Sigma_{2}}

induced by the morphism in (
4.4.25
) (
({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\rm tw}
is
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
with the
\Sigma_{2}
-action given by the “natural” one multiplied by the sign character). Hence the claim reduces to the statement that, given
g\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
(i.e.
g\in{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
) the induced homomorphism

(4.4.26)

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)=\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\longrightarrow\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})={\mathbb{C}}

is identified with
g
. One way to see this is to go through Serre duality.
∎

Definition 4.30
.

Given
p\in S
and an
a
-dimensional subspace
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
,
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
is the sheaf fitting into the exact sequence

(4.4.27)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})\overset{\lambda}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}\longrightarrow 0

with extension class the identity map in (see Proposition
4.29
)

\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\otimes(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}=(\Ann W_{a})\otimes(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}.

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{a}
. Thus we have the exact sequence in (
4.3.13
). The morphism
f
appearing in (
4.3.13
) is identified with a linear map
f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes V_{a}\to{\mathbb{C}}
, which viewed as a map
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V^{\vee}_{a}
is surjective by Lemma
3.5
. It follows that the transpose
f(p)^{t}\colon V_{a}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
is injective.
Note that
p
is the unique singular point of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
.

Proposition 4.31
.

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}^{a}
, and let
p
be the unique singular point of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
.
Abusing notation let
V_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be the image of
f(p)^{t}
. Then
{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})^{+}
is isomorphic to the sheaf
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}
defined in Subsubsection
4.3.2
. More precisely we have a commutative diagram

(4.4.28)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes V_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{+}_{V_{a}}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{2\Id}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes V_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{+}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where the left vertical arrow is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows from Claim
4.14
.
∎

Claim 4.32
.

Let
p\in S
,
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be an
a
-dimensional vector subspace, and
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
.
Then

(4.4.29)

\ch({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a}))=\ch({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})).

Proof.

The right hand side of (
4.4.29
) does not depend on which
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
we choose. Let

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}^{a}
, with unique singular point
p
. By (
4.4.27
), (
4.4.22
) and (
4.3.14
) we have

\ch({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a}))=a\ch({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})+a\ch({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})-\ch({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}))=\ch({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})).

∎

4.5.
Slope stability of extensions

Throughout the present subsection Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
holds, and the polarization
h_{S}
is

{\mathsf{a}}(v_{2})
-generic. We prove that the sheafs defined in Subsubsections
4.4.1
and
4.4.2
are slope stable for certain polarizations of
S^{[2]}
.

4.5.1.
Slope stability of
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})

The main result of the present subsubsection is the following.

Proposition 4.33
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
.
Let
h_{S^{[2]}}
be a polarization of
S^{[2]}
which is
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w}(D,a))
-suitable for
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
. Let
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
be as in Definition
4.16
.
The sheaf
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
of Definition
4.18
is
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable.

Proposition
4.33
is proved by applying Proposition
2.8
. Hence we start
by proving results on slope semistability for

(4.5.1)

P\coloneq\bm{\mu}(h_{S}).

Proposition 4.34
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
, and
let
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
be as in Definition
4.16
.
The sheaf
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
of Definition
4.18
is
P
slope (and HK slope) semistable.

Proof.

Since the BBF square of
P
is positive,
P
slope (semi)semistability is the same as

P
HK slope (semi)stability, see Remark
2.3
. We prove that
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
is
P
HK slope (semi)semistable.
Let
{\mathscr{B}}={\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
and
{\mathscr{B}}^{-}={\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}
.
Let
0={\mathscr{B}}_{0}\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{3}={\mathscr{B}}
be the filtration with

{\mathscr{B}}_{2}\coloneq{\mathscr{B}}^{-}
,

{\mathscr{B}}_{1}\coloneq{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}

(see (
4.4.9
)).
Let
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}
be a subsheaf with
0<r({\mathscr{S}})<r({\mathscr{B}})
.
Let
0\eqcolon{\mathscr{S}}_{0}\subset{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{S}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{S}}_{3}\coloneq{\mathscr{S}}
be the filtration induced by the filtration of
{\mathscr{B}}
.
Thus
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
is the kernel of the restriction of
\lambda
to
{\mathscr{S}}
, where
\lambda
is as in (
4.4.7
), and

{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{2}={\mathscr{B}}^{-}
is the kernel of the restriction of
\lambda^{-}
to
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
, where
\lambda^{-}
is as in (
4.4.9
).
For
i\in\{1,2,3\}
let
{\mathscr{A}}_{i}\coloneq{\mathscr{S}}_{i}/{\mathscr{S}}_{i-1}
. We have

(4.5.2)

{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee},\quad{\mathscr{A}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}),\quad{\mathscr{A}}_{3}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes U_{k}.

Recalling (
4.3.11
) and (
4.3.12
) we get that

\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})=\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))=\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{B}})=(D\cdot h_{S})/2a.

The vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm}
is
P
slope stable (see Remark
4.8
), and by Proposition
4.6
so is
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
.
It follows that if
r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\not=0
then
\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\leq(D\cdot h_{S})/2a
. Thus

(4.5.3)

\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{S}})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})}{r({\mathscr{S}})}\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})}{r({\mathscr{S}})}\frac{D\cdot h_{S}}{2a}=\frac{D\cdot h_{S}}{2a}=\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{B}}).

(If
r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})=0
we set
r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\mu_{P}({\mathscr{A}}_{i})/r({\mathscr{S}})=0
.)
∎

Since
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
is
P
slope (and HK slope) semistable, in order to apply Proposition
2.8
one needs to control
subsheaves
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
such that

\mu_{P}({\mathscr{S}})=\mu_{P}({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k}))
. Referring to the commutative diagram in (
4.2.12
) (and recalling (
4.2.5
)), we have
\pi\circ\tau(\Delta)=D(S)
, where
D(S)\subset S^{2}
is the diagonal.

Proposition 4.35
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
, and
let
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
be as in Definition
4.16
.
Let
T^{+}\subset U_{k}
be a non zero subspace, and let
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}
be a subsheaf such that

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}/{\mathscr{S}}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
. Then the inclusion

\iota\colon{\mathscr{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes U_{k}
does not lift to a homomorphism
{\mathscr{S}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
(see the exact sequence in (
4.4.5
)).

Proof.

Let
\rho\colon X(S)\to S^{[2]}
and
\tau\colon S^{[2]}\to S^{2}
be as in (
4.2.12
). Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be the
vector bundle appearing in (
4.4.11
) (see Definition
4.22
).
We claim that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\rho^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\rho^{*}{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\rho^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes U_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{F}}\oplus{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes U_{k}.}

In fact the first and third vertical arrows exist by definition of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}
respectively, and existence of a vertical arrow
f
making the diagram commute follows from Proposition
4.23
(recall that
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}\subset{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})
).
If there exists a lift
\widetilde{\iota}\colon{\mathscr{S}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
of
\iota
then

\rho^{*}(\iota)\colon\rho^{*}{\mathscr{S}}\longrightarrow\rho^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes U_{k}

lifts to

\rho^{*}(\widetilde{\iota})\colon\rho^{*}{\mathscr{S}}\longrightarrow\rho^{*}{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
.
Pushing forward by
\tau
we get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{F}}\oplus{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes U_{k}}
\textstyle{\tau_{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathscr{S}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{g}}
\scriptstyle{g}

By hypothesis the map
g
factors as

(4.5.4)

\tau_{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathscr{S}})\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes T^{+}\overset{G}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes U_{k},

and the quotient
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes T^{+}/\tau_{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathscr{S}})
has support of codimension at least
2
because
\tau
contracts
\rho^{-1}(\Delta)
. The lift
\widetilde{g}
of
g
defines a lift of
G

away from the support of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes T^{+}/\tau_{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathscr{S}})
, which has codimension at least
2
. It follows that this lift extends to a lift
\widetilde{G}\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes T^{+}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{F}}\oplus{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
of the inclusion
G
. The extension class of the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{F}}\oplus{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes U_{k}\longrightarrow 0

is given by

(\overline{e},\overline{e})\in\Ext^{1}_{S^{2}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes U_{k}^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{l=1}^{2}\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\otimes U_{k}^{\vee},

where
\overline{e}
is the extension class of (
4.4.11
) - see the proof of Proposition
4.23
. Since
\overline{e}
is given by the inclusion
\Ann(U_{k})\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
there is no such lift. This contradiction shows that
\iota
does not lift.

∎

Proposition 4.36
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
, and
let
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
be as in Definition
4.16
.
Let
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}
be a subsheaf, and let
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\coloneq\ker(\lambda^{-}_{|{\mathscr{S}}_{2}})
, where
\lambda^{-}
is as in (
4.4.9
). If

(1)

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})/\lambda^{-}({\mathscr{S}}_{2})
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
, and

(2)

there exists a subspace
T^{-}\subset\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}
such that
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-}/{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
,

then
T^{-}=\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition
4.35
. Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be the vector bundle appearing in (
4.4.18
) (see definition
4.24
). Let
{\mathscr{D}}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{F}}\oplus{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
be the image of the “diagonal” embedding

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{F}}\oplus{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
induced by the embedding

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}
.
By Proposition
4.25
there exists a commutative diagram
of sheaves on
X(S)
:

\textstyle{\rho^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\rho^{*}{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\rho^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}(({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{F}}\oplus{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})/{\mathscr{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{H}}\oplus{\mathscr{H}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}),}

One finishes the proof arguing as in the proof of Proposition
4.35
.
∎

Recall that
P\coloneq\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
.

Proposition 4.37
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
, and
let
{\mathscr{H}}
,
U_{k}
be as in Definition
4.16
.
If
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
is a sheaf with
0<r({\mathscr{S}})<r({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k}))
such that

\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{S}})=\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k}))
then one of the following holds:

(a)

There exists a non zero subspace
T^{-}\subset\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}
such that
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-}
and

({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-})/{\mathscr{S}}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
.

(b)

{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}
and the quotient
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{-}/{\mathscr{S}}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from

\Delta
.

(c)

There exists a non zero subspace
T^{+}\subsetneq U_{k}
such that
{\mathscr{S}}\subset\lambda^{-1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+})

and
\lambda^{-1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+})/{\mathscr{S}}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
.

Proof.

We adopt the notation introduced in the proof of Proposition
4.34
.
