We claim that the following hold:

(1)

There exists a subspace
T^{-}\subset\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}
such that

{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-}
and the quotient

({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-})/{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
.

(2)

If
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}\not=0
then
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})/{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
.

(3)

There exists a subspace
T^{+}\subset U_{k}
such that

{\mathscr{S}}_{3}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}
and

({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+})/{\mathscr{S}}_{3}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
.

In fact by hypothesis the inequality in (
4.5.3
) is an equality.
It follows that for each
i\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
r({\mathscr{S}}_{i})>0
(i.e.
{\mathscr{S}}_{i}\not=0
because
{\mathscr{S}}_{i}
is a subsheaf of a locally-free sheaf) we have

\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{S}}_{i})=\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{B}}_{i}/{\mathscr{B}}_{i-1})
.
By
P
HK slope polystability of the sheaves
{\mathscr{B}}_{i}/{\mathscr{B}}_{i-1}

(recall the inclusions in (
4.5.2
)) one gets Items (1), (2), (3) above except for the statement on the support of the quotient sheaf. The latter holds because
P^{3}\cdot D\geq 0
for every non zero effective divisor
D
on
S^{[2]}
, with equality only if
D=\Delta
.

What is left to prove is the following: if
i\in\{2,3\}
and
{\mathscr{S}}_{i}\not=0
then the cokernel of the inclusion

{\mathscr{S}}_{i-1}\hookrightarrow({\mathscr{B}}_{i}/{\mathscr{B}}_{i-1})
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
. Suppose that
{\mathscr{S}}_{3}\not=0
.
If
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}=0
then the inclusion

\iota\colon{\mathscr{S}}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes U_{k}
lifts to a homomorphism
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})^{+}
. This contradicts
Proposition
4.35
. Thus
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}\not=0
and hence
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})/{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
by Item (2) above. If
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}\not=0
one gets that
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\not=0
and
T^{-}=\Ann(U_{k})^{\vee}
by
Proposition
4.36
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.33
.

First note that since the BBF squares of
P=\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
and
h_{S^{[2]}}
are positive,
P
(
h_{S^{[2]}}
) slope (semi)semistability is the same as

P
(
h_{S^{[2]}}
) HK slope (semi)stability.
Suppose that
{\mathscr{B}}={\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},U_{k})
is not
h_{S^{[2]}}
HK slope stable. Since
{\mathscr{B}}
is modular (see Claim
4.19
),

P
slope semistable
(see Proposition
4.34
), and
h_{S^{[2]}}
is
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w}(D,a))
-suitable for
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
, it follows from Proposition
2.8
that there exists a subsheaf
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}
, with
0<r({\mathscr{S}})<r({\mathscr{B}})
, such that

(4.5.5)

\mu_{P}({\mathscr{S}})=\mu_{P}({\mathscr{B}}),\qquad\mu_{h_{S^{[2]}}}({\mathscr{S}})\geq\mu_{h_{S^{[2]}}}P({\mathscr{B}}).

By the equality in (
4.5.5
) one of Items (a), (b), (c) of Proposition
4.37
holds.
One checks that in each case the inequality in (
4.5.5
) does not hold. If Item (a) or (b) holds, this follows at once from the equalities in (
4.3.11
) and (
4.3.12
). Lastly suppose that Item (c) of Proposition
4.37
holds. Then we have

(4.5.6)

c_{1}({\mathscr{S}})=c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\cdot k+c_{1}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))+c_{1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})\cdot\dim T^{+}-m\delta,

for some
m\geq 0
. Computing one gets that

(4.5.7)

\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{S}})}{r({\mathscr{S}})}=\frac{c_{1}({\mathscr{B}})}{r({\mathscr{B}})}-\left(\frac{a(k-\dim T^{+})+m}{r({\mathscr{S}})}\right)\delta.

We have
\dim\Ann(U_{k})=\dim U_{k}=k
(see Definition
4.16
) and thus
\dim T^{+}<k
. Hence the equality in (
4.5.7
) contradicts the inequality in (
4.5.5
).
∎

4.5.2.
Stability of certain extensions, II

The main result of the present subsubsection is the following.

Proposition 4.38
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
.
Let
h_{S^{[2]}}
be a polarization of
S^{[2]}
which is
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w}(D,a))
-suitable for
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
.
Let
p\in S
and
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be an
a
-dimensional subspace.
The sheaf
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
of Definition
4.30
is
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable.

Before proving the above result we go through some preliminary results.

Proposition 4.39
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
, and let
P=\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
. Let
p\in S
and
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be an
a
-dimensional subspace. The sheaf
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
is
P
slope (and HK slope) semistable.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Proposition
4.34
.
Since the BBF square of
P
is positive,
P
slope (semi)semistability is the same as

P
HK slope (semi)semistability. We prove that
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
is
P
HK slope (semi)semistable.
Let
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
be a non zero subsheaf (i.e. with non zero rank) such that
\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{S}})=\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a}))
.
Let
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{S}}
be the kernel of the restriction of
\lambda
to
{\mathscr{S}}
, and let
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}={\mathscr{S}}
,
{\mathscr{S}}_{0}=0
.
For
i\in\{1,2\}
let
{\mathscr{A}}_{i}\coloneq{\mathscr{S}}_{i}/{\mathscr{S}}_{i-1}
. We have

(4.5.8)

{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee},\quad{\mathscr{A}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}.

The sheaves
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}
and
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}
are
P
HK slope polystable , see Remark
4.8
and, for
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}
, the exact sequence in (
4.4.22
).
It follows that if
r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\not=0
then

\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\leq\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{\pm})=(D\cdot h_{S})/2a.

Setting
r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\mu_{P}({\mathscr{A}}_{i})/r({\mathscr{S}})=0
if

r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})=0
, we get that

(4.5.9)

\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{S}})=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})}{r({\mathscr{S}})}\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{A}}_{i})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{r({\mathscr{A}}_{i})}{r({\mathscr{S}})}\frac{D\cdot h_{S}}{2a}=\frac{D\cdot h_{S}}{2a}.

Since
\mu^{HK}_{P}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a}))=(D\cdot h_{S})/2a
, we are done.
∎

Let
X
be a smooth variety and let
j\colon Y\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of a smooth (pure) codimension
2
subvariety. Let
{\mathscr{U}},{\mathscr{V}}
be locally free sheaves on
X
. Let
{\mathscr{I}}_{Y}\subset{\mathscr{O}}_{X}
be the ideal sheaf of
Y
. We have the map

(4.5.10)

\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{I}}_{Y}\otimes{\mathscr{U}},{\mathscr{V}})\longrightarrow H^{0}(X,\underline{Ext}^{1}({\mathscr{I}}_{Y}\otimes{\mathscr{U}},{\mathscr{V}}))=H^{0}(X,\underline{Ext}^{2}(j_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{Y}\otimes{\mathscr{U}},{\mathscr{V}}))=\\
=j_{*}\det{\mathscr{N}}_{Y/X}\otimes{\mathscr{U}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{V}}=H^{0}(X,\underline{Hom}({\mathscr{U}}_{|Y},{\mathscr{V}}_{|Y}\otimes j_{*}\det{\mathscr{N}}_{Y/X})).

The following result is an exercise left to the reader.

Lemma 4.40
.

Let

(4.5.11)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{I}}_{Y}\otimes{\mathscr{U}}\longrightarrow 0

be an exact sequence, and let
\varphi\in H^{0}(X,\underline{Hom}({\mathscr{U}}_{|Y},{\mathscr{V}}_{|Y}\otimes j_{*}\det{\mathscr{N}}_{Y/X}))
be the image of the extension class of (
4.5.11
) by
the map in (
4.5.10
). If
\varphi
is an injection of vector bundles then
{\mathscr{F}}
is locally free.

Recall the exact sequences (see Definitions
4.28
and
4.30
)

(4.5.12)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes W_{a}\xrightarrow{\Phi_{W_{a}}^{+}}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\longrightarrow 0,

(4.5.13)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})\overset{\lambda}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}\longrightarrow 0.

Apply the functor
\underline{Hom}(\cdot,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})
to the exact sequence in (
4.5.12
) to get the isomorphism

(4.5.14)

\underline{Ext}^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}^{+}(p,W_{a}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underline{Ext}^{2}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}).

Recall that
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\cong j_{*}(b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
, where
j\colon S_{p}\hookrightarrow S^{[2]}
is the inclusion map, see (
4.3.21
). Since
S_{p}
is smooth of codimension
2
in
S^{[2]}
, and
b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
is locally free, we have an isomorphism

(4.5.15)

\underline{Ext}^{2}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\cong j_{*}(b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}).

Hence the local-to-global spectral sequence abutting to
\Ext^{\bullet}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}^{+}(p,W_{a}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})
gives the exact sequence

(4.5.16)

0\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}^{+}(p,W_{a}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\overset{\epsilon}{\longrightarrow}H^{0}(S_{p},b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\longrightarrow\\
\longrightarrow H^{2}(S^{[2]},\underline{Hom}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}))\otimes W_{a}^{\vee}.

(Note that
\underline{Hom}({\mathscr{B}}^{+}(p,W_{a}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})=\underline{Hom}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})
.)

Remark 4.41
.

We have the isomorphism

\Ext^{2}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{0}(S^{[2]},\underline{Ext}^{2}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}))=\\
=H^{0}(S_{p},b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}).

because
\underline{Ext}^{p}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})=0
for
p\in\{0,1\}
(
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})
is supported on
S_{p}
which has codimension
2
in
S^{[2]}
). Via the above identification, the exact sequence in (
4.5.16
) is identified with the exact sequence in (
4.4.23
) with
p=1
.

Similarly to the exact sequence in (
4.3.22
) we have the exact sequence

(4.5.17)

0\longrightarrow j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\overset{h}{\longrightarrow}\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes i_{p,*}{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}\longrightarrow 0,

where
h
is defined via the projection of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)=\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\oplus\bigwedge^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)
onto the first direct summand.
We have the isomorphism

(4.5.18)

\Psi\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H^{0}(S_{p},b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})

given by the inverse of the composition

H^{0}(S_{p},b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{0}(S_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\\
\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes H^{0}(S,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1})={\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}}={\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p),

where the first isomorphism is obtained from the long exact sequence associated to (
4.5.17
) and the isomorphism

\omega_{S_{p}}\cong{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
.

Proposition 4.42
.

Let
p\in S
and
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be an
a
-dimensional subspace.
Let
q\colon(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow U
be a non zero quotient of
(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}
, and let
{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}
be the sheaf fitting into the exact sequence

(4.5.19)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes U\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}\overset{\lambda_{U}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}\longrightarrow 0

with extension class in
(see Proposition
4.29
)

(4.5.20)

\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})\otimes U=(\Ann W_{a})\otimes U^{\vee}

given by the transpose
q^{t}\colon U^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\Ann W_{a}
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}
is locally free.

Proof.

Since
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}
is locally free, and

{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}
is locally free away from
S_{p}
,
{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}
is locally free away from
S_{p}
. We must show that

{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}
is locally free at each point
x\in S_{p}
.
On a sufficiently small affine open subset
A\subset S^{[2]}
containing
x
we have

(4.5.21)

{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}\cong{\mathscr{I}}_{S_{p}}\otimes{\mathscr{U}}\oplus{\mathscr{V}}

where

{\mathscr{U}},{\mathscr{V}}
are locally free sheaves on
A
, and the restrictions of
{\mathscr{U}}
and
b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
to
A\cap S_{p}
are naturally identified.
Because of the isomorphism in (
4.5.21
) we may apply Lemma
4.40
. Let

\varphi_{U}\in H^{0}(S_{p},b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes U)

be the image of the extension class of (
4.5.19
) via the map
\epsilon
in (
4.5.16
) (note that
\det{\mathscr{N}}_{S_{p}/S^{[2]}}\cong\omega_{S_{p}}
). Then
\varphi_{U}
gives a map

b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\longrightarrow j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes U
:
by Lemma
4.40
it suffices to prove that it
is injective (as map of vector bundles) at every point of
A\cap S_{p}
. Since
U
is any non zero quotient of
(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}
we need to show that for any non zero
v\in{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)
the map

(4.5.22)

\Psi(v)\colon b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\longrightarrow j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}

is an injection of vector bundles.

First we prove that the map in (
4.5.22
) is an injection of vector bundles away from
R_{p}
. Over
S_{p}\setminus R_{p}
we have

j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1|(S_{p}\setminus R_{p})}
, and the canonical bundle

\omega_{S_{p}}
is trivial. Hence the map in (
4.5.22
) is given by

(4.5.23)

\begin{matrix}b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1|(S_{p}\setminus R_{p})}&\longrightarrow&{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1|(S_{p}\setminus R_{p})}\\
s&\mapsto&v\otimes s\end{matrix}

This is an injection of vector bundles.

Lastly we prove that the map in (
4.5.22
) is an injection of vector bundles on
R_{p}
. Tensoring the exact sequence in (
4.5.17
) with

\omega_{S_{p}}\cong{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
we get the exact sequence

0\to j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})\to{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})\to\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes i_{p,*}{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}(-1)\to 0.

Restricting to
R_{p}
one gets the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}\longrightarrow j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\omega_{S_{p}}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}\longrightarrow\bigwedge^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}(-1)\longrightarrow 0.

It follows that the restriction
\Psi(v)_{\mid R_{p}}
factors through a morphism
\overline{\Psi(v)}_{|R_{p}}
to

\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}
. We claim that

(4.5.24)

\begin{matrix}b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1|R_{p}}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}&\xrightarrow{\overline{\Psi(v)}_{|R_{p}}}&\Sym^{2}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{R_{p}}\\
s&\mapsto&v\cdot s\end{matrix}

Before giving the proof of this we note that it shows that the map in (
4.5.22
) is an injection of vector bundles on
R_{p}
also at points of
R_{p}
. Let us prove that (
4.5.24
) holds.
Let
\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\colon b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
be the morphism obtained by composing
\Psi(v)
with the inclusion
j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
. Let
s
be a local section of
b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
, and let
z
be a local generator of the ideal of
R_{p}
in
S_{p}
. Then

(4.5.25)

\widetilde{\Psi}(v)(s)=v\otimes s=z(v\otimes s+s\otimes v)z^{-1}/2+z(v\otimes s-s\otimes v)z^{-1}/2.

Viewing
j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
as a subsheaf of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\otimes b^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
, we have the following:

\frac{1}{2}(v\otimes s-s\otimes v)z^{-1}
is a local section of
j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
, and since in (
4.5.25
) it is multiplied by
z

\widetilde{\Psi}(v)(s)_{|R_{p}}=\left(z(v\otimes s+s\otimes v)z^{-1}/2\right)_{\mid R_{p}}.

Now
z(v\otimes s+s\otimes v)z^{-1}/2
is a non zero local section of
j^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{S_{p}}(R_{p})
along
R_{p}
, in fact its restriction to a local section of
R_{p}
is equal to
v\cdot s
in the notation of (
4.5.24
).
∎

Letting
q=\Id_{(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}}
one gets the following result.

Corollary 4.43
.

Let
p\in S
and
W_{a}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
be an
a
-dimensional subspace.
Then
{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
is locally free.

Proposition 4.44
.

Let hypotheses be as in Proposition
4.39
. Let
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
be a non zero subsheaf such that

\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{S}})=\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a}))
. Then one of the following holds.

(a)

There exists a subspace
T^{-}\subset(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}
such that
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-}
(this makes sense by (
4.5.13
)) and
({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-})/{\mathscr{S}}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from

\Delta
.

(b)

There exists a subspace
T^{+}\subset W_{a}
such that
\theta(\lambda({\mathscr{S}}))\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}
and
(\theta\circ\lambda)^{-1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+})/{\mathscr{S}}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from

\Delta
.

Proof.

We adopt the notation of the proof of Proposition
4.39
.
We claim that the following hold:

(1)

There exists a non zero subspace
T^{-}\subset(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}
such that

{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-}
and

({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes T^{-})/{\mathscr{A}}_{1}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
.

(2)

There exists a subspace
T^{+}\subset W_{a}
such that

\theta({\mathscr{A}}_{2})\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}
and

({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+})/\theta({\mathscr{A}}_{2})
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
.

The key point is that the inequality in (
4.5.9
) is an equality. The rest of the argument is the same as that which was given to prove Items (1), (2) and (3) in the proof of Proposition
4.37
.

It remains to prove that if
T^{+}\not=0
then
(\theta\circ\lambda)^{-1}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+})/{\mathscr{S}}
is torsion with support of codimension at least
2
away from
\Delta
. By Item (1) above it suffices to show that
T^{-}=(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}
. Suppose the contrary. Let
U\coloneq(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}/T^{-}
and let
(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow U
be the quotient map. Note that
U
is non zero because by
hypothesis
T^{+}\subsetneq W_{a}
. The morphism

\lambda\colon{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})\to{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}
defines an inclusion
\overline{\iota}\colon{\mathscr{A}}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}
which lifts to an inclusion

\iota\colon{\mathscr{A}}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{U}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{A}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\iota}}

Let
T^{+}\subset W_{a}
be as in Item (2) above.
Away from
\Delta
the inclusion
{\mathscr{A}}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}
is an isomorphism in codimension
1
. By Proposition
4.42
the sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}
is locally free and hence by Hartog’s Theorem
away from
\Delta
there exists an inclusion

\bm{\iota}\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}(p,W_{a})_{U}
which restricts to
\iota
on
{\mathscr{A}}_{2}
. This implies that

away from
\Delta
we have

(4.5.26)

\Phi_{W_{a}}^{+}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+})=0.

The map
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes T^{+}\to{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},p)
given by the restriction of
\Phi_{W_{a}}^{+}
is surjective by
Proposition
4.13
. Since the support of
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},p)
is
S_{p}
and
S_{p}
is not contained in
\Delta
(in fact
\Delta\cap S_{p}=R_{p}
), the equality in (
4.5.26
) is absurd. This proves that
T^{-}=(\Ann W_{a})^{\vee}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.38
.

It is analogous to the proof of Proposition
4.33
.
Since the BBF squares of
P=\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
and
h_{S^{[2]}}
are positive,
P
(
h_{S^{[2]}}
) slope (semi)stability is the same as

P
(
h_{S^{[2]}}
) HK slope (semi)stability.
Suppose that
{\mathscr{B}}={\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})
is not
h_{S^{[2]}}
HK slope stable. Since
{\mathscr{B}}
is modular (see Claim
4.32
),

P
HK slope semistable
(by Proposition
4.39
), and
h_{S^{[2]}}
is
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w}(D,a))
-suitable for
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
, Proposition
2.8
gives that there exists a subsheaf
{\mathscr{S}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}
, with
0<r({\mathscr{S}})<r({\mathscr{B}})
, such that

(4.5.27)

\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{S}})=\mu_{P}^{HK}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})),\qquad\mu_{h_{S^{[2]}}}^{HK}({\mathscr{S}})\geq\mu_{h_{S^{[2]}}}^{HK}({\mathscr{B}}(p,W_{a})).

By the equality in (
4.5.5
) one of Items (a), (b), of Proposition
4.44
holds.
In each case the inequality in (
4.5.27
) does not hold.
∎

5.
Extension of the map
{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}\dashrightarrow M_{{\bf w}(D,a)}
.

5.1.
Main result

Throughout the section we assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
, and we suppose that
S,h_{S},h_{S^{[2]}}
are as in
the statement of Proposition
4.2
.
We often set

{\mathscr{M}}={\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
,
{\bf w}={\bf w}(D,a)
,
M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}=M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
(see Corollary
4.3
for the definition of the latter),
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}={\mathscr{D}}^{k}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
,
{\mathscr{D}}={\mathscr{D}}^{1}
.
We have defined (see Corollary
4.3
) the regular and birational map

(5.1.1)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}&\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}&M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}\\
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]&\mapsto&[{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})]\end{matrix}

If
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}
the sheaf
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is unstable (see Propositions
4.9
and
4.11
), and in addition not locally free if

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}
. Nonetheless the map
\psi
extends to a regular map
\overline{\psi}
which is an isomorphism.
In the present section we prove this result, and we show that
M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
is a connected (projective!) component of

M_{{\bf w}}=M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
.

In order to describe the restriction of
\overline{\psi}
to
{\mathscr{D}}
we recall a few results. Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1})
where
a>k\geq 1
. By Proposition
3.10

{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
fits into the exact sequence

(5.1.2)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0,

where
{\mathscr{H}}
is an
h_{S}
slope stable vector with

[{\mathscr{H}}]\in({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a-k)}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{v(a-k)}(S,h_{S}))
. Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},-)
to the exact sequence in (
5.1.2
) we get the inclusion (see Remark
3.12
)
j_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\colon\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
. Let

(5.1.3)

J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\coloneq j_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee})\subset\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}).

Next let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}
. Then

{\mathscr{D}}^{a}={\mathscr{B}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})

(see Proposition
3.15
), and by
Proposition
3.3

{\mathscr{E}}_{2}^{\vee}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{a}
where
V_{a}
is an
a
-dimensional (complex) vector space. Thus we have a unique exact sequence

(5.1.4)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{a}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}\longrightarrow 0.

The map
f
defines a linear map

(5.1.5)

f(p)\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)\to V_{a}^{\vee}

By
Lemma
3.5

f(p)
is surjective, and hence
\im(f(p)^{t})\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
has dimension
a
. Thus

(5.1.6)

\im(f(p)^{t})\in\mathrm{Gr}(a,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}).

Theorem 5.1
.

Assume Hypothesis-Definition
4.1
.
Let
{\bf w}\coloneq{\bf w}(D,a)
, and let
h_{S^{[2]}}
be a polarization of
S^{[2]}
which is
{\mathsf{a}}({\bf w})
-suitable for
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
.

(a)

The birational map in (
5.1.1
) extends to a regular map

(5.1.7)

{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\overset{\overline{\psi}}{\longrightarrow}M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}.

(b)

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}(S,h_{S})\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}(S,h_{S})
where
a\geq k\geq 1
, and let

[{\mathscr{F}}]=\overline{\psi}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])
. If
a>k\geq 1
then
{\mathscr{F}}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})
where
{\mathscr{H}}

is as
in (
5.1.2
), and

J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
is as in (
5.1.3
). If
k=a
then
{\mathscr{F}}\cong{\mathscr{B}}(p,\im(f(p)^{t}))
where
p
,
\im(f(p)^{t})
are as
in (
5.1.4
) and (
5.1.6
)
respectively.

(c)

The map
\overline{\psi}
is bijective.

Theorem
5.1
is proved in Subsection
5.6
. Let

\widetilde{M}_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}\to M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
be the normalization map.
Below is a consequence of Theorem
5.1
.

Corollary 5.2
.

The map
\overline{\psi}
in (
5.1.7
) lifts to an isomorphism

(5.1.8)

\widetilde{\psi}\colon{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{M}_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}.

Remark 5.3
.

Computations suggest that
M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
is smooth only if
a=1
.

5.2.
Road map

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
for
1\leq k\leq a
, let

f\colon(T,0)\to({\mathscr{M}},[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])
be a map of pointed varieties, where
T
is a smooth curve, and let
f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
be the pull-back of the
{\mathscr{M}}
-flat) sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}
with fiber
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})
on
[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}
(such a sheaf always exists locally
{\mathscr{M}}
in the classical topology).
The desemitabilizing sequence for
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})
given in (
4.3
) dictates an elementary modification
\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}}
of
f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
which is isomorphic to
f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
away from the central fiber
S^{[2]}\times\{0\}
.
The main result of Subsection
5.3
is the following: if the image of the differential
df(0)
does not belong to the tangent cone to
{\mathscr{D}}
at
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]
then the restriction of
\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}}
to
S^{[2]}\times\{0\}
is slope stable, and its isomorphism class depends on
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]
but
not
on
f
.

As was explained in Subsection
1.3
, in order to prove Theorem
5.1
one needs to consider the blow-up
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
of
{\mathscr{M}}
defined in Subsection
3.4
and perform elementary modifications. The picture one gets is iterative. In order to carry it out we need to discuss certain extensions of sheaves and relate them to spaces of complete collineations: this is the subject of Subsection
5.4
.

In Subsection
5.5
we prove (Proposition
5.23
) that
\psi
extends to a regular map
\widehat{\psi}\colon\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}\to M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
and we identify the values of
\widehat{\psi}
on points of the exceptional set
\widehat{E}^{a}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{1}
. The proof is a monstruous semistable reduction. Theorem
5.1
follows easily from Proposition
5.23
.

5.3.
First step of semistable reduction along a curve

5.3.1.
Preliminaries: elementary modifications and extension classes

We collect some results that are useful in proving Items (a) and (b) of Theorem
5.1
. Let
X,T
be varieties, and
D\subset T
be an effective divisor (a closed subscheme with locally principal ideal sheaf).
Let
\iota\colon X\times D\hookrightarrow X\times T
be the inclusion morphism. Assume that
{\mathscr{F}}
is a sheaf on
X\times T
, and that we are given an exact sequence of sheaves on
X\times D
:

(5.3.1)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}\overset{\lambda}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{F}}_{|X\times D}\overset{\phi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow 0.

Then
\phi
defines a morphism
\widetilde{\phi}\colon{\mathscr{F}}\to\iota_{*}{\mathscr{B}}
. Let
{\mathscr{H}}\coloneq\ker\widetilde{\phi}
. Hence we have the following exact sequence of sheaves on
X\times T
:

(5.3.2)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{F}}\overset{\widetilde{\phi}}{\longrightarrow}\iota_{*}{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow 0.

Lemma 5.4
.

Keep notation as above. Assume that
{\mathscr{F}}
is
T
-flat and that
{\mathscr{B}}
is locally free.
Then
{\mathscr{H}}
is
T
-flat.

Proof.

We must show that
Tor_{1}^{{\mathscr{O}}_{T}}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{S}})=0

for every sheaf of
{\mathscr{O}}_{T}
-modules
{\mathscr{S}}
. By the exact sequence in (
5.3.2
) and
T
-flatness of
{\mathscr{F}}
it suffices to show that

Tor_{2}^{{\mathscr{O}}_{T}}(\iota_{*}{\mathscr{B}},{\mathscr{S}})=0
for every
{\mathscr{S}}
as above.
Since
{\mathscr{B}}
is locally free it suffices to prove that
Tor_{2}^{{\mathscr{O}}_{T}}(\iota_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X\times D},{\mathscr{S}})=0
. The latter holds because, since
D
is Cartier,

\iota_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{X\times D}
has a two-step locally-free resolution.
∎

Now assume that
T
is a smooth curve and
D=\{0\}\subset T
. Let
X_{0}\coloneq X\times\{0\}
,

{\mathscr{F}}_{0}\coloneq{\mathscr{F}}_{|X_{0}}
,
{\mathscr{H}}_{0}\coloneq{\mathscr{H}}_{|X_{0}}
.
Tensoring the exact sequence in (
5.3.2
) with
{\mathscr{O}}_{X_{0}}
we get the exact sequence

(5.3.3)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}_{0}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}\longrightarrow 0.

One describes the extension class of (
5.3.3
) as follows. Let

(5.3.4)

\Theta_{T}(0)\overset{\kappa}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{0},{\mathscr{F}}_{0})

be the Kodaira-Spencer map at
0
, and let

(5.3.5)

\begin{matrix}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{0},{\mathscr{F}}_{0})&\overset{\mu}{\longrightarrow}&\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{A}}_{0},{\mathscr{B}}_{0})\\
e&\mapsto&\lambda\cup e\cup\phi\end{matrix}

where
\lambda,\phi
are the maps in (
5.3.1
).

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume that
T
is a smooth curve and
D=\{0\}\subset T
. The extension class
of (
5.3.3
) is (up to
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
) equal to
\mu\circ\kappa(v)
where
v
is a generator of

\Theta_{T}(0)
.

Proof.

Let
Y\coloneq\Spec{\mathbb{C}}[t]/(t^{2})
. The non zero
v\in\Theta_{T}(0)
corresponds to an embedding
j\colon Y\hookrightarrow T
mapping the closed point to
0
, and

\kappa(v)
is the extension class of the exact sequence

(5.3.6)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{0}\overset{\cdot\overline{t}}{\longrightarrow}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{0}\longrightarrow 0

(ve view it as a sequence of sheaves on
X_{0}
).
Let
\alpha
be the morphism in (
5.3.2
).
The map
j^{*}(\alpha)\colon j^{*}{\mathscr{H}}\to j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}
has image equal to

\lambda({\mathscr{A}})
, the subsheaf
\overline{t}{\mathscr{H}}\subset j^{*}{\mathscr{H}}
is mapped to
\overline{t}\lambda({\mathscr{A}})
. It follows that we have an isomorphism between the extension in (
5.3.3
) with the extension obtained from (
5.3.6
) after pull-back via

\lambda\colon{\mathscr{A}}\to{\mathscr{F}}_{0}
and push-out via
\phi\colon{\mathscr{F}}_{0}\to{\mathscr{B}}
. This proves the lemma.
∎

5.3.2.
First step of semistable reduction along a curve, I

Let
a>k\geq 1
and let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1})
. Let
(T,0)
be a smooth pointed curve and let

(5.3.7)

f\colon(T,0)\to({\mathscr{M}},[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])

be a (regular) map of pointed varieties. We assume that
f^{-1}({\mathscr{D}})=\{0\}
set-theoretically. Let
f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
be the sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times T
given by
(\Id_{S^{[2]}},f)^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
. Then
f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
is
T
-flat because
{\mathbb{G}}
is
{\mathscr{M}}
-flat. For
t\in T
let
(f^{*}{\mathbb{G}})_{t}
be the restriction of

f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
to
S^{[2]}\times\{t\}
: it is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}})
where
[{\mathscr{F}}]=f(t)
. If
t\not=0
then
(f^{*}{\mathbb{G}})_{t}
is
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable by Proposition
4.2
.
By (
4.3.10
) we have an exact sequence

(5.3.8)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\xrightarrow{\Psi_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{-}}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0.

The above exact sequence is slope destabilizing (for any polarization) by Proposition
4.9
.
Moreover (see (
4.3.8
)) we have the exact sequence

(5.3.9)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0,

where
{\mathscr{H}}
is the vector bundle on
S
appearing in the exact sequence in (
5.1.2
). Let
J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
be given by (
5.1.3
).
Then by definition of
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}

(5.3.10)

{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}.

Hence Proposition
4.23
gives the next result.

Claim 5.6
.

The extension class of (
5.3.9
) belongs to

\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+},{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}))\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\overset{\BKR}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

Let
\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}}
be the elementary modification of
f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}
determined by the exact sequence in (
5.3.8
), i.e. the sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times T
fitting into the exact sequence

(5.3.11)

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}}\longrightarrow f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}\overset{\widetilde{\Psi}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{-}}{\longrightarrow}\iota_{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0.

For
t\in T
let
(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{t}\coloneq\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}}_{|S^{[2]}\times\{t\}}
. Then
(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{t}\cong(f^{*}{\mathbb{G}})_{t}
if
t\not=0
. On the other hand

(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{0}
fits into the exact sequence (see Subsubsection
5.3.1
and Claim
5.6
)

(5.3.12)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{0}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}\longrightarrow 0.

Here we determine the extension class of the above exact sequence. First we note that by Proposition
4.17
we have the BKR isomorphism

(5.3.13)

\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\overset{\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}).

Let
\overline{df}_{0}
be the composition of the linear maps

(5.3.14)

\Theta_{T}(0)\overset{df_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\Theta_{{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]),

where
p
is the projection. By Remark
3.16
we have the identification

(5.3.15)

{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])=\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
to the exact sequence in (
5.1.2
) we get the inclusion

(5.3.16)

\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

Lemma 5.7
.

The inclusion in (
5.3.16
) has image equal to
\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
.

Proof.

Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
to the exact sequence in (
5.1.2
) we get the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\xrightarrow{\partial_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\longrightarrow 0.

Let
e\in\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
be the extension class of (
5.1.2
). Then
e=\sum_{i=1}^{k}j_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v_{i})\otimes v_{i}
where
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
is a basis of

\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
.
If
s\in\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{H}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
then
\partial_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(s)=s\cup e=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(s\cup j_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v_{i}))\otimes v_{i}
. The result follows because, since

\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})={\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
, the cup product

(s\cup j_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v_{i}))
is identified with Serre duality.
∎

The identification in (
5.3.15
) together with Lemma
5.7
gives the isomomorphism

(5.3.17)

\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\colon{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}.

Composing
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
and the isomorphism in (
5.3.13
)
we get the isomorphism

(5.3.18)

{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\overset{\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\circ\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\xrightarrow{\ \ \ \sim\ \ \ }}\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}).

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
v
be a generator of
\im\overline{df}_{0}
(see (
5.3.14
)). The extension class of the exact sequence in (
5.3.12
) is equal to
\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\circ\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathscr{G}}\coloneq{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
and
V_{k}\coloneq\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}
. By the BKR equivalence we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\BKR}
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\scriptstyle{\BKR}
\textstyle{V_{k}\otimes\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{G}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\zeta}\hskip 16.38895pt\ }
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\overline{\xi}}
\textstyle{V_{k}\otimes\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}

Here
\zeta
,
\xi
are obtained by applying the functors
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},-)
,
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k})
to the exact sequence in (
5.1.2
) respectively, and the domain of
\xi
is as indicated by Lemma
5.7
.
Moreover the maps
\overline{\zeta}=\BKR(\zeta)
,
\overline{\xi}=\BKR(\xi)
are obtained by applying the functors

\Hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},-)
,
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{k})
to the exact sequence in (
5.3.8
) respectively, given the
isomorphisms
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}
and
\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
in (
5.3.13
).

Let
\widetilde{v}\in\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
be such that
v=p(\widetilde{v})
, where
p
is the projection to the normal space (see (
5.3.14
)). By Lemma
5.5
the extension class of (
5.3.12
) is given (up to
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
)
by
\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(\overline{\xi}\circ\overline{\zeta}(\BKR(\widetilde{v})))
.
On the other hand
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)=\xi\circ\zeta(\widetilde{v})
. By commutativity of the diagram,
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)=\overline{\xi}\circ\overline{\zeta}(\BKR(\widetilde{v}))
.
∎

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
v
generate
\im\overline{df(0)}
. If
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)\colon\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\to\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}

is an isomorphism then
(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{0}
is isomorphic to the vector bundle
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})
, and it is
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable.

Proof.

By Definition
4.18
the extension class of the exact sequence

(5.3.19)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes(\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})\overset{\lambda}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}\longrightarrow 0

is given by the identity
\Id\colon\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\to\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
.
By Proposition
5.8
the extension class of the exact sequence in (
5.3.12
) is equal to

\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\circ\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)
. Since
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)\colon\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\to\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
is an isomorphism, the two extensions are isomorphic. More precisely: the transpose

\alpha^{t}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)\colon\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{\vee}\to\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}
is an isomorphism defining an isomorphism

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes(\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}
which extends to an isomorphism between the exact sequence in (
5.3.19
) and the exact sequence in (
5.3.12
).
∎

5.3.3.
First step of semistable reduction along a curve, II

This is the analogue of Subsusbsection
5.3.2
with
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
replaced by
{\mathscr{D}}^{a}
. We adopt notation introduced in that subsusbsection, and we avoid repeating statements that carry over without modifications.
Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}
, and let
f\colon(T,0)\to({\mathscr{M}},[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])
be a (regular) map of pointed varieties, with
T
a smooth curve. We assume that that (set-theoretically)

f^{-1}({\mathscr{D}})=\{0\}
. By (
4.3.17
) we have an exact sequence

(5.3.20)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\overset{\Psi^{-}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{a}\longrightarrow 0.

The above exact sequence is slope destabilizing (for any polarization) by Proposition
4.11
.
We have the exact sequence (see (
4.3.16
))

(5.3.21)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes V_{a}\overset{\Phi^{+}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}_{p})\longrightarrow 0.

Let
p\in S
be the unique singular point of
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
. Recall that
f(p)^{t}\colon V_{a}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}(p)^{\vee}
is injective. Abusing notation we denote

\im f(p)^{t}
by
V_{a}
. Proposition
4.31
gives an isomorphism

(5.3.22)

{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})^{+}.

Let
\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}}
be the sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times T
fitting into the exact sequence

(5.3.23)

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}}\longrightarrow f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}\overset{\widetilde{\Psi}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{-}}{\longrightarrow}\iota_{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{a}\longrightarrow 0.

Then
(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{t}\cong(f^{*}{\mathbb{G}})_{t}
if
t\not=0
, and

(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{0}
fits into the exact sequence (see Subsubsection
5.3.1
and (
5.3.22
))

(5.3.24)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{a}\longrightarrow(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{0}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})^{+}\longrightarrow 0.

We describe the extension class of the above exact sequence. We have (see Proposition
4.29
) the BKR isomorphism

(5.3.25)

\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\colon V_{a}\otimes\Ann V_{a}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})^{+},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{a}).

Let
\overline{df}_{0}
be the composition of the linear maps

(5.3.26)

\Theta_{T}(0)\overset{df_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\Theta_{{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{k}/{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]),

where
p
is the projection. By Remark
3.16
we have the isomorphism

(5.3.27)

{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{a}/{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}).

Applying the functor
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
to (
5.1.4
) we get the isomorphism

(5.3.28)

{\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}}\otimes V_{a}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}),

and the exact sequence

(5.3.29)

0\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\overset{\partial}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\overset{f(p)}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\otimes V_{a}^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0.

In the above exact sequence
f(p)
is the map in (
5.1.5
), and one identifies it with the map
\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\longrightarrow\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
via Serre duality. It follows that the coboundary map in (
5.3.29
) defines an isomorphism

(5.3.30)

\begin{matrix}\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})&\xrightarrow{\sim}&\Ann V_{a}\subset\Ext^{2}_{S}({\mathbb{C}}_{p},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\\
x&\mapsto&\partial(x)\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\end{matrix}

Hence the isomorphism in (
5.3.27
) reads

(5.3.31)

\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\colon{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{a}/{\mathscr{M}}}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{a}\otimes\Ann V_{a}.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
v
be a generator of
\im\overline{df}_{0}
(see (
5.3.14
)). The extension class of the exact sequence in (
5.3.24
) is equal to
\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\circ\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathscr{G}}\coloneq{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})
. The BKR equivalence gives the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\BKR}
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{a})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\scriptstyle{\BKR}
\textstyle{V_{a}\otimes\Ann V_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{G}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\zeta}\hskip 16.38895pt\ }
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{a})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\overline{\xi}}
\textstyle{V_{k}\otimes\Ann V_{a}}

Here
\zeta
,
\xi
are obtained by applying the functors
\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},-)
,
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes V_{k})
to the exact sequence in (
5.1.4
) respectively, and the domain of
\xi
is as indicated because of the isomorphism in (
5.3.30
).
Moreover the maps
\overline{\zeta}=\BKR(\zeta)
,
\overline{\xi}=\BKR(\xi)
are obtained by applying the functors

\Hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{G}},-)
,
\Hom_{S}(-,{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V_{k})
to the exact sequence in (
5.3.20
) respectively, given the
isomorphisms
{\mathscr{A}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})^{+}\cong{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})^{+}
(see (
5.3.22
)) and
\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
(see (
5.3.25
)).

Let
\widetilde{v}\in\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
be such that
v=p(\widetilde{v})
, where
p
is the projection to the normal space (see (
5.3.26
)). By Lemma
5.5
the extension class of (
5.3.24
) is given (up to
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
)
by
\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(\overline{\xi}\circ\overline{\zeta}(\BKR(\widetilde{v})))
.
On the other hand
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)=\xi\circ\zeta(\widetilde{v})
. By commutativity of the diagram,
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)=\overline{\xi}\circ\overline{\zeta}(\BKR(\widetilde{v}))
.
∎

Arguing as in the proof of Corollary
5.9
one proves the following result (recall that
V_{a}
is naturally isomorphic to
\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}
).

Corollary 5.11
.

Let
v
generate
\im\overline{df(0)}
. If
\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(v)\colon\Hom({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})\to\Ann V_{a}
is an isomorphism then
(\widetilde{f^{*}{\mathbb{G}}})_{0}
is isomorphic to the vector bundle
{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})
, and it is
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable.

Remark 5.12
.

The results in Corollaries
5.9
,
5.11

are quite close to a proof Items (a) and (b) of Theorem
5.1
. This is clear if one looks at the arguments in Subsection
5.6
.

5.4.
Extensions and complete collineations

5.4.1.
A family of extensions and its parameter space

Throughout the present subsubsection
X
is a projective variety, and
{\mathscr{F}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}}_{3}
are sheaves on
X
such that

(5.4.1)

\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})=\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})=0,\quad\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})={\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}}.

Definition 5.13
.

Given a subspace
U\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
, let

(5.4.2)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes U^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{3}\longrightarrow 0

be the exact sequence with extension class (in
U^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
) given by the inclusion map

U\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
.

Applying the functor

\Hom_{X}(-,{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
to the exact sequence in (
5.4.2
)
we get the exact sequence

(5.4.3)

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}}\otimes U\overset{\partial_{U}}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})\longrightarrow 0.

Since
\partial_{U}
is the inclusion map
U\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
, we get an isomorphism

(5.4.4)

\eta_{U}\colon\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}).

Let
W
be a vector space such that

(5.4.5)

k\coloneq\dim W\leq\dim\left(\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U\right).

Definition 5.14
.

If
\varphi\colon W\to\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U
is a linear map, let

(5.4.6)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes W^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U}\longrightarrow 0

be the exact sequence with extension class (in
W^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
) given by
\eta_{U}\circ\varphi
.

Since the isomorphism class of
{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}
depends only on the homothety class
[\varphi]
of

\varphi
we also denote
{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}
by
{\mathscr{S}}_{[\varphi]}
.
Fix
U,W
as above, and let

(5.4.7)

L\coloneq(U,W),\qquad{\mathbf{P}}_{L}\coloneq{\mathbb{P}}(\Hom(W,\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U)).

Let
{\mathscr{F}}_{1},{\mathscr{F}}_{3}
and
U,W
be as above. The Künneth decomposition for sheaves on
X\times{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
defines an isomorphism (note that
\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})=0
)

(5.4.8)

\Ext^{1}({\mathscr{S}}_{U}\boxtimes{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(-1),({\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes W^{\vee})\boxtimes{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}})\cong\\
\cong\Hom_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}({\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(-1),W^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}).

Definition 5.15
.

Let
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}
be the sheaf on
X\times{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
fitting into the exact sequence

(5.4.9)

0\longrightarrow({\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes W^{\vee})\boxtimes{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U}\boxtimes{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(-1)\longrightarrow 0

with extension class the tautological section of the right-hand side of (
5.4.8
).

If
[\varphi]\in{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
then

(5.4.10)

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}|X\times[\varphi]}\cong{\mathscr{S}}_{[\varphi]}.

Claim 5.16
.

Let
[\varphi]\in{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
, and let
U({\varphi})\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
be the subspace containing
U
such that
U(\varphi)/U=\im\varphi
.
There is a well-defined split exact sequence

(5.4.11)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

Applying the functor
\Hom_{X}(-,{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee})
to the exact sequence in (
5.4.6
) we get the exact sequence

\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}\longrightarrow\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee})\longrightarrow\\
\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}}\otimes W\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}\overset{\partial}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}

The coboundary
\partial
is given by composition with

\varphi
, and hence vanishes. It follows that there is a surjection
{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}
, and since
\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})=0
it is unique. The kernel of the above surjection is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}
, and the exact sequence in (
5.4.11
) is split.
∎

Let
{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{L}\subset{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
be the determinantal subscheme defined (set theoretically) by

{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{L}\coloneq\{[\varphi]\in{\mathbf{P}}_{L}\mid\dim\ker\varphi\geq s\}.

We have the chain of closed subsets

(5.4.12)

\emptyset={\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{L}\subsetneq{\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{L}\subsetneq{\mathbf{D}}^{k-2}_{L}\subsetneq\ldots\subsetneq{\mathbf{D}}^{1}_{L}\subsetneq{\mathbf{D}}^{0}_{L}={\mathbf{P}}_{L}.

Remark 5.17
.

Let
[\varphi]\in({\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{L}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}^{s+1}_{L})
. We have an isomorphism

(5.4.13)

w_{\varphi}\colon{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{L}/{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}([\varphi])\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Hom(\ker\varphi,\coker\varphi),

defined as follows.
Let

\widehat{{\mathbf{D}}}^{s}_{L}\subset\Hom(W,\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U)\setminus\{0\}
be the punctured cone over
{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{L}
, i.e. the determinantal variety parametrizing non zero maps
W\to\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U
with kernel of dimension at least
s
. The differential at
\varphi
of the projection map
\Hom(W,\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U)\setminus\{0\}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
induces an isomorphism

(5.4.14)

{\mathscr{N}}_{\widehat{{\mathbf{D}}}^{s}_{L}/\Hom(W,\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U)}(\varphi)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{L}/{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}([\varphi]).

A straightfoward computation gives that

\Theta_{\widehat{{\mathbf{D}}}^{s}_{L}}(\varphi)=\{\alpha\in\Hom(W,\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U)\mid\alpha(\ker\varphi)\subset\im\varphi\}.

Hence, letting
\pi\colon\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U\longrightarrow\coker\alpha
be the quotient map, we have a well-defined isomorphism

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{N}}_{\widehat{{\mathbf{D}}}^{s}_{W}/W^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})}(\varphi)&\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}&\Hom(\ker\varphi,\coker\varphi)\\
\overline{\alpha}&\mapsto&\pi\circ\left(\alpha_{|\ker\varphi}\right)\end{matrix}

This, in conjunction with (
5.4.14
), defines the isomorphism in (
5.4.13
).

5.4.2.
From
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
to a space of complete collineations

We replace
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
by a space of complete collineations (see
[
Vai84
]
) and we modify
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}
via elementary modifications dictated by the exact sequences in (
5.4.11
). The end result is a family of sheaves on
X
, each of which is isomorphic to

{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}
where
\varphi
is an injection

W\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U
. We start by introducing the blow-ups which define the space of complete collineations: they are analogous to those appearing in Definition
3.18
.

Definition 5.18
.

Let
varieties
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(k-1),\ldots,{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(0)
, birational maps

{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(0)\xrightarrow{g^{L}_{0}}{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(1)\xrightarrow{g^{L}_{1}}\ldots{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(k-1)\xrightarrow{g^{L}_{k-1}}{\mathbf{P}}_{L},

and closed subsets
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j)^{s},{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{t}\subset{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)
for
j\in\{k-1,\ldots,0\}
,
s>j\geq t\geq 0

be as follows.

(1)

{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(k-1)={\mathbf{P}}_{L}
,
g_{k-1}\colon{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(k-1)\to{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
is the identity,
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(k-1)^{s}=\emptyset
and

{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(k-1)^{t}={\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{t}
.

(2)

g^{L}_{j-1}
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{s},{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j-1)^{t}
are defined by iteration starting from
j=k-1
by the following prescriptions:

(2a)

g^{L}_{j-1}\colon{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j-1)\to{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)
is the blow up of
{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{j}

(2b)

If
s>j
then
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{s}
is the strict transform of
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j)^{s}
, while

{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}
is the exceptional divisor of
g^{L}_{j-1}
.

(2c)

If
j-1\geq t\geq 0
then

{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j-1)^{t}
is the strict transform of
{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{t}
.

We have the chain of closed subsets

(5.4.15)

\emptyset\subsetneq{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{j}\subsetneq\ldots\subsetneq{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{1}\subsetneq{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{0}={\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j).

analogous to that in (
5.4.12
). For
k-1\geq s>j
we let

(5.4.16)

{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j)^{\geq s}\coloneq{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j)^{k-1}\cup{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j)^{k-2}\cup\ldots{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j)^{s}

Remark 5.19
.

Let
s\in\{k-1,\ldots,1\}
.
The restriction of
g^{L}_{k-1}\circ g^{L}_{k-2}\circ\ldots\circ g^{L}_{s-1}
to

{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{\geq s+1}

maps to
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1}
, and is identified with the blow up of
{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1}
. Of course the exceptional divisor is

{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{\geq s+1}
. Let
[\varphi]\in\left({\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1}\right)
: the isomorphism
in (
5.4.13
) defines an isomorphism

(5.4.17)

(g^{L}_{k-1}\circ g^{L}_{k-2}\circ\ldots\circ g^{L}_{s-1})^{-1}([\varphi])\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{P}}(\Hom(\ker\varphi,\coker\varphi)).

Let
k-1\geq s\geq j\geq 1
.
Then
g^{L}_{k-1}\circ g^{L}_{k-2}\circ\ldots\circ g^{L}_{j-1}
maps

{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s+1}
to
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{\geq s+1}
. Let

(5.4.18)

h_{j-1,\geq s}^{L}\colon({\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s+1})\longrightarrow({\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1})

be the composition

{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s+1}\longrightarrow({\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(s-1)^{\geq s+1})\longrightarrow({\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1}).

Key Remark 5.20
.

Let
k-1\geq s\geq j\geq 1
, and let
[\varphi]\in\left({\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1}\right)
. Let
U(\varphi)\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
be as in Claim
5.16
. Let

(5.4.19)

W(\varphi)\coloneq\ker\varphi,\qquad L(\varphi)\coloneq(U(\varphi),W(\varphi)).

We have a tautological identification
{\mathbb{P}}(\Hom(\ker\varphi,\coker\varphi))={\mathbf{P}}_{L(\varphi)}
, and hence the isomorphism in (
5.4.17
) reads

(5.4.20)

u_{\varphi}\colon(g^{L}_{k-1}\circ g^{L}_{k-2}\circ\ldots\circ g^{L}_{s-1})^{-1}([\varphi])\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{P}}_{L(\varphi)}.

A local computation (as in the proof of Lemma
3.21
) gives the schematic equality

(5.4.21)

u_{\varphi}^{-1}({\mathbf{D}}_{L(\varphi)}^{r})=(g^{L}_{k-1}\circ g^{L}_{k-2}\circ\ldots\circ g^{L}_{s-1})^{-1}([\varphi])\cap{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(s-1)^{r}.

It follows that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{(h_{j-1,\geq s}^{L})^{-1}([\varphi])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{g^{L}_{s}\circ\ldots\circ g^{L}_{j-1}}_{|\ldots}\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt\ }
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{(g^{L}_{k-1}\circ g^{L}_{k-2}\circ\ldots\circ g^{L}_{s-1})^{-1}([\varphi])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{\varphi}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{P}}_{L(\varphi)}(j-1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\bar{L}}_{s}\circ\ldots\circ g^{\bar{L}}_{j-1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{P}}_{L(\varphi)}}

5.4.3.
Analogue of Subsubsections
5.3.2
and
5.3.3

Let
[\varphi]\in\left({\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1}\right)
, let
T
be a a smooth curve, and let

f\colon T\to{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
be a map such that
f(0)=[\varphi]
. Let
{\mathscr{S}}_{T}
be the sheaf on
X\times T
given by
{\mathscr{S}}_{T}\coloneq(\Id_{X}\times f)^{*}{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}
. We modify
{\mathscr{S}}_{T}
according to the exact sequence
in (
5.4.11
), i.e.

(5.4.22)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}\xrightarrow{\zeta}{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}\xrightarrow{\xi}{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0.

More precisely let
\iota\colon X=X\times\{0\}\hookrightarrow X\times T
be the inclusion map, and let
\widetilde{{\mathscr{S}}}_{T}
be the sheaf on

X\times T
fitting into the exact sequence

(5.4.23)

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathscr{S}}}_{T}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{T}\longrightarrow\iota_{*}{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0.

The restriction of
\widetilde{{\mathscr{S}}}_{T}
to
X\times\{0\}
fits into the exact sequence (see Claim
5.16
)

(5.4.24)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathscr{S}}}_{T|X\times\{0\}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}\longrightarrow 0,

where
U(\varphi)\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
is as in Claim
5.16
.
Our next goal is to describe the extension class of the exact sequence in (
5.4.24
). Applying the functor

\Hom_{X}(-,{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
to the exact sequence

(5.4.25)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes U(\varphi)^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{3}\longrightarrow 0

we get the exact sequence

(5.4.26)

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}}\otimes U(\varphi)\overset{\partial}{\longrightarrow}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})\longrightarrow 0.

Since the coboundary map is identified with the inclusion
U(\varphi)\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
, we get an isomorphism

\coker\varphi\xrightarrow{\sim}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
. Tensorizing by
(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}
we get an isomorphism

(5.4.27)

\iota_{\varphi}\colon\Hom(\ker\varphi,\coker\varphi)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee})

Let
\overline{df}_{0}\coloneq p\circ df_{0}
be the composition

(5.4.28)

\Theta_{T}(0)\overset{df_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\Theta_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}([\varphi])\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{N}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}/{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}([\varphi]),

where
p
is the quotient map.

Lemma 5.21
.

Suppose that the equalities in (
5.4.1
) hold. The extension class of the exact sequence in (
5.4.24
) is equal to

\iota_{\varphi}\circ w_{\varphi}\circ\overline{df}_{0}(v)
, where
v\in\Theta_{T}(0)
is non zero,
w_{\varphi}
is as in (
5.4.13
) and

\iota_{\varphi}
is as in (
5.4.27
).

Proof.

The exact sequences (
5.4.2
), (
5.4.6
) and (
5.4.22
) give the diagram

(5.4.29)

\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes W^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi\circ\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\ \alpha}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{S}}_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{U}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\beta\circ\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\gamma_{U(\varphi)}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{3}}

We have the maps

(5.4.30)

\Theta_{T}(0)\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi},{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\mu}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}),

where
\kappa

is the Kodaira-Spencer map at
0
, and
\mu(\gamma)\coloneq\xi\cup\gamma\cup\zeta
.
By Lemma
5.5
the extension class is equal to
\mu\circ\kappa(v)
.
After shrinking
T
around
0
there is
a lift
\widetilde{f}\colon T\to W^{\vee}\otimes\left(\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U\right)
of
f\colon T\to{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
.
Let

d\widetilde{f}_{0}\colon\Theta_{T}(0)\to W^{\vee}\otimes\left(\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U\right)
be the differential of
\widetilde{f}
at
0
. Tensorizing both sides of (
5.4.4
) by
W^{\vee}
we get the isomorphism

(5.4.31)

\eta_{L}\colon W^{\vee}\otimes(\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes W^{\vee}).

Then
\mu\circ\kappa(v)=(\xi\circ\alpha)\cup\eta_{L}(d\widetilde{f}_{0}(v))\cup(\beta\circ\zeta)
.
Hence it suffices to prove that

(5.4.32)

(\xi\circ\alpha)\cup\eta_{L}(d\widetilde{f}_{0}(v))\cup(\beta\circ\zeta)=\iota_{\varphi}\circ w_{\varphi}\circ\overline{df}_{0}(v).

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{U\otimes W^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{U}\hskip 16.38895pt}
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes W^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup\gamma_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\xi\circ\alpha\cup}
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes W^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\xi\circ\alpha)\cup-\cup(\beta\circ\zeta)}
\textstyle{U(\varphi)\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{U(\varphi)}\hskip 16.38895pt}
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup\gamma_{U(\varphi)}\ \ }
\textstyle{\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes(\ker\varphi)^{\vee})}

We claim that the above diagram is commutative. This is clear for the left-most square. To prove that the right-most square is commutative it suffices to show that
\gamma_{U}\circ\beta\circ\zeta=\gamma_{U(\varphi)}
. This holds because
\beta\circ\zeta
fits into the commutative diagram

(5.4.33)

\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes U^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{S}}_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{U}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes U(\varphi)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{U(\varphi)}}
\scriptstyle{\beta\circ\zeta}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{3}}

where the left-most vertical arrow is obtained from the transpose of the inclusion
U\hookrightarrow U(\varphi)
. In other words the extension defining
{\mathscr{S}}_{U}
is the push-out of the extension defining
{\mathscr{S}}_{U(\varphi)}
via the map

{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes U(\varphi)^{\vee}\to{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes U^{\vee}
.
Let

d\widetilde{\widetilde{f}}_{0}(v)\colon W\to\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
be a lift of
d\widetilde{f}_{0}(v)\colon W\to(\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U)
. By commutativity of the diagram above we get that

(\xi\circ\alpha)\cup\eta_{L}(d\widetilde{f}_{0}(v))\cup(\beta\circ\zeta)=(\xi\circ\alpha)\cup\gamma_{U}\cup d\widetilde{\widetilde{f}}_{0}(v)\cup(\beta\circ\zeta)=\\
=\gamma_{U(\varphi)}\cup(\xi\circ\alpha)\cup d\widetilde{\widetilde{f}}_{0}(v).

Since
\gamma_{U(\varphi)}\cup(\xi\circ\alpha)\cup d\widetilde{\widetilde{f}}_{0}(v)=\iota_{\varphi}\circ w_{\varphi}\circ\overline{df}_{0}(v)
, this proves the validity of (
5.4.32
).
∎

5.4.4.
Sheaves
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j)
on
X\times{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)

Suppose that (
5.4.1
) holds. Let
U\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
be a subspace, let
W
be a vector space such that (
5.4.5
) holds, and let
L=(U,W)
. Let
j\in\{k-1,\ldots,0\}
. In the present subsubsection we construct
coherent sheaves

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j)
on
X\times{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)
, for
j\in\{k-1,\ldots,0\}
, such that

(
1_{j}
)

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j)
is flat over
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)
.

(
2_{j}
)

If
z\in\left({\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{s+1}\right)
there exist a vector space
V(z)
of dimension
s
, a subspace
U(z)\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
of dimension
k-s
containing
U
, and
an exact sequence

(5.4.34)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U(z)}\longrightarrow{{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j)}_{|X\times\{z\}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\otimes V(z)\longrightarrow 0.

We have
{\mathbf{P}}_{W}(k-1)={\mathbf{P}}_{W}
, and
g_{k-1}\colon{\mathbf{P}}_{W}(k-1)\to{\mathbf{P}}_{W}
is the identity. We let

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{W}}(k-1)={\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{W}}
. Then
Item (
1_{k-1}
) holds because
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{W}}
is an extension of sheaves which are flat over
{\mathbf{P}}_{W}(j)
, and
Item (
2_{k-1}
) holds by Claim
5.16
.
If
\dim W=1
only Items (
1_{0}
) and
Item (
2_{0}
) need to be checked, and we have proved that they hold. The rest of the proof is
by induction on
k
(the dimension of
W
) and, once
U,W
are fixed, we prove that (
1_{k-1}
), (
2_{k-1}
),…,(
1_{0}
), (
2_{0}
) hold working our way downwards. Hence we fix a subspace
U\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
and a vector space
W
with

2\leq\dim W\leq\dim\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})/U
, and we assume that the statements above hold whenever
k<\dim W
, and that Items (
1_{j}
), (
2_{j}
) hold for the given
U,W
.

Our first task is to define
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{W}}(j-1)
. Let
z\in\left({\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{s+1}\right)
, and let
U(z)
,
V(z)
be as in
Item (
1_{j}
).
Applying the functor

\Hom_{X}(-,{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
to the exact sequence in (
5.4.34
) one gets an isomorphism

(5.4.35)

{\mathbb{C}}\Id_{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}}\otimes V(z)^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{X}({{\mathscr{S}}_{W}(j)}_{|X\times\{z\}},{\mathscr{F}}_{1}).

(We have
\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{S}}_{U(z)},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})=0
because
\Hom_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})=0
.) Since
{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{s}=\emptyset
if
s>j
it follows that there exists an exact sequence

(5.4.36)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}\longrightarrow{{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j)}_{|X\times{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{j}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Q}}\longrightarrow 0

which gives the exact sequence in (
5.4.34
) when restricted to
X\times\{z\}
for every
z\in{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{j}
.
Let
h_{j-1}\colon X\times{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}\to X\times{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{j}
be the restriction of
\Id_{X}\times g_{j-1}
.
Pulling back by
h_{j-1}
we get the exact sequence

(5.4.37)

0\longrightarrow h_{j-1}^{*}{\mathscr{K}}\longrightarrow h_{j-1}^{*}{{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j)}_{|X\times{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}}\longrightarrow h_{j-1}^{*}{\mathscr{Q}}\longrightarrow 0.

Note that
h_{j-1}^{*}{\mathscr{Q}}
is flat over
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}
.
Let
\nu\colon X\times{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}\hookrightarrow X\times{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}
be the inclusion map. We let
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j-1)
be the sheaf fitting into the exact sequence

(5.4.38)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j-1)\longrightarrow(\Id_{X}\times g_{j-1})^{*}{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)}\overset{\tau_{j-1}}{\longrightarrow}\nu_{*}\left(h_{j-1}^{*}{\mathscr{Q}}\right)\longrightarrow 0,

Let us prove that Item (
1_{j-1}
) holds. Let
{\mathscr{A}}
be a sheaf of

{\mathscr{O}}_{X\times{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)}
-modules
(not necessarily coherent). Since
h_{j-1}^{*}{\mathscr{Q}}
is flat over
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}
, and
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j}
is a Cartier divisor on
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)
, the spectral sequence for
\Tor
in
[
StP25
, Example 15.62.2]
gives that the sheaf

Tor_{2}^{{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)}}(\nu_{*}\left(h_{j-1}^{*}{\mathscr{Q}}\right),{\mathscr{A}})
vanishes. By the long exact sequence of
Tor
’s
that one gets by tensorizing the exact sequence in (
5.4.38
), we get that

Tor_{1}^{{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)}}({\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j-1),{\mathscr{A}})
vanishes. This proves that

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j-1)
is flat over
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j-1)
, i.e. that Item (
1_{j-1}
) holds.

Now we prove that Item (
2_{j-1}
) holds. Away from
{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq j}
(see (
5.4.16
)) the map
g_{j-1}
defines an isomorphism

({\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j-1)\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq j})\xrightarrow{\sim}({\mathbf{P}}_{L}(j)\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{j})
mapping

({\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j-1)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{j})
to
({\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j)^{j})
. If

z\notin{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq j}
then

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j-1)\cong{{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j)}_{|X\times\{g_{j-1}(z)\}}.

Hence the statement in (
2_{j-1}
) holds for all
z\notin{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq j}
because (
2_{j}
) holds. It remains to prove that the statement in (
2_{j-1}
) holds for all
z\in{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq j}
. Let
s\in\{k-1,\ldots,j\}
be such that

(5.4.39)

z\in({\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s}\setminus{\mathbf{E}}_{L}(j-1)^{\geq s+1}).

(Notation as in (
5.4.16
).)
Adopting the notation of Remark
5.19
we get that

(5.4.40)

h_{j-1,\geq s}^{L}(z)=[\varphi]\in({\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s}\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{L}^{s+1}).

Key-Remark
5.20
gives an isomorphism

(5.4.41)

v_{\varphi}\colon(h_{j-1,\geq s}^{L})^{-1}([\varphi])\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbf{P}}_{\bar{L}(\varphi)}(j-1),

where
\bar{L}(\varphi)=(\overline{U}(\varphi),\overline{W}(\varphi))
with
\overline{U}(\varphi)\subset\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})
the subspace containing
U
such that
\im\varphi=\overline{U}(\varphi)/U
, and

\overline{W}(\varphi)=\ker\varphi
. Thus
\dim\overline{W}(\varphi)<\dim W
and hence, by the inductive hypothesis, there exists a sheaf
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\bar{L}(\varphi)}}(j-1)
on
X\times{\mathbf{P}}_{\bar{L}(\varphi)}(j-1)
for which Items (
1_{j-1}
), (
2_{j-1}
) hold. By construction we have an isomorphism

(5.4.42)

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{L}}(j-1)_{|X\times(h_{j-1,\geq s}^{L})^{-1}([\varphi])}\cong(\Id_{X}\times v_{\varphi})^{*}{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\bar{L}(\varphi)}}(j-1).

Item (
2_{j-1}
) holds for
z\in(h_{j-1,\geq s}^{L})^{-1}([\varphi])
because it holds for

{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\bar{L}(\varphi)}}(j-1)
and because

(5.4.43)

{\mathbf{D}}_{L}(j-1)^{s}\cap(h_{j-1,\geq s}^{L})^{-1}([\varphi])=v_{\varphi}^{-1}({\mathbf{D}}_{\bar{L}(\varphi)}(j-1)^{s}).

5.5.
Extension of
\psi
to
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}

Recall that
{\mathscr{M}}={\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}(S,h_{S})
.
We assume that there is a universal family
{\mathbb{E}}_{2}
on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}
. If a universal family does not exist then it exist locally on
{\mathscr{M}}
(étale topology), and the proof that we give goes through by replacing
{\mathscr{M}}

with an étale covering
\{{\mathscr{U}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that there exists a universal family on
S\times{\mathscr{U}}_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Let
{\mathbb{G}}
be the sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}
given by (see
[
OG26
, Subsect. 2.1]
)

(5.5.1)

{\mathbb{G}}\coloneq{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathscr{M}}},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}).

Then
{\mathbb{G}}
is flat over
{\mathscr{M}}
by Prop. 2.5 in loc. cit., and for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}
the restriction of
{\mathbb{G}}
to
S^{[2]}\times\{[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\}
is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
. For
x\in{\mathscr{M}}
let
{\mathbb{G}}_{x}\coloneq{\mathbb{G}}_{|S^{[2]}\times\{x\}}
. By Proposition
4.2
and Subsection
4.3
the sheaf
{\mathbb{G}}_{x}
is stable if
x\notin{\mathscr{D}}
. We let

(5.5.2)

\begin{matrix}{\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}&\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}&M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}\\
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]&\mapsto&[{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})]\end{matrix}

be the map associated to the restriction of
{\mathbb{G}}
to
S^{[2]}\times({\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}})
, see (
5.1.1
).

In Subsection
3.4
we have defined a chain of birational maps

\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}={\mathscr{M}}(0)\xrightarrow{f_{0}}{\mathscr{M}}(1)\xrightarrow{f_{1}}\ldots{\mathscr{M}}(a)\xrightarrow{f_{a}}{\mathscr{M}}.

Remark 5.22
.
