In Subsection
3.4
we have defined closed prime divisors
E(j)^{s}\subset{\mathscr{M}}(j)
for
s>j
(note:
E(j)^{s}=\emptyset
if
s>a
) with the following properties:

(1)

f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j}
maps
{\mathscr{M}}(j)_{*}\coloneq{\mathscr{M}}(j)\setminus(E(j)^{a}\cup E(j)^{a-1}\cup\ldots\cup E(j)^{j+1})
isomorphically to

{\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{j+1}
.

(2)

Let
k>j
. Then
f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j}
maps
E(j)^{k}\setminus E(j)^{k+1}
to
{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
.

In particular we have the disjoint union

(5.5.3)

{\mathscr{M}}(j)={\mathscr{M}}(j)_{*}\sqcup\left(E(j)^{j+1}\setminus E(j)^{j+2}\right)\sqcup\ldots\sqcup\left(E(j)^{a-1}\setminus E(j)^{a}\right)\sqcup E(j)^{a}.

For
j=0
, i.e. for
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
, we get the disjoint union

(5.5.4)

\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}=\left({\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}\right)\sqcup\left(\widehat{E}^{1}\setminus\widehat{E}^{2}\right)\sqcup\ldots\sqcup\left(\widehat{E}^{a-1}\setminus\widehat{E}^{a}\right)\sqcup\widehat{E}^{a}.

In the present subsection we prove the following key result.

Proposition 5.23
.

The map
\psi
extends to a regular map
\widehat{\psi}\colon\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}\to M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
. Let
x\in\widehat{E}^{1}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{a}
. The

h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable vector bundle
{\mathscr{F}}
on
S^{[2]}
such that
[{\mathscr{F}}]=\widehat{\psi}(x)
is described as follows.
Let
k\in\{1,\ldots,a\}
be such that
x\in\widehat{E}^{k}\setminus\widehat{E}^{k+1}
.
Let
f_{a}\circ\ldots\circ f_{0}(x)=[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]
(hence
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
).
If
k<a
then
{\mathscr{F}}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})
where
{\mathscr{H}}

is as
in (
5.1.2
), and

J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
is as in (
5.1.3
). If
k=a
then
{\mathscr{F}}\cong{\mathscr{B}}(p,\im(f(p)^{t}))
where
p
,
\im(f(p)^{t})
are as
in (
5.1.4
) and (
5.1.6
)
respectively.

The proof of the above proposition is at the end of the subsection.

Definition 5.24
.

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1})
. If
a>k>0
let

be the vector bundle on

fitting into the exact

), let

If

Then the equalities in (

) hold (see (

), Proposition

, and

4.29
).
Let

(5.5.5)

{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\coloneq{\mathbb{P}}(\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\Ext^{1}_{X}({\mathscr{F}}_{3},{\mathscr{F}}_{1})).

In other words
{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}={\mathbf{P}}_{L}
, where

(see (

)). This makes sense
because the inequality in (

) holds, in fact it is an equality. Let

and

.

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1})
, and let

k>j\geq 0
.
We define an isomorphism

(5.5.6)

\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)\colon{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)\xrightarrow{\sim}(f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])

as follows. Away from

the map
f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{k-1}\colon{\mathscr{M}}(k-1)\to{\mathscr{M}}
is the blow-up of

{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
. Hence, referring to (
5.3.18
), we have the isomorphism

\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(k-1)\coloneq{\mathbb{P}}((\beta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\circ\alpha_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{-1})\colon{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(k-1)\xrightarrow{\sim}(f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]).

By
Lemma
3.21

\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(k-1)
defines an isomorphism

(5.5.7)

{\mathbf{D}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}^{h}\xrightarrow{\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(k-1)_{|\ldots}}{\mathscr{D}}(k-1)^{h}\cap(f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{k-1})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]).

If
j<k-1
we have series of birational maps

(f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\xrightarrow{f_{j|\ldots}}\ldots\xrightarrow{f_{k-2|\ldots}}(f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{k-1})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])

which are identified with the composition
{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)\xrightarrow{g_{j}}\ldots\xrightarrow{g_{k-2}}{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(k-1)
of the blow ups in
Definition
5.18
because of the isomorphism in (
5.5.7
). This identifcation defines the isomorphism
in (
5.5.6
).

Note that if
h\leq j
then

\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)
defines an isomorphism

(5.5.8)

{\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)}_{|\ldots}\colon{\mathbf{D}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)^{h}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{h}\cap(f_{a}\circ f_{a-1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]).

Proposition 5.25
.

For
j\in\{a,a-1,\ldots,0\}
there exists a sheaf
{\mathbb{G}}(j)
on
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}(j)
which is flat over
{\mathscr{M}}(j)
and such that the following hold:

(
A_{j}
)

Let
x\in{\mathscr{M}}(j)\setminus(E(j)^{a}\cup\ldots\cup E(j)^{j+1})
. Then

{\mathbb{G}}(j)_{x}\cong{\mathbb{G}}_{f_{a}\circ\ldots f_{j}(x)}
.

(
B_{j}
)

Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1})
, and let

k>j\geq 0
. Then we have an isomorphism

(5.5.9)

(\Id_{S^{[2]}}\times\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j))^{*}{\mathbb{G}}(j)\cong{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}(j),

where
{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)}
is the sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)
of Subsubsection
5.4.4
.

Proof.

By definition
{\mathscr{M}}(a)={\mathscr{M}}
. We set
{\mathbb{G}}(a)\coloneq{\mathbb{G}}
. Assuming that there exists a sheaf
{\mathbb{G}}(j)
such that Items (
A_{j}
), (
B_{j}
) hold for
j\in\{a,a-1,\ldots,1\}
, we prove that there exists a sheaf
{\mathbb{G}}(j-1)
such that Items (
A_{j-1}
), (
B_{j-1}
)
hold. Let
x\in{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}
, and let
{\mathbb{G}}(j)_{x}\coloneq{\mathbb{G}}(j)_{S^{[2]}\times\{x\}}
.
We claim that

(5.5.10)

\hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathbb{G}}(j)_{x},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})=j,

and that the tautological map

(5.5.11)

{\mathbb{G}}(j)_{x}\longrightarrow\Hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathbb{G}}(j)_{x},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}

is surjective. To see why, suppose first that
x\in{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}\setminus(E(j)^{a}\cup\ldots\cup E(j)^{j+1})
. Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\coloneq f_{a}\circ\ldots f_{j}(x)
. Note that

[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{j}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{j+1}
(see Remark
5.22
). If
a>j
then (
5.5.10
) and surjectivity of (
5.5.11
) follow from the exact sequence in (
5.3.8
), the isomorphism in (
5.3.10
), and Proposition
4.17
. If
a=j
then (
5.5.10
) and surjectivity of (
5.5.11
) follow from (
5.3.20
), (
5.3.22
), and Proposition
4.29
. Now suppose that
x\in{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}\cap(E(j)^{a}\cup\ldots\cup E(j)^{j+1})
. Then
(see (
5.5.3
)) there exists
a\geq k>j
such that

x\in{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}\cap E(j)^{k}\setminus E(j)^{k+1}
. By the isomorphism in (
5.5.8
) we have
z\in{\mathbf{D}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)^{j}
.
By Item (
B_{j}
) and Item (
2_{j}
) of Subsubsection
5.4.4
there exists an exact sequence

(5.5.12)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}}_{U(z)}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{G}}(j)_{x}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\otimes V(z)\longrightarrow 0,

where
\dim V(z)=j
. Since
\Hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{S}}_{U(z)},{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-})=\{0\}
this proves that (
5.5.10
) holds and also the surjectivity of the map in (
5.5.11
).

Recall that
E(j-1)^{j}=f_{j-1}^{-1}({\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j})
. By the result on
{\mathbb{G}}(j)_{x}
for
x\in{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}

that we have just proved there exist a vector bundle
{\mathscr{U}}^{j}
on
E(j-1)^{j}
(pulled back from a vector bundle on
{\mathscr{D}}(j)^{j}
) and a surjection

(5.5.13)

(\Id_{S^{[2]}}\times f_{j-1})^{*}{\mathbb{G}}(j)_{|S^{[2]}\times E(j-1)^{j}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{U}}^{j}\longrightarrow 0.

Let
{\mathbb{G}}(j-1)
be the sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}(j-1)
fitting into the exact sequence

(5.5.14)

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{G}}(j-1)\longrightarrow(\Id_{S^{[2]}}\times f_{j-1})^{*}{\mathbb{G}}(j)\overset{\tau}{\longrightarrow}\iota_{*}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{U}}^{j}\right)\longrightarrow 0,

where
\iota\colon S^{[2]}\times E(j-1)^{j}\to S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}(j-1)
is the inclusion map, and the map
\tau
is defined by the surjection in (
5.5.13
).
The sheaf
{\mathbb{G}}(j-1)
is flat over
{\mathscr{M}}(j-1)
by Lemma
5.4
. It remains to prove that Items (
A_{j-1}
), (
B_{j-1}
)
hold. Let
x\in{\mathscr{M}}(j)\setminus(E(j-1)^{a}\cup\ldots\cup E(j-1)^{j})
. We have an isomorphism

because

. Since

we get that Item (
A_{j-1}
) holds. Lastly we prove that Item (
B_{j-1}
)
holds. First we note that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\quad{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j-1)\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j-1)\hskip 16.38895pt\ }
\scriptstyle{g^{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}_{j-1}}
\textstyle{\quad(f_{a}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j-1})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{j-1|\ldots}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j)\hskip 16.38895pt}
\textstyle{\ \ \,(f_{a}\circ\ldots\circ f_{j})^{-1}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])}

Pulling back the exact sequence in (
5.5.14
) by the map

, and recalling the

), we get the exact sequence

0\to(\Id_{S^{[2]}}\times\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(j-1))^{*}{\mathbb{G}}(j-1)\longrightarrow(\Id_{S^{[2]}}\times g^{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}_{j-1})^{*}{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}}(j)\overset{\pi_{j-1}}{\longrightarrow}\nu_{*}\left({\mathscr{R}}\right)\to 0,
