∎

Proof of Proposition
5.23
.

Let
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}\coloneq{\mathbb{G}}(0)
. Thus
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}
is a sheaf on
S^{[2]}\times\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
which is flat over
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
(recall that
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}={\mathscr{M}}(0)
). Let
x\in\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
, and let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\coloneq f_{a}\circ\ldots\circ f_{0}(x)
.

If

x\notin\widehat{E}^{a}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{1}
then

(5.5.15)

\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}_{x}\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})

by Item (
A_{0}
) of Proposition
5.25
.
Moreover
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}
(because
x\notin\widehat{E}^{a}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{1}
) and hence
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
is

h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable by Proposition
4.2
.

If
x\in\widehat{E}^{a}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{1}
then by (
5.5.4
) there exists
k\in\{1,\ldots,a\}
such that

x\in\widehat{E}^{k}\setminus\widehat{E}^{k+1}
(if
k=a
this means that
x\in\widehat{E}^{a}
). If
k<a
let
{\mathscr{H}}
and
J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
be as in
Definition
5.24
,
if
k=a
let
p
and
V_{a}
be as in loc. cit.
By the isomorphism in (
5.5.6
) there exists
z\in{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(0)
such that
\theta_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(0)(z)=x
, and by Item (
B_{0}
) of Proposition
5.25
we have

(5.5.16)

\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}_{x}\cong{{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(0)}}_{|S^{[2]}\times\{z\}}.

By (
5.4.15
) (see also Definition
5.18
)
z\in{\mathbf{D}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(0)\setminus{\mathbf{D}}_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}(1)
. By Item (
2_{0}
) of Subsubsection
5.4.4
the right-hand side of (
5.5.16
) is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{S}}_{U(z)}
where

(5.5.17)

U(z)\subset\begin{cases}\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}\cong\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}&\text{if $k<a$,}\\
\Ext^{1}_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})^{+}\cong\Ann V_{a}&\text{if $k=a$,}\end{cases}

is a subspace of dimension
k
. (The isomorphisms above are given by Propositions
4.17
and
4.29
.) Since

\dim\Ann J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}=k
and
\dim\Ann V_{a}=a
(
=k
), the inclusion in (
5.5.17
) is an equality. It follows that

(5.5.18)

\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}_{x}\cong\begin{cases}{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})&\text{if $k<a$,}\\
{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})&\text{if $k=a$.}\end{cases}

Hence
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}_{x}
is locally-free and
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable. In fact local-freeness is clear if
k<a
because
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})
is an extension of locally-free sheaves, and if
k=a
it is Corollary
4.43
, while stability of
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})
is the content of Proposition
4.33
, and stability of
{\mathscr{B}}(p,V_{a})
is the content of Proposition
4.38
. Since
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}
is a flat
family of locally-free
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable sheaves on
S^{[2]}
parametrized by
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
(and hence locally-free itself), and (
5.5.15
) holds for a general
x\in\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
, it induces a regular classifying morphism
\widehat{\psi}\colon\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}\to M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
. The
isomorphism in (
5.5.18
) gives the description of the vector bundle
{\mathscr{F}}
such that
[{\mathscr{F}}]=\widehat{\psi}(x)
for

x\in\widehat{E}^{k}\setminus\widehat{E}^{k+1}
.
∎

5.6.
Proof of Theorem
5.1

Let
\Gamma\subset{\mathscr{M}}\times M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
be the image of
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}\xrightarrow{\widehat{f}\times\widehat{\psi}}{\mathscr{M}}\times M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
.
Then
\Gamma
is closed, and by Proposition
5.23
the projection
p_{\gamma}\colon\Gamma\to{\mathscr{M}}
is bijective. Hence
p_{\gamma}
is an isomorphism because
{\mathscr{M}}
is smooth. Thus
\widehat{\psi}
descends to a regular map
\overline{\psi}\colon{\mathscr{M}}\to M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
which extends
\psi
. This proves Item (a). Item (b) follows at once from Proposition
5.23
.
It remains to prove Item (c), i.e. that
\overline{\psi}
is bijective. It is surjective because the image is closed and dense in
M_{{\bf w}}^{\bullet}
. We proceed to prove that

\overline{\psi}
is injective. Let
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}
. If
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
with
1\leq k<a
let
{\mathscr{H}}
and
J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
be as in
Definition
5.24
,
if
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}
let
p
and
V_{a}
be as in loc. cit.
Let
{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})
be the
h_{S^{[2]}}
slope stable vector bundle on
S^{[2]}
such that

\overline{\psi}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])=[{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})]
.
Then

\hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2}),{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+})=\hom_{S^{[2]}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-},{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2}))=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}^{*}\coloneq{\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}$,}\\
k&\text{if $[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}$.}\end{cases}

In fact the equality for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}^{*}
follows from Lemma
4.15
for
{\mathscr{H}}={\mathscr{E}}_{2}
, because
{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
.
The equality for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}

follows from the exact sequences (
4.4.5
), (
4.4.7
) and (
4.4.2
), Lemma
4.15
if
k<a
, and from the exact sequences (
4.4.22
), (
4.4.27
)
and (
4.4.2
), Proposition
4.29
if
k=a
. Suppose that
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}],[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}
and that
\overline{\psi}([{\mathscr{E}}_{2}])=\overline{\psi}([{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})])
. By Items (1), (2) above
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}],[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]
belong to the same stratum of the stratification

{\mathscr{M}}={\mathscr{M}}^{*}\sqcup\left({\mathscr{D}}^{1}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{2}\right)\sqcup\ldots\sqcup\left({\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}\right)\sqcup\ldots\sqcup{\mathscr{D}}^{a}.

If
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}],[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}^{*}
then
{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})
,
{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})
. Thus

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2})\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})
. It follows at once from the BKR correspondence that
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\cong{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}

and hence
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]=[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]
.

Suppose that
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}],[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{k+1}
with
1\leq k<a
.
Then
{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}_{2})\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})
and
{\mathscr{F}}({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2})\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}}^{\prime},J_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}})
. Thus

{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}}^{\prime},J_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}})
.
It follows from the exact sequences (
4.4.5
), (
4.4.7
) and Item (2) above that

{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}}^{\prime},J_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}})^{+}
and

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}}^{\prime})
. By the BKR correspondence we get that
{\mathscr{H}}\cong{\mathscr{H}}^{\prime}
. Thus (see (
3.3.9
)) we have exact sequences

(5.6.1)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0

and

(5.6.2)

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\otimes\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0,

with extension classes given by inclusions
\iota\colon\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
and

\iota^{\prime}\colon\Hom_{S}({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{S}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})
.
Since
{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}\cong{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}})^{+}
, the extension classes of the exact sequences (see (
4.4.5
))

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}})^{+}\overset{\lambda^{+}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}\longrightarrow 0

and

0\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{H}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}({\mathscr{H}},J_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}})^{+}\overset{\lambda^{+}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{+}\otimes J_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}}\longrightarrow 0

are the same (up to
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
). By Proposition
4.17
we get that
J_{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}=J_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}}
, i.e.
\im(\iota)=\im(\iota^{\prime})
. This proves that the extension classes of (
5.6.1
) and (
5.6.2
) are the same. Thus
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\cong{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}

and hence
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]=[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]
.

If
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}],[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}^{a}
the proof that
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]=[{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}]
is analogous.
∎

6.
The Donaldson-Mukai map

6.1.
The Mukai map: recap

Let
S
be a
K3
surface.
Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a sheaf on
S\times T
, flat over
T
.
Set

(6.1.1)

\begin{matrix}H(S)&\overset{\theta_{{\mathscr{F}}}}{\longrightarrow}&H^{2}(T)\\
x&\mapsto&q_{T,*}\left[\ch({\mathscr{F}})\cdot q_{S}^{*}(x^{\vee}\cdot\Td_{S}^{1/2})\right]_{6},\end{matrix}

where
q_{S},q_{T}
are the projections of
S\times T
to
S
and
T
,

(x_{0}+x_{2}+x_{4})^{\vee}\coloneq x_{0}-x_{2}+x_{4}
if

x_{2i}\in H^{2i}(S)
, and the subscript
6
denotes the component in
H^{6}(S\times S^{[2]})
.

Now assume that the Mukai vector of
{\mathscr{F}}_{|S\times\{t\}}
is equal to a fixed
v
:

(6.1.2)

v({\mathscr{F}}_{|S\times\{t\}})=v=(r,l,s)\in H^{0}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})_{>0}\oplus\NS(S)\oplus H^{4}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})\qquad\forall t\in T.

Let
{\mathscr{L}}
be a line-bundle on
T
. The restrictions of
\theta_{{\mathscr{F}}}
and
\theta_{{\mathscr{F}}\otimes q_{T}^{*}{\mathscr{L}}}

to the orthogonal of
v
for Mukai’s symmetric bilinear pairing (defined as
\langle x,y\rangle\coloneq\int_{S}(-x^{\vee}\cdot y)
) are the same (they are not equal on all of
H(S)
).

Suppose that
h_{S}
is a polarization of
S
, and let
{\mathscr{M}}_{v}\coloneq{\mathscr{M}}_{v}(S,h_{S})
. Assume that there exists a universal sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}_{v}
on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v}
(in particular
v
is primitive). One sets

(6.1.3)

\begin{matrix}v^{\bot}&\overset{\theta_{v}}{\longrightarrow}&H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}_{v})\\
x&\mapsto&q_{{\mathscr{M}}_{v},*}\left[\ch({\mathscr{F}}_{v})\cdot q_{S}^{*}(x^{\vee}\cdot\Td_{S}^{1/2})\right]_{6}.\end{matrix}

Suppose that
v^{2}>0
, i.e.
\dim{\mathscr{M}}_{v}>2
. Then
\theta_{v}
is an isomorphism of integral Hodge structures, if
F^{2}H(S)\coloneq F^{2}H^{2}(S)
and

F^{1}H(S)\coloneq(F^{1}H^{2}(S)\oplus H^{0}(S)\oplus H^{4}(S))
, and it matches Mukai’s pairing and the BBF quadratic form on
H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}_{v})
, see
[
OG97a
,
Y01
]
.

Here and in the sequel the following consequence of the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch Theorem will be handy.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
i\colon X\hookrightarrow Y
be the inclusion of a smooth closed subvariety
X
of pure codimension
d
in a smooth variety
Y
. Let
{\mathscr{F}}
be a coherent sheaf on
X
. Then,
modulo
H^{>2d+2}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
, we have

(6.1.4)

\ch(i_{*}{\mathscr{F}})\equiv r({\mathscr{F}})\cl(Y)+i_{*}c_{1}({\mathscr{F}})-\frac{r({\mathscr{F}})}{2}\cl(Y)\cdot c_{1}(Y)+\frac{r({\mathscr{F}})}{2}i_{*}c_{1}(X).

Let
v_{0}\coloneq(1,0,-1)
. Associating to
[Z]\in S^{[2]}
the isomorphism class of the ideal sheaf

{\mathscr{I}}_{Z}
we get an isomorphism
S^{[2]}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{0}}
. We identify
S^{[2]}
with
{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{0}}
via this map.
If
{\mathscr{Z}}\subset S\times S^{[2]}
is the universal subscheme then

{\mathscr{I}}_{{\mathscr{Z}}}
is a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{0}}
. Hence we have the isomorphism
\theta_{v_{0}}\colon v_{0}^{\bot}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(S^{[2]})
.
One checks that

(6.1.5)

\delta\coloneq\theta_{v_{0}}(1,0,1)=\cl(\{[Z]\in S^{[2]}\mid\text{$Z$ is non-reduced}).

(Use Lemma
6.1
.) For
\beta\in H^{2}(S)
we set

(6.1.6)

{\bm{\mu}}(\beta)\coloneq\theta_{v_{0}}(0,\beta,0).

The notation agrees with that in
[
OG26
, (2.2.6)-(2.2.7)]
(except that here
\mu
morphed into
{\bm{\mu}}
).

Remark 6.2
.

Let
v_{2}
be as in (
4.2.2
). If
m_{0}\equiv 3\pmod{4}
then there exists a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v_{2}}
, see
[
Muk87
, Rmk. A.7]
. If
m_{0}\equiv 1\pmod{4}
there exists a universal sheaf if there exists a divisor
C
on
S
such that
C\cdot D
is odd, see loc. cit. We have (independently of the existence of a universal sheaf)

(6.1.7)

\theta_{v_{2}}(v_{1})=\cl({\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}).

(This makes sense because
v_{1}\bot v_{2}
.)

6.2.
Families of
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
-bundles and the Donaldson-Mukai map

Let
X
be a HK manifold, let
T
be a complex space, and let
{\mathscr{P}}\to X\times T
a
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
-bundle. One may define a
Donaldson-Mukai map
\lambda_{{\mathscr{P}}}\colon H^{2}(X)\to H^{2}(T)
proceeding as follows.
Let
2n
be the dimension of
X
. Let

{\mathbf{D}}\colon H^{2}(X)\to H^{4n-2}(X)
be the composition of the isomorphism
H^{2}(X)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(X)^{\vee}
defined by the BBF bilinear symmetric form and the isomorphism
H^{2}(X)^{\vee}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H^{4n-2}(X)
given by Poincarè duality. Thus
{\mathbf{D}}
is characterized by the equality

(6.2.1)

\int_{X}{\mathbf{D}}(\alpha)\wedge\beta=\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle_{X},\qquad\forall\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(X),

where
\langle-,-\rangle_{X}
is the BBF bilinear symmetric form of
X
.
Let
p_{X},p_{T}
be the projections of
X\times T
to the two factors. The
Donaldson-Mukai map
associated to
{\mathscr{P}}
is given by

(6.2.2)

\begin{matrix}H^{2}(X)&\overset{\lambda_{{\mathscr{P}}}}{\longrightarrow}&H^{2}(T)\\
\alpha&\mapsto&p_{T,*}\left[c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}}))\cdot p_{X}^{*}({\mathbf{D}}(\alpha))\right]\end{matrix}

Remark 6.3
.

Suppose that
F
is a vector bundle on
X\times T
. Then
\mathfrak{g}({\mathbb{P}}(F))\cong End^{0}(F)
(see (
1.1.4
)),
and hence

(6.2.3)

\lambda_{{\mathbb{P}}(F)}(\alpha)=p_{T,*}\left[\Delta(F)\cdot p_{X}^{*}({\mathbf{D}}(\alpha))\right].

Remark 6.4
.

Let
X
be projective of dimension
2n
, and
H
be a very ample divisor on
X
. Let
D_{1},\ldots,D_{2n-1}\in|H|
, with transverse intersection
C=D_{1}\cap\ldots\cap D_{2n-1}
. Let
h\coloneq\cl(H)\in H^{2}(X)
, and
c\coloneq\cl(C)\in H_{2}(X)
. Then

(6.2.4)

\lambda_{{\mathscr{P}}}(h)=\frac{1}{c_{X}\cdot(2n-1)!!}c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}}))/c,

where
/
denotes slant product. In fact the above formula follows from the equality

(6.2.5)

{\mathbf{D}}(h)=\frac{1}{c_{X}\cdot(2n-1)!!}h^{2n-1}.

Let
\overline{{\bf w}}
be a mock Mukai vector for
X
, see (
1.1.6
).
Let

{\mathscr{P}}_{\overline{{\bf w}}}\to X\times M_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(X,\omega)

be the universal bundle of projective spaces, i.e.

(6.2.6)

{{\mathscr{P}}_{\overline{{\bf w}}}}_{|X\times\{[{\mathscr{P}}]\}}\cong{\mathscr{P}}.

for any
[{\mathscr{P}}]\in M_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(X,\omega)
.
Set

(6.2.7)

\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}}\coloneq\lambda_{{\mathscr{P}}_{\overline{{\bf w}}}}.

Below is a result valid in the case that
\omega=c_{1}(L)
where
L
is an ample line bundle.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
(X,L)
be a polarized HK manifold. Let
Y\subset M_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(X,\omega)
be a projective subscheme. The restriction of

\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(c_{1}(L))
to
Y
is ample.

Proof.

Let
2n=\dim X
. There exists
m
such that
L^{\otimes m}
is very ample and the following hold.

(1)

If
D_{1},\ldots,D_{2n-1}\in|L^{\otimes m}|
have transverse intersection
C=D_{1}\cap\ldots\cap D_{2n-1}
, then the restriction
{\mathscr{P}}_{|C}
is slope stable for every
[{\mathscr{P}}]\in Y
.

(2)

If
[{\mathscr{P}}]\in Y
then the map
H^{1}(X,\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}}))\to H^{1}(C,\mathfrak{g}({\mathscr{P}})_{|C})
is injective.

In fact Item (1) holds by Theorem
2.17
and the analogous Bogomolov Effective Restriction Theorem for slope-stable vector bundles, see
[
Bog93
]
or
[
HL10
, Thm. 7.3.5]
if
\dim X=2
, and
[
L04
, Thm. 5.2]
in general.
Item (2) holds by Serre’s classical vanishing results. Hence we have a regular restriction map

(6.2.8)

\begin{matrix}Y&\xrightarrow{\rho}&\overline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{C}\\
[{\mathscr{P}}]&\mapsto&[{\mathscr{P}}_{|C}]\end{matrix}

where
\overline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{C}
is a moduli space of semi-stable projective bundles on the curve
C
(a moduli space of semistable vector bundles because
the corresponding Brauer class is necessarily trivial), and
\rho

is finite onto its image. By the equality in (
6.2.4
) we get that, up to a positive multiple,
\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(c_{1}(L))
is equal to

\rho^{*}(\alpha)
where
\alpha
is the ample determinant line-bundle on
\overline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{C}
, and hence is ample.
∎

6.3.
Stable vector bundles as stable projective bundles

Let
X
be a compact Kähler manifold, with Kähler class
\omega
, and let
{\mathscr{P}}\to X
be a
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle. There is a notion of

\omega
slope-stability for
{\mathscr{P}}
, defined in terms of the associated principal
\PGL_{r}({\mathbb{C}})
-bundle on
X
, see Remark
2.16
. If

V\to X
is an
\omega
slope-stable rank-
r
vector bundle then
{\mathbb{P}}(V)\to X
is
\omega
slope-stable as
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle.
It follows that by mapping
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
(notation of Theorem
5.1
) to
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{F}})
we get a map from
M_{{\bf w}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
to a suitable moduli space of
{\mathbb{P}}^{8a^{3}-1}
-bundles. This map is an embedding because
S^{[2]}
has no non-trivial line-bundles. The numerical invariants (see Subsection
1.1
) of the relevant

{\mathbb{P}}^{8a^{3}-1}
-bundles are the following.
Set
{\bf w}_{a}\coloneq{\bf w}(D,a)
.
Let

(6.3.1)

\widehat{\gamma}_{a}\coloneq[-({\bm{\mu}}(D)-a\delta)]\in H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})/2aH^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}}).

Let
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in M_{{\bf w}_{a}}
. By
[
HS03
, Lemma 2.5]
we have

(6.3.2)

\eta_{8a^{3}}({\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{F}}))=\overline{\gamma}_{a}\coloneq[4a^{2}\widehat{\gamma}_{a}]\in H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})/8a^{3}H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}}).

Moreover
\Delta({\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{F}}))=\Delta({\mathscr{F}})=4a^{6}c_{2}(S^{[2]})/3
. Let (see (
1.1.9
))

(6.3.3)

\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}\coloneq(8a^{3},\overline{\gamma}_{a},\frac{4a^{6}}{3}c_{2}(S^{[2]})),

and let
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
be the moduli space of slope-stable
{\mathbb{P}}^{8a^{3}-1}
-bundles
{\mathscr{P}}
on
S^{[2]}

with
\eta_{8a^{3}}({\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{F}}))=\overline{\gamma}_{a}
and
\Delta({\mathscr{P}})=4a^{6}c_{2}(S^{[2]})/3
.
By the preceding discussion we have an isomorphism

(6.3.4)

\begin{matrix}M_{{\bf w}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}&\xrightarrow{\sim}&M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}\\
[{\mathscr{F}}]&\mapsto&[{\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{F}})]\end{matrix}

where
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}\subset M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})
is closed and irreducible.
Let

(6.3.5)

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\nu_{a}}M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}

be the normalization.
Let
v(a)
be as in (
3.1.3
). By the isomorphism in (
6.3.4
) and Corollary
5.2
we have an isomorphism

(6.3.6)

\widetilde{\psi}\colon{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}(S,h_{S})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}\\

Remark 6.6
.

Let
\gamma_{a}\coloneq-({\bm{\mu}}(D)-a\delta)
, so that
\widehat{\gamma}_{a}=[\gamma_{a}]
. Then

(6.3.7)

\divisore(\gamma_{a})=1,\qquad q_{S^{[2]}}(\gamma_{a})\equiv 2a^{2}-2\pmod{4a}.

In fact let
\cl(D)=l\beta
where
\beta\in H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})
is primitive. Then
\gcd\{l,2a\}=1
by (
4.2.1
). It follows that

\divisore(\gamma_{a})=1
. The congruence is straightforward.
Note:

(6.3.8)

q_{S^{[2]}}(\gamma_{a})\equiv\begin{cases}-2\pmod{4a}&\text{if $a$ is even,}\\
2a-2\pmod{4a}&\text{if $a$ is odd.}\end{cases}

6.4.
The Donaldson-Mukai map for
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}

6.4.1.
Statement of the result

Hypotheses and notation are as in Subsections
6.2
and
6.3
. We often set

M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet}=M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},h_{S^{[2]}})^{\bullet}
etc.
Let

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\coloneq\nu_{a}^{*}\circ\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
(see (
6.2.7
) and (
6.3.5
)). Thus we have maps

(6.4.1)

H^{2}(S^{[2]})\overset{\widetilde{\lambda}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet})\overset{\widetilde{\psi}^{*}}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}).

Let
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\colon H^{2}(S^{[2]})\to H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet})
and

\varphi_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\colon H^{2}(S^{[2]})\to H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)})
be given by

(6.4.2)

\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\coloneq 32^{-1}\cdot a^{-4}\cdot\widetilde{\lambda}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}},\qquad\varphi_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\coloneq\widetilde{\psi}^{*}\circ\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}.

Proposition 6.7
.

Let hypotheses be as in Subsection
5.1
. Suppose that there exists a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
.
Then

\varphi_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\gamma)=\begin{cases}\theta_{v(a)}(\beta+\frac{(\beta,D)}{2a}\eta_{S}),&\text{if $\gamma=\bm{\mu}(\beta)$}.\\
\theta_{v(a)}(v_{1})&\text{if $\gamma=\delta$},\end{cases}

where
(-,-)
is the intersection form, and
\eta_{S}\in H^{4}(S)
is the orientation form.

Corollary 6.8
.

The map
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\colon H^{2}(S^{[2]})\to H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet})
is a rational Hodge isometry
(we are not assuming that there exists a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
).

Proof.

It is clear that
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
is a morphism of Hodge structures. It remains to prove that it is a rational isometry. Suppose first that there exists a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. The map
\bm{\mu}\colon H^{2}(S)\to H^{2}(S^{[2]})
matches the intersection form on
H^{2}(S)
with the BBF form on
H^{2}(S^{[2]})
, and the image is the orthogonal of
\delta
, which has square
-2
. The map
H^{2}(S)\to H(S)

defined by
\beta\mapsto\beta+\frac{(\beta,D)}{2a}\eta_{S}
matches the intersection form on
H^{2}(S)
with the Mukai form, and the image is Mukai-orthogonal to
v_{1}
. Moreover
v_{1}^{2}=-2=\delta^{2}
(squares are for the Mukai form). Mukai’s map

\theta_{v(a)}
matches the Mukai form on
v(a)^{\bot}
with the BBF form on
H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)})
. It follows
that
\varphi_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
is a rational Hodge isometry, and hence so is
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
.

If no universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
exists, we may specialize
S
so that a universal sheaf exists, see Remark
6.2
. Since
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
is locally constant it follows that it is a rational isometry.
∎

The result below will be useful later on, when considering twistor families.

Corollary 6.9
.

There exists a non empty subcone
{\mathscr{U}}\subset{\mathscr{K}}(S^{[2]})
of the Kähler cone containing
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
in its closure and such that

\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\omega)\in{\mathscr{K}}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet})
for all
\omega\in{\mathscr{U}}
.

Proof.

For
\epsilon>0
and small enough,
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})-\epsilon\delta
is in the ample cone of
S^{[2]}
. By Proposition
6.7
and (
6.1.7
) we have

(6.4.3)

\varphi_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\bm{\mu}(h_{S})-\epsilon\delta)=\theta_{v(a)}\left(h_{S}+\frac{(h_{S},D)}{2a}\eta_{S}\right)-\epsilon\cl({\mathscr{D}}_{v_{2}}).

We claim that, as is well-known,
\theta_{v(a)}(h_{S}+(h_{s},D)\eta_{S}/2a)
is big and nef. We sketch the argument for the reader’s convenience. We are free to replace
h_{S}
by a high positive multiple. Let
C
be a smooth curve in the complete linear system
|h_{S}|
. Then there is an open
V_{C}\subset({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}\setminus{\mathscr{B}}_{v(a)})
with complement of codimension at least
2
which has the following properties.

(1)

If
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in V_{C}
the restriction
{\mathscr{F}}_{|C}
is slope semistable.

(2)

The map
m_{C}\colon V_{C}\to U_{C}(2a^{2},{\mathscr{O}}_{C}(D))
to the moduli space
U_{C}(2a^{2},{\mathscr{O}}_{C}(D))
(of slope semistable rank-
2a^{2}
vector bundles on

C
with determinant isomorphic to
{\mathscr{O}}_{C}(D)
) defined by
[{\mathscr{F}}]\mapsto[{\mathscr{F}}_{|C}]
is injective.

We claim that

(6.4.4)

-m_{C}^{*}c_{1}(K_{U_{C}(2a^{2},{\mathscr{O}}_{C}(D))})=4a^{2}\theta_{v(a)}(\beta+(\beta,D)\eta_{S}/2a)_{V_{C}}.

To prove this, let
{\mathscr{H}}\coloneq{\mathscr{F}}_{|C\times V_{C}}
, where
{\mathscr{F}}
is a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. Let
p_{C},p_{V_{C}}
and

q_{S},q_{{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}}
be the projections of
C\times V_{C}
and
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
to first and second factors respectively. The equality in (
6.4.4
) follows from the equation below (given by the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch Theorem):

c_{1}\left(R^{1}p_{V_{C},*}End^{0}({\mathscr{E}})\right)=p_{V_{C},*}\left(\Delta({\mathscr{H}})\right)=\\
=q_{{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)},*}\left(\Delta({\mathscr{F}})\cdot q_{S}^{*}\beta\right)_{|V_{C}}=4a^{2}\theta_{v(a)}\left(\beta+(\beta,D)\eta_{S}/2a\right)_{|V_{C}}.

(Note that
{\mathscr{F}}_{|C}
is simple for every
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in U_{C}
because every sheaf parametrized by
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
is simple and
C\in|Nh_{S}|
for
N
very large.)
Since
U_{C}(2a^{2},{\mathscr{O}}_{C}(D))
is a Fano variety, it follows from Items(1), (2) and (
6.4.4
) that
\theta_{v(a)}(\beta+(\beta,D)\eta_{S}/2a)
is big.
Nefness follows from a more refined analysis.

Since
\theta_{v(a)}(h_{S}+(h_{s},D)\eta_{S}/2a)
is big and nef, the equation in (
6.4.3
) shows that
\varphi_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\bm{\mu}(h_{S})-\epsilon\delta)
is ample for
\epsilon>0
small enough. The corollary follows by openness of the Kähler cone.
∎

The proof of Proposition
6.7
is in Subsection
6.4.4
.

6.4.2.
First step

Hypotheses as in Proposition
6.7
. Set
{\mathscr{M}}\coloneq{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
. Let
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
be the pull-back of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
to
S\times{\mathscr{M}}
, and
let
{\mathbb{E}}_{2}
be a universal sheaf on
S\times{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}
.
Then we have the sheaf
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})\coloneq{\mathscr{G}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2})
on
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}
(see
[
OG26
, Subsect. 2.1]
),
such that for
[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\in{\mathscr{M}}
we have

{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})_{|S^{[2]}\times\{[{\mathscr{E}}_{2}]\}}\cong{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2}).

In the present Subsubsection we prove the following result.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let hypotheses be as in Proposition
6.7
. Let
\gamma\in H^{2}(S^{[2]})
. Then

(6.4.5)

\lambda_{{\bf w}}(\gamma)=q_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\Delta({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}}))\cdot q_{S^{[2]}}^{*}({\mathbf{D}}(\gamma))\right]-16a^{4}\langle\gamma,\delta\rangle\theta_{v_{2}}(v_{1}),

where
q_{S^{[2]}},q_{{\mathscr{M}}}
are the projections of
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}
to
S^{[2]}
,
{\mathscr{M}}
, and
\langle-,-\rangle
is the BBF bilinear symmetric form of
S^{[2]}
.

We prove the result above after a series of preliminary computations.

Let
{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}\coloneq{\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{2}
(Note: if
a=1
then
{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}={\mathscr{M}}
). The restriction of
\widehat{f}
to

\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}\setminus(\widehat{E}^{2}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{a})
(if
a=1
the latter is
\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}
) is an isomorphism

(6.4.6)

\widehat{{\mathscr{M}}}\setminus(\widehat{E}^{2}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}^{a})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}

mapping
\widehat{E}^{1}\setminus\widehat{E}^{2}
isomorphically to
{\mathscr{D}}^{1}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{2}
. In the following we identify
{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}
with the left-hand side of (
6.4.6
), and
{\mathscr{D}}^{1}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}^{2}
with
\widehat{E}^{1}\setminus\widehat{E}^{2}
. Let

{\mathbb{G}}^{{\dagger}}\coloneq{\mathbb{G}}(0)_{|S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}}
, and let
{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})^{{\dagger}}\coloneq{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})_{|S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}}
. The restriction
to
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}
of the exact sequence in (
5.5.14
) reads

(6.4.7)

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{G}}^{{\dagger}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})^{{\dagger}}\longrightarrow\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}\right)\longrightarrow 0,

where
\iota^{{\dagger}}\colon S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}}\hookrightarrow S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}
is the inclusion, and
{\mathscr{L}}
is a line-bundle on

{\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}}
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let hypotheses be as in Proposition
6.7
. Let
\gamma\in H^{2}(S^{[2]})
. Then

(6.4.8)

\lambda_{{\bf w}}(\gamma)_{|{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}}=p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}},*}\left[\Delta({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})^{{\dagger}})\cdot p_{S^{[2]}}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]-16a^{4}\langle\gamma,\delta\rangle\cl({\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}}),

where
p_{S^{[2]}},p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}}
are the projections of
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}
to
S^{[2]}
,
{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}
respectively.

Proof.

Let
r_{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}}}
be the projection of
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}}
to the second factor.
Then (use Lemma
6.1
and
[
O’G22a
, Etns (5.4.2)-(5.4.3)]
)
modulo
H^{>4}(S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}})
we have

\ch(\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}))=4a^{2}p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}}^{*}\cl({\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}})+\\
+p_{S^{[2]}}^{*}(2a{\bm{\mu}}(D)-a(2a+1)\delta)\cdot p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}}^{*}\cl({\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}})+4a^{2}\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}\left(r^{*}_{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}}}c_{1}({\mathscr{L}})\right)-2a^{2}p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}}^{*}\cl({\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}})^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}},*}\left[\ch_{2}(\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}))\cdot p_{S^{[2]}}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\gamma,2a{\bm{\mu}}(D)-a(2a+1)\delta\rangle\cl({\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}})

\displaystyle p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}},*}\left[\ch_{1}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})^{{\dagger}})\cdot\ch_{1}(\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}))\cdot p_{S^{[2]}}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\gamma,16a^{4}{\bm{\mu}}(D)-16a^{5}\delta\rangle\cl({\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}})

\displaystyle p_{{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}},*}\left[\ch_{1}(\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}))^{2}\cdot p_{S^{[2]}}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

The lemma follows from the formulae above and the equality

\Delta({\mathbb{G}}^{{\dagger}})=\Delta({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})^{{\dagger}})+16a^{3}\ch_{2}(\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}))-\\
-2\ch_{1}({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})^{{\dagger}})\cdot\ch_{1}(\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}))+\ch_{1}(\iota^{{\dagger}}_{*}({\mathscr{E}}_{1}[2]^{-}\boxtimes{\mathscr{L}}))^{2}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
6.10
.

By (
6.1.7
) we have
\cl({\mathscr{D}}^{{\dagger}})=\theta_{v_{2}}(v_{1})
. Thus by Lemma
6.11
the restrictions to

{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}
of the two sides of (
6.4.5
) are equal. Since
{\mathscr{M}}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}}
has codimension
4
in
{\mathscr{M}}
(or is empty if
a=1
) the restriction map
H^{2}({\mathscr{M}})\to H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}^{{\dagger}})
is an isomorphism. It follows that the two sides of (
6.4.5
) are equal.
∎

6.4.3.
Passing to
X(S)

Hypotheses and notation are as in Subsubsection
6.4.2
. The goal of the present subsubsection is to prove the following result.

Proposition 6.12
.

Let hypotheses be as in Proposition
6.7
. Let
\gamma\in H^{2}(S^{[2]})
. Then

q_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\Delta({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}}))\cdot q_{S^{[2]}}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]=\begin{cases}16a^{3}\theta_{v_{2}}(2a\beta+(\beta,D)\eta_{S}),&\text{if $\gamma=\bm{\mu}(\beta)$},\\
0&\text{if $\gamma=\delta$},\end{cases}

where
q_{S^{[2]}},q_{{\mathscr{M}}}
are the projections of
S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}
to
S^{[2]}
,
{\mathscr{M}}
respectively,
\beta\in H^{2}(S)
,
\delta
is as in (
6.1.5
),
(-,-)
is the intersection product on
H^{2}(S)
.

The proof is at the end of the subsubsection.

We pass to
X(S)
, see the commutative diagram in (
4.2.12
). We adopt the notation introduced therein.
Let
\overline{q}_{X(S)},\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}}}
be the projections of
X(S)\times{\mathscr{M}}
to first and second factor respectively.
Recall that
\rho\colon X(S)\to S^{[2]}
is a double cover. Let
{\bm{\rho}}\colon X(S)\times{\mathscr{M}}\to S^{[2]}\times{\mathscr{M}}
be given by

{\bm{\rho}}\coloneq\rho\times\Id
.

Claim 6.13
.

Let hypotheses be as in Proposition
6.7
. Let
\gamma\in H^{2}(S^{[2]})
. Then

(6.4.9)

q_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\Delta({\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}}))\cdot q_{S^{[2]}}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]=\frac{1}{2}\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\Delta({\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}}))\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right].

Proof.

This holds because
\rho
has degree
2
.
∎

The pull-back
{\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})
is described as follows. For
i\in\{1,2\}
let
{\bm{\tau}}_{i}\colon X(S)\times{\mathscr{M}}\to S\times{\mathscr{M}}
be given by
{\bm{\tau}}_{i}=\tau_{i}\times\Id
.
Let

(6.4.10)

{\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2})\coloneq{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}_{1}\otimes{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\oplus{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\otimes{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.

Let
E\subset X
be the exceptional divisor of the blow-up (of the diagonal)
\tau\colon X(S)\to S^{2}
. There is a
morphism

{\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2})
which is an isomorphism
away from
E
. Let
\epsilon\colon E\to S
be the restriction of
\tau
to
E
(the image is the diagonal of
S^{2}
, which we identify with
S
), and let

{\bm{\epsilon}}\colon E\times{\mathscr{M}}\to S\times{\mathscr{M}}
be given by
{\bm{\epsilon}}=\epsilon\times\Id
. We let
{\mathscr{R}}\coloneq{\bm{\epsilon}}^{*}({\mathbb{E}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbb{E}}_{2})
.
Then
{\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})
fits into the exact sequence

(6.4.11)

0\longrightarrow{\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2})\overset{{\bm{\varphi}}}{\longrightarrow}{\bm{\iota}}_{*}{\mathscr{R}}\longrightarrow 0,

where

{\bm{\iota}}\colon E\times{\mathscr{M}}\hookrightarrow X\times{\mathscr{M}}
is the inclusion, and
{\bm{\varphi}}(s_{1},s_{2})\coloneq(s_{1|E\times{\mathscr{M}}}-s_{2|E\times{\mathscr{M}}})
for the components
s_{1},s_{2}
are of a (local) section with respect to the direct sum decomposition in (
6.4.10
).
Then (use Lemma
6.1
)
modulo
H^{>4}(X(S)\times{\mathscr{M}})

\ch({\bm{\iota}}_{*}{\mathscr{R}})=4a^{3}\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e)+\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e)\cdot\left(2a^{2}{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}c_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{1})+2a{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}c_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\right)-2a^{3}\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e^{2}).

Since
\ch({\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}}))=\ch({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))-\ch({\bm{\iota}}_{*}{\mathscr{R}})
, the equation above gives that

(6.4.12)

\Delta({\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}}))=\Delta({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))+\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e)\cdot\left(32a^{5}{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}c_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{1})+32a^{4}{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}c_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\right)-\\
-16a^{6}\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e^{2})-8a^{3}\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e)\cdot c_{1}({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2})).

Claim 6.14
.

Let hypotheses be as in Proposition
6.7
. Let
\gamma\in H^{2}(S^{[2]})
. Then

\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\Delta({\bm{\rho}}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}({\mathscr{M}}))\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]=\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\Delta({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right].

Proof.

This follows from (
6.4.12
) and the formulae (note that
\rho^{*}(\delta)=e
)

\displaystyle\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e)\cdot{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}c_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{1})\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e)\cdot{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}c_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\langle\gamma,\delta\rangle\theta_{{\mathbb{E}}_{2}}(\eta_{S}),

\displaystyle\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e^{2})\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(e)\cdot c_{1}({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}(\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8a\langle\gamma,\delta\rangle\theta_{{\mathbb{E}}_{2}}(\eta_{S}),

where
\eta_{S}\in H^{4}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})
is the orientation class.
∎

Proposition 6.15
.

Let
\gamma\in H^{2}(S^{[2]})
. Then

q_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\Delta({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))\cdot q_{S^{[2]}}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]=\begin{cases}32a^{3}\theta_{v_{2}}(2a\beta+(\beta,D)\eta_{S}),&\text{if $\gamma=\bm{\mu}(\beta)$}.\\
0&\text{if $\gamma=\delta$}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
\overline{p}_{S},\overline{p}_{{\mathscr{M}}}
be the projections of
S\times{\mathscr{M}}
to first and second factor.
We have

\ch_{1}({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))^{2}=4a^{4}\left({\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)^{2}+2{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)\cdot{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)+{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)^{2}\right)+\\
+8a^{3}\left({\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)+{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)\right)\cdot\left({\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})+{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\right)+\\
+4a^{2}\left({\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})^{2}+2{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\cdot{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})+{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})^{2}\right)

\ch_{2}({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))=2a^{2}\left({\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\overline{p}^{*}_{S}\ch_{2}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})+{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\overline{p}^{*}_{S}\ch_{2}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})\right)+\\
+{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)\cdot{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})+{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\cdot{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\cl(D)+\\
+2a\left({\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\ch_{2}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})+{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\ch_{2}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\right).

From the above equalities one gets that

\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\ch_{1}({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))^{2}\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]=\begin{cases}64a^{3}(\beta,D)\theta_{{\mathbb{E}}_{2}}(\eta_{S})&\text{if $\gamma=\bm{\mu}(\beta)$},\\
0&\text{if $\gamma=\delta$}.\end{cases}

\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\ch_{2}({\mathscr{F}}({\mathbb{E}}_{1},{\mathbb{E}}_{2}))\cdot\overline{q}_{X(S)}^{*}\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\gamma)\right]=\begin{cases}2(\beta,D)\theta_{{\mathbb{E}}_{2}}(\eta_{S})-4a\theta_{{\mathbb{E}}_{2}}(\beta),&\text{if $\gamma=\bm{\mu}(\beta)$},\\
0&\text{if $\gamma=\delta$}.\end{cases}

In deriving the above equalities one uses the equalities

\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}({\bm{\mu}}(\beta))=\tau_{1}^{*}\beta\cdot\tau_{2}^{*}\eta_{S}+\tau_{1}^{*}\eta_{S}\cdot\tau_{2}^{*}\beta,\qquad\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\delta)=2e\cdot(\tau_{1}^{*}\eta_{S}+\tau_{2}^{*}\eta_{S}),

\overline{p}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})^{2}\cdot\overline{p}_{S}^{*}\beta\right]=2a(\beta,D)\theta_{{\mathbb{E}}_{2}}(\eta_{S})=\overline{q}_{{\mathscr{M}},*}\left[{\bm{\tau}}_{1}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\cdot{\bm{\tau}}_{2}^{*}\ch_{1}({\mathbb{E}}_{2})\cdot\rho^{*}{\mathbf{D}}(\bm{\mu}(\beta)\right].

The proposition follows from the above equalities.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.12
.

Put together Claims
6.13
,
6.14
, and Proposition
6.15
.
∎

6.4.4.
Proof of Proposition
6.7

Put together Propositions
6.10
and
6.12
.

6.4.5.
Relation between the integral lattices

By Corollary
6.8
we have the isometry

(6.4.13)

\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\colon H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Q}})\longrightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet}).

Here we are concerned with the relation between the cohomology groups with integral coefficients, i.e.
H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})
and

H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet};{\mathbb{Z}})
.
Let

(6.4.14)

H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})_{\widehat{\gamma}_{a}}\coloneq\{\alpha\in H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})\mid(\alpha,\gamma_{a})_{S^{[2]}}\equiv 0\pmod{2a}\},

where
\widehat{\gamma}_{a},\gamma_{a}
are as in (
6.3.1
) and Remark
6.6
respectively. The notation is coherent because the right-hand side of (
6.4.14
) depends only on
\widehat{\gamma}_{a}
.
Then
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
maps
H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})_{\overline{\gamma}_{a}}
to

H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet};{\mathbb{Z}})
, see Proposition
6.7
.
The restriction to
H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})_{\widehat{\gamma}_{a}}
of the BBF quadratic form on
H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})
has discriminant
8a^{2}
.
Since the BBF quadratic form on
H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet};{\mathbb{Z}})
has discriminant
2a^{2}
, it follows that

(6.4.15)

[H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet};{\mathbb{Z}}):\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})_{\widehat{\gamma}_{a}})]=2.

The “missing element” is as follows. There exists
\beta_{0}\in H^{2}(S;{\mathbb{Z}})
such that (see the sentence following (
6.3.7
))

(6.4.16)

(\beta_{0},D)=a(k+2t)-1.

Then

(6.4.17)

\frac{1}{2}\varphi_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}((\bm{\mu}(2\beta_{0}-D)+\delta)=\theta_{v(a)}(a+\beta_{0}+t\eta_{S})\in H^{2}({\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)};{\mathbb{Z}}).

Hence

(6.4.18)

H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet};{\mathbb{Z}})=\Span\left(\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}\left(H^{2}(S^{[2]};{\mathbb{Z}})_{\widehat{\gamma}_{a}}\right),\,\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\bm{\mu}(2\beta_{0}-D)+\delta)\right).

7.
Proofs of the main results

7.1.
Twistor families and projectively hyperholomorphic vector bundles

Let
X
be a HK manifold,
\omega\in{\mathscr{K}}(X)
,
\sigma
a holomorphic symplectic form, and

F(\alpha)_{{\mathbb{C}}}\coloneq\langle\sigma,\overline{\sigma},\omega\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}}
. Let

(7.1.1)

T(\omega)\coloneq\{[\alpha]\in{\mathbb{P}}(F(\alpha)_{{\mathbb{C}}})\mid q_{X}([\alpha])=0\}

be the twistor conic associated to

(X,\omega)
and

f\colon{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\to T(\omega)
be the corresponding twistor family. Thus
f
is a holomorphic map of complex manifolds. The smooth manifold underlying
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)
is uniquely identified with the product of
the smooth manifolds underlying
X
and
T(\omega)
, and the smooth map underlying
f
is the projection.
In particular the space of differential forms of any fiber
X_{t}\coloneq f^{-1}(t)
(an
\omega
-twistor deformation of
X
) is identified with that of
X
.
For

(7.1.2)

t=[x\omega+y\sigma+z\overline{\sigma}]\in T(\omega)

let
\sigma_{t}\coloneq x\omega+y\sigma+z\overline{\sigma}
(determined up to rescaling by
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
), and let

0\not=\omega_{t}\in F(\alpha)_{{\mathbb{C}}}
be real, with cohomology class orthogonal (for the BBF quadratic form) to

\sigma_{t}
(and hence also to
\overline{\sigma}_{t}
), and such that
\{\omega_{t},\sigma_{t}+\overline{\sigma}_{t},i\sigma_{t}-i\overline{\sigma}_{t}\}
defines the same orientation of
F(\alpha)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\coloneq\langle\omega,\sigma+\overline{\sigma},i\sigma-i\overline{\sigma}\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}}
as

\{\omega,\sigma+\overline{\sigma},i\sigma-i\overline{\sigma}\}
(thus
\omega_{t}
is determined up to rescaling by
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
).
Then
\omega_{t}\in{\mathscr{K}}(X_{t})
is the
twistor Kähler class of
X_{t}
, and

(7.1.3)

H^{0}(\Omega^{2}_{X_{t}})=\langle x\sigma+y\overline{\sigma}+z\omega\rangle.

Remark 7.1
.

For
t\in T(\omega)
we have an integral isometry
\Pi_{t}\colon H^{2}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(X_{t})
because
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)
, as smooth manifold, is the product of

X
and
C(\omega)
. Thus we may view the period map of the twistor family as

(7.1.4)

\begin{matrix}T(\omega)&\overset{\wp_{{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)}}{\longrightarrow}&{\mathbb{P}}(H^{2}(X))\\
t&\mapsto&[\Pi_{t}^{*}(\sigma_{t})]\end{matrix}

By (
7.1.3
) the image is equal to
T(\omega)
(here we make no difference between a closed
2
-form and its cohomolgy class) and
\wp_{{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)}(t)=t
for all
t
.

Remark 7.2
.

The twistor conic
T(\omega)
(or the twistor family) is
generic
if

(7.1.5)

F(\omega)_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\bot}\cap H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)=\{0\}.

Equivalently,
\omega_{t}^{\bot}\cap H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X_{t})=\{0\}
for every
t\in T(\omega)
.
If
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\to T(\omega)
is generic then
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X_{t})=\{0\}
for
t
away from a countable subset of
T(\omega)
.

Let
\eta\in H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})
, and let
\alpha=i(\eta)
where
i\colon H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})\to H^{2}(X_{t},{\mathscr{O}}_{X}^{*})
is the natural map. Let
t\in T(\omega)
. Since the twistor family is differentiably a product
\eta
defines a class
\eta_{t}\in H^{2}(X_{t},\mu_{r})
.
Let
\alpha_{t}=i_{t}(\eta_{t})
where
i_{t}\colon H^{2}(X_{t},\mu_{r})\to H^{2}(X_{t},{\mathscr{O}}_{X_{t}}^{*})
is the natural map.
Below is a fundamental result of Verbitsky, see
[
Ver96
, Thm 11.1. Cor. 10.1]
and
[
P21
]
for the twisted version.

Theorem 7.3

(Verbitsky)
.

Let
X
be a HK manifold, with Kähler class
\omega
, and let
\eta\in H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})
.

(1)

Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be an
\alpha
-twisted vector bundle of rank
r
on
X
. If
{\mathscr{E}}
is

\omega
-slope-stable and the discriminant
\Delta(F)
remains a Hodge class on all
\omega
-twistor deformations of
X
then there exists a
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle (locally trivial in the classical topology)

{\mathscr{P}}\to{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)
whose restriction to
X_{0}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathscr{E}})
, or equivalently there exists an
\alpha_{t}
-twisted vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{t}
on
X_{t}
extending
{\mathscr{E}}
.

(2)

Let
t\in T(\omega)
. Then
{\mathscr{E}}_{t}
is
\omega_{t}
-slope-stable.

(3)

Let
t\in T(\omega)
be as in Item (2). The universal deformation space (see
[
MO25
]
) of
{\mathscr{E}}_{t}
is isomorphic to the
universal deformation space of
{\mathscr{E}}
.

7.2.
Families of projective bundles and twistor families

Let
X
be a HK manifold, and
\omega\in{\mathscr{K}}(X)
. Let
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\to T(\omega)
be the twistor family, with
X_{t_{0}}=X
.
Suppose that
{\mathscr{Y}}\to T(\omega)
is a proper holomorphic submersive map, and that
{\mathscr{Q}}\to{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\times_{T(\omega)}{\mathscr{Y}}
is a (holomorphic)
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle. For
t\in T(\omega)
we have the Donaldson-Mukai map

(7.2.1)

H^{2}(X_{t})\overset{\lambda_{t}}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(Y_{t})

given by the formula in (
6.2.2
) with
{\mathscr{P}}
the restriction of
{\mathscr{Q}}
to
X_{t}\times Y_{t}
.

Assumption 7.4
.

With notation as above, suppose the following.

(a)

Y_{t_{0}}
is a HK manifold.

(b)

There exists
c\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}
such that
c\cdot\lambda_{t_{0}}
is a (rational) Hodge isometry.

(c)

\widehat{\omega}\coloneq\lambda_{t_{0}}(\omega)
is a Kähler class of
Y_{t_{0}}
.

Under Assumption
7.4
we have the twistor conic for

Y_{t_{0}}
given by
T(\widehat{\omega})
.
Since
c\cdot\lambda_{t_{0}}
is a (rational) Hodge isometry we have
the isomorphism

(7.2.2)

\begin{matrix}T(\omega)&\overset{u}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}&T(\widehat{\omega})\\
[\alpha]&\mapsto&c\cdot\lambda_{t_{0}}([\alpha]).\end{matrix}

Let
{\mathscr{Z}}={\mathscr{Z}}(\widehat{\omega})\to T(\widehat{\omega})
be the twistor family of
(Y_{t_{0}},\widehat{\omega})
.
Since
u(t_{0})=[c\cdot\widehat{\omega}]
we have
Z_{u(t_{0})}\cong Y_{t_{0}}
. For
t\in T(\omega)
let
\Pi_{t}\colon H^{2}(X_{t_{0}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(X_{t})
,

\Upsilon_{t}\colon H^{2}(Z_{u(t_{0})})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(Z_{u(t)})
be the twistor isomorphisms
(see Remark
7.1
), and let

(7.2.3)

\psi_{t}\colon H^{2}(Z_{u(t)})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(Y_{t})

be
the composition

H^{2}(Z_{u(t)})\xrightarrow{\Upsilon_{t}^{-1}}H^{2}(Z_{u(t_{0})})=H^{2}(Y_{t_{0}})\xrightarrow{(c\cdot\lambda)^{-1}_{t_{0}}}H^{2}(X_{t_{0}})\xrightarrow{\Pi_{t}}H^{2}(X_{t})\xrightarrow{c\cdot\lambda_{t}}H^{2}(Y_{t}).

Claim 7.5
.

Keep Assumption
7.4
. Let
t\in T(\omega)
, and suppose that
Y_{t}
is Kähler (and hence hyperkähler). Then
\psi_{t}
is an isomorphism of integral Hodge structures matching the BBF quadratic forms.

Proof.

The map
\lambda_{t}\circ\Pi_{t}\circ\lambda^{-1}_{t_{0}}\colon H^{2}(Y_{t_{0}})\longrightarrow H^{2}(Y_{t})
is an isomorphism over
{\mathbb{Z}}
matching the BBF quadratic forms because
\lambda_{t},\lambda_{t_{0}}
are defined via the
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle

{\mathscr{Q}}\to{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\times_{T(\omega)}{\mathscr{Y}}
. Since
\Upsilon_{t}
is also an isomorphism over
{\mathbb{Z}}
matching the BBF quadratic forms, it follows that
\psi_{t}
is an isomorphism over
{\mathbb{Z}}
matching the BBF quadratic forms. It remains to prove that
\psi_{t}
is an isomorphism of Hodge structures. Since it is an integral isometry it suffices to show that

(7.2.4)

\psi_{t}(H^{2,0}(Z_{u(t)}))=H^{2,0}(Y_{t}).

Let
t=[x\omega+y\sigma+z\overline{\sigma}]
(notation as in (
7.1.2
)). Then

(7.2.5)

\displaystyle H^{2,0}(X_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle x\omega+y\sigma+z\overline{\sigma}\rangle,

(7.2.6)

\displaystyle H^{2,0}(Z_{u(t)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Upsilon_{t}(xu(\omega)+yu(\sigma)+zu(\overline{\sigma}))\rangle.

Since
c\cdot\lambda_{t}
is an isomorphism of (rational) Hodge structures,

H^{2,0}(Y_{t})=\langle c\cdot\lambda_{t}(x\omega+y\sigma+z\overline{\sigma})\rangle.

The equality in (
7.2.4
) follows.
∎

Let

(7.2.7)

T(\omega)_{0}\coloneq\{t\in T(\omega)\mid\text{$Y_{t}$ is HK and $\exists f_{t}\colon Y_{t}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z_{u(t)}$ s.t.\penalty\ $H^{2}(f_{t})=\psi_{t}$}\}.

Note
that by construction
t_{0}\in T(\omega)_{0}
. By openness of Kählerianity and
local Torelli
T(\omega)_{0}
is open in

T(\omega)
.

Proposition 7.6
.

Keep Assumption
7.4
. Let
t_{1}\in\partial T(\omega)_{0}=\overline{T(\omega)}_{0}\setminus T(\omega)_{0}
.
Then

(1)

H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X_{t_{1}})\not=\{0\}
, and

(2)

\lambda_{t_{1}}(\omega_{t_{1}})
is not a Kähler class of
Y_{t_{1}}
.

Proof.

The dimension of the space of global holomorphic
2
-forms of
Y_{t}
is equal to
1
for
t\in T(\omega)_{0}
. By upper-semicontinuity of direct images of analytic coherent sheaves it follows that there exists
0\not=\sigma(t_{1})\in H^{0}(Y_{t_{1}},\Omega^{2}_{Y_{t_{1}}})
extending to a global holomorphic symplectic form on
Y_{t}
for
t\in T(\omega)_{0}
. It follows that

\sigma(t_{1})
is also a symplectic form. In particular
Y_{t_{1}}
has the volume-form
(\sigma(t_{1})\wedge\overline{\sigma}(t_{1}))^{2n}
where

2n=\dim Y_{t}
.
The argument for the next step is similar to those which appear (for example) in
[
H99
, Sect. 4]
.
The graph of the isomorphism
Y_{t_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z_{t_{0}}
extends to the graph
\Gamma(f_{t})
of
f_{t}
for all
t\in T(\omega)_{0}
. The volume of
\Gamma(f_{t})
is uniformly bounded with respect to the product of the volumes forms on
Y_{t}
and
Z_{u(t)}
given by

(\sigma(t)\wedge\overline{\sigma}(t))^{2n}
,
(\tau(t)\wedge\overline{\tau}(t))^{2n}
respectively where
\sigma(t)
is as above, and
\tau(t)
is a symplectic form on
Z_{u(t)}
such that
q_{Z_{u(t)}}(\tau(t),\overline{\tau}(t))=1
.
Hence
\Gamma(f_{t})
specializes, for
t\mapsto t_{1}
to an (effective) analytic cycle
\Gamma
on
Y_{t_{1}}\times Z_{u(t_{1})}
, of pure dimension equal to
\dim Y_{t}=\dim Z_{u(t)}
, given by

(7.2.8)

\Gamma=\Gamma_{0}+\sum_{i}m_{i}W_{i},

where
\Gamma_{0}
is the graph of a bimeromorphic map

g\colon Y_{t_{1}}\dashrightarrow Z_{u(t_{1})}
, and each
W_{i}
is mapped by the two projections to proper closed subsets of

Y_{t_{1}}
,
Z_{u(t_{1})}
.

(1): Suppose that
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X_{t_{1}})=\{0\}
. Then
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(Z_{u(t_{1})})=\{0\}
because the composition

\Pi_{t_{1}}\circ(c\cdot\lambda_{t_{0}})^{-1}\circ\Upsilon_{t_{1}}\colon H^{2}(Z_{u(t_{1})})\to H^{2}(X_{t_{1}})
is an isomorphism of rational Hodge structures. Hence

Z_{u(t_{1})}
contains no prime divisors, and since
g
is an isomorphism away from closed subsets of codimension at least
2
, the analogous statement holds for
Y_{t_{1}}
.
Hence each
W_{i}
is mapped by the projections to
Z_{u(t_{1})}
,
Y_{t_{1}}
to (closed) subsets of codimensions at least
2
. It follows that
g^{*}\colon H^{2}(Z_{u(t_{1})})\to H^{2}(Y_{t_{1}})
is equal to

\psi_{t_{1}}
. Moreover
g
is a regular (holomorphic) map because
Z_{u(t_{1})}
contains no curves. Since
g
is an isomorphism and

g^{*}=\psi_{t_{1}}
, we get that
t_{1}\in T(\omega)_{0}
, contradiction.

(2): Assume that
\lambda_{t_{1}}(\omega_{t_{1}})
is a Kähler class of
Y_{t_{1}}
, in particular the latter is Kähler and hence hyperkähler.
The proof of
[
H03
, Thm. 2.5]
gives that each
W_{i}
is mapped by the two projections to proper closed subsets of

Y_{t_{1}}
,
Z_{u(t_{1})}
of codimension at least
2
. It follows that
g^{*}\colon H^{2}(Z_{u(t_{1})})\to H^{2}(Y_{t_{1}})
is equal to

\psi_{t_{1}}
. Since
\psi_{t_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}_{u(t_{1})})=c\cdot\lambda_{t_{1}}(\omega_{t_{1}})
with
c>0
we get that

g^{*}(\widehat{\omega}_{u(t_{1})})
(here
\widehat{\omega}_{u(t_{1})}
is the twistor Kähler class of
Z_{u(t_{1})}
) is a Kähler class on
Y_{t}
, and hence
g
is an isomorphism by Prop. 2.1 loc. cit.
Thus
t_{1}\in T(\omega)_{0}
because
g^{*}=\psi_{t_{1}}
, contradiction.
∎

Corollary 7.7
.

Keep Assumption
7.4
. Let
t\in T(\omega)
. If
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X_{t})=\{0\}
, or
\lambda_{t}(\omega_{t})\in{\mathscr{K}}(Y_{t})
,
then
t\in T(\omega)
, and in particular
Y_{t}
is isomorphic to
Z_{u(t)}
.

Remark 7.8
.

In the proof of Proposition
7.6
we have shown that, for every
t\in T(\omega)
,

Y_{t}
is a holomorphic symplectic manifold bimeromorphic to
Z_{u(t)}
.

7.3.
The proof of Theorem
1.1

Let
\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}
be as in (
6.3.3
). If
[{\mathscr{F}}]\in M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}^{\bullet}
then by (
2.3.5
) and
[
OG26
, Ex. 2.12]
we have

(7.3.1)

{\mathsf{a}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathscr{F}})={\mathsf{a}}(a)\coloneq 2^{16}\cdot 5\cdot a^{18}.

By hypothesis the Kähler class
\omega
of
X
belongs to an open
{\mathsf{a}}(a)
-chamber of
{\mathscr{K}}(X)
. Replacing
\omega
by a Kähler class belonging to the same open
{\mathsf{a}}(a)
-chamber (see Proposition
2.18
) we may assume that

(7.3.2)

\omega^{\bot}\cap H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)=\{0\}.

Let
(S,h_{S})
be a polarized
K3
surface as in Theorem
5.1
, and let
\widetilde{\omega}\in{\mathscr{K}}(S^{[2]})

which belongs to an open
{\mathsf{a}}(a)
-chamber
containing
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
in its closure, and such that

(7.3.3)

\widetilde{\omega}^{\bot}\cap H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(S^{[2]})=\{0\}.

By Theorem
5.1
(and Proposition
2.18
)

M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widetilde{\omega})
contains an irreducible component
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widetilde{\omega})^{\bullet}
whose normalization
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widetilde{\omega})^{\bullet}
is isomorphic to the HK manifold
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}(S,h_{S})
.

By
[
H12
, Props. 3.7, 5.4]
there exist a finite sequence of generic twistor families
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega_{i})\to T(\omega_{i})
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, points
t_{i}\in T(\omega_{i})
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
and
s_{j}\in T(\omega_{j})

j\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
such that the following hold.

(A)

{\mathscr{X}}(\omega_{1})\to T(\omega_{1})
is the twistor family associated to
(S^{[2]},\widetilde{\omega})
,

(B)

{\mathscr{X}}(\omega_{n})\to T(\omega_{n})
is the twistor family associated to
(X,\omega)
,

(C)

For
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
the twistor fibers
X(\omega_{i})_{t_{i}}
and
X(\omega_{i+1})_{s_{i+1}}
are isomorphic, and they have trivial Picard group.

Parallel transport of
\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}
via the chain of twistor families above gives a mock Mukai vector
\overline{{\bf w}}^{\prime}_{a}
for
X
. The first (and main) step is to prove that the statement of Theorem
1.1
holds for
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}^{\prime}_{a}}(X,\omega)
.

Applying Theorem
7.3
to
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widetilde{\omega})^{\bullet}
and the first twistor family we get a holomorphic map

(7.3.4)

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}({\mathscr{X}}(\omega_{1}))^{\bullet}\to T(\omega_{1})

with fiber over
t
isomorphic to the normalization

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}(t)}({\mathscr{X}}(\omega_{1})_{t})^{\bullet}
of the component of the moduli space
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}(t)}({\mathscr{X}}(\omega_{1})_{t})

corresponding to
M_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widetilde{\omega})^{\bullet}
via Theorem
7.3
.
We wish to apply Corollary
7.7
to
{\mathscr{Y}}\to T(\omega_{1})
given by (
7.3.4
), and
{\mathscr{Q}}
the universal bundle of projective spaces. We need to check that Assumption
7.4
holds. Item (a) holds because

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widetilde{\omega})^{\bullet}
is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{M}}_{v(a)}(S,h_{S})
, which is a HK manifold of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
.
Item (b) holds with
\lambda_{t_{0}}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
and
c=32^{-1}\cdot a^{-4}
by Corollary
6.8
. We claim that for suitable choices of
\widetilde{\omega}
also Item (c) holds.
Let
L
be an ample class on
S^{[2]}

which belongs to an open
{\mathsf{a}}(a)
-chamber
containing
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
in its closure. Then
\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(c_{1}(L))
is ample by Proposition
6.5
.
This is not enough because (
7.3.3
) cannot hold with

\widetilde{\omega}=c_{1}(L)
. However, since
\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
is a Hodge isometry
\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\widetilde{\omega})
is a Kähler class for
\widetilde{\omega}
close enough to
c_{1}(L)
. Thus we may choose
\widetilde{\omega}
as above and such that

\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\widetilde{\omega})
is a Kähler class. Then Assumption
7.4
holds. By Item (C) above it follows that

X(\widetilde{\omega})_{t_{1}}\cong X(\omega_{2})_{s_{2}}
is a HK manifold of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
with trivial Picard group. By Corollary
7.7
we may iterate the above argument up to the last twistor family
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega_{n})\to T(\omega_{n})
,
i.e.
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\to T(\omega)
. From this Item (1) of Theorem
1.1
follows at once, see Remark
7.8
. Item (2) if
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)=\{0\}
follows from Corollary
7.7
. If
X
is projective with ample line bundle
L
, arguing as above we get that if

\omega
is close enough to
c_{1}(L)
then
\lambda_{\overline{{\bf w}}^{\prime}_{a}}(\omega)
is a Kähler class. Thus Item (2) follows from Corollary
7.7
also in this case.

We have proved that Items (1) and (2) of Theorem
1.1
hold for a certain choice of
mock Mukai vector
(8a^{3},\overline{\gamma}^{*}_{a},4a^{6}c_{2}(X)/3)
.
Recall that

\overline{\gamma}^{*}_{a}=[4a^{2}\widehat{\gamma}^{*}_{a}]
where
\widehat{\gamma}^{*}_{a}=[\gamma_{a}]\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})/2aH^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})
and (
1.1.8
) holds.
By the divisibility condition in (
1.1.8
) it follows that there exists
\gamma_{a}\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
such that

\overline{\gamma}^{*}_{a}=[4a^{2}\gamma_{a}]
and

(7.3.5)

\divisore(\gamma_{a})=1,\qquad q_{X}(\gamma_{a})=2a^{2}-2.

The monodromy group
\Mon^{2}(X)<O(H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}}))
is the index-
2
subgroup of positive spinor norm orthogonal transfomations, and hence it acts transitively on the set of
\gamma_{a}\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
such that (
7.3.5
) holds. From this one gets the validity
of Items (1) and (2) of Theorem
1.1
for any
\overline{\gamma}_{a}
by repeating the argument above with twistor families whose twistor conics (in the period space) connect two markings
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
of
H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})
such that
\phi(\gamma^{*}_{a})=\phi^{\prime}(\gamma_{a})
.

Item (3) of Theorem
1.1
follows from Item (1) and Corollary
6.8
.

∎

7.4.
The proof of Theorem
1.3

Let hypotheses and notation be as in Subsection
5.1
. By Corollary
6.9
there exists a Kähler class

\omega\in{\mathscr{K}}(S^{[2]})
such that the following hold:

(a)

\omega
belongs to an open
{\mathsf{a}}(a)
-chamber which contains
\bm{\mu}(h_{S})
in its closure,

(b)

\omega^{\bot}\cap H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)=\{0\}
, and

(c)

\widetilde{\omega}\coloneq\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(\omega)\in{\mathscr{K}}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widehat{\omega})^{\bullet})
.

By
[
H12
, Props. 3.7, 5.4]
there exist generic Twistor families
{\mathscr{Z}}(\widetilde{\omega}(i))\to T(\widetilde{\omega}(i))
of HK manifolds of Type
K3^{[a^{2}+1]}
, for
i\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
, with the following properties:

(1)

There exists
x_{0}\in T(\widetilde{\omega}(0))
such that
Z(\widetilde{\omega}(0))_{x_{0}}\cong\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(S^{[2]},\widehat{\omega})^{\bullet}
,

\widetilde{\omega}(0)=\widetilde{\omega}
.

(2)

There exists
x_{m}\in T(\widetilde{\omega}(m))
such that
Z(\widetilde{\omega}(m))_{x_{m}}\cong W
.

(3)

For all
i\in\{0,\ldots,m-1\}
there exist
s_{i}\in T(\widetilde{\omega}(i))
,
\overline{s}_{i+1}\in T(\widetilde{\omega}(i+1))
with

(7.4.1)

Z(\widetilde{\omega}(i))_{s_{i}}\cong Z(\widetilde{\omega}(i+1))_{\overline{s}_{i+1}},\qquad H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(Z(\widetilde{\omega}(i))_{s_{i}})=\{0\}.

Next we define twistor families of HK manifolds of Type
K3^{[2]}
via
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
and the isometries obtained from
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
via parallel transport.
Let
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\to T(\omega)
be the twistor family of HK manifolds of Type
K3^{[2]}
associated to
\omega
.
Then we have the isomorphism
u\colon T(\omega)\xrightarrow{\sim}T(\widetilde{\omega}(0))
defined by
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}
: let

t_{0}\coloneq u^{-1}(s_{0})
.
By Corollary
7.7
(and the second equality in (
7.4.1
)) applied to the family
{\mathscr{Y}}\to T(\omega)
with fiber the moduli space
\widetilde{M}(X(\omega)_{t},\omega_{t})^{\bullet}
over
t

we have
t_{0}\in T(\omega)_{0}
, in particular there exists an isomorphism

(7.4.2)

f_{t_{0}}\colon\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a,t_{0}}}(X(\omega)_{t_{0}},\omega_{t_{0}})^{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z(\widetilde{\omega}(0))_{s_{0}}

By (
7.4.1
) the Kähler cone of
Z(\widetilde{\omega}(0))_{s_{0}}
(and of
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a,t_{0}}}(X(\widetilde{\omega}(0))_{t_{0}},\widetilde{\omega}(0)_{t_{0}})^{\bullet}
) is one connected component of the cone of positive (BBF) square elements of
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
.
Since
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a,t_{0}}}
is an isometry over
{\mathbb{Q}}
it follows that also the Kähler cone of
X(\omega)_{t_{0}}

is one connected component of the cone of positive (BBF) square elements of
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and also that

(7.4.3)

\widetilde{\varphi}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a,t_{0}}}({\mathscr{K}}(X(\omega)_{t_{0}}))={\mathscr{K}}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}(t_{0})}(X(\omega)_{t_{0}}).

Now
Z(\widetilde{\omega}(1))_{u_{1}}\cong Z(\widetilde{\omega}(0))_{s_{0}}
, hence the twistor family
{\mathscr{Z}}(\omega(1))
is given by a Kähler class in

Z(\widetilde{\omega}(0))_{s_{0}}
, which corresponds to a Kähler class
\omega(1)
on
X(\omega)_{t_{0}}
by (
7.4.3
).
Iterating we get generic Twistor families

{\mathscr{X}}(\omega(i))\to T(\omega(i))
of HK manifolds of Type
K3^{[2]}
, for
i\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
, and isomorphisms
u(i)\colon T(\omega(i))\xrightarrow{\sim}T(\widetilde{\omega}(i))
of conics with
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega(0))\to T(\omega(0))
equal to
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega)\to T(\omega)
and
u(0)=u
.
Let
\overline{{\bf w}}(i)_{t}
be the mock Mukai vector for
X(\omega(i))_{t}
obtained by parallel transport from
\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
, and

t_{i}\coloneq u(i)^{-1}(s_{i})
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,m-1\}
.
By Corollary
7.7

we have
t_{i}\in T(\omega(i))_{0}
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,m-1\}
, and hence

(7.4.4)

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}(i)_{t_{i}}}(X(\omega(i))_{t_{i}},\omega(i)_{t_{i}})^{\bullet}\cong Z(\omega(i))_{\overline{s}_{i}},\qquad i\in\{0,\ldots,m\}.

Now apply Corollary
7.7
and Remark
7.8
to the twistor family
{\mathscr{X}}(\omega(m))\to T(\omega(m))

and the corresponding family
{\mathscr{Y}}\to T(\omega(m))
with fiber
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}(m)_{t}}(X(\omega(m))_{t},\omega(m)_{t})^{\bullet}
over
t
.
Let
t_{*}\coloneq u(m)^{-1}(x_{m})
(recall that
Z(\widetilde{\omega}(m))_{x_{m}}\cong W
).
We get that
W
is bimeromorphic to

\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}(m)_{t_{*}}}(X(\omega(m))_{t_{*}},\omega(m)_{t_{*}})^{\bullet}
, and that they are isomorphic if

H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X(\omega(m))_{t_{*}})=\{0\}
, i.e. if
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(W)=\{0\}
.

If
W
is projective we argue differently. Suppose first that
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(W)={\mathbb{Z}}\alpha
with
q_{W}(\alpha)>0
. We have proved that there exist a HK manifold
X
of Type
K3^{[2]}
and a Kähler class
\omega
on
X
for which
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}
is holomorphic symplectic manifold and there is a bimeromorphic map

(7.4.5)

g\colon W\dashrightarrow\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}.

The composition of the rational isogeny
H^{2}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet})

in (
1.1.12
) and the rational isogeny
H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(W)
is
rational isogeny
H^{2}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(W)
. It follows that
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)={\mathbb{Z}}h
where
q_{X}(h)>0
and
h,\omega
belong to the same connected component of the cone of classes in
H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{R}}}(X)
of positive square. Thus
h
is ample, and moreover there is a single
open
{\mathsf{a}}(a)
-chamber in
{\mathscr{K}}(X)
. Thus
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,\omega)^{\bullet}\cong\widetilde{M}(X,h)^{\bullet}
, in particular it is projective (and hence hyperkähler). Since it has Picard number
1
, the map
g
in (
7.4.5
) is an isomorphism.
This proves the last statement in Theorem
1.3
if
W
is projective with cyclic Picard group.

Now we prove the last statement in Theorem
1.3
for an arbitrary projective
W
. There exist a projective family

f\colon{\mathscr{W}}\to T
over a smooth connected curve
T
and
t_{0},t_{1}\in T
such that
W_{t_{0}}\coloneq f^{-1}(t_{0})
has cyclic Picard group,
W\cong W_{t_{1}}\coloneq f^{-1}(t_{1})
. We have proved that
W_{t_{0}}\cong\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{0},h_{0})^{\bullet}
for
(X_{0},h_{0})
a suitable polarized HK manifold of Type
K3^{[2]}
.
Starting from the rational isogeny
in (
1.1.12
) we associate to
f\colon{\mathscr{W}}\to T
a projective family

g\colon{\mathscr{X}}\to T
of HK manifolds of Type
K3^{[2]}
, with relative polarization
{\mathscr{H}}
, such that
(X_{0},h_{0})\cong(g^{-1}(t_{0}),H_{|g^{-1}(t_{0})})
and such that the following holds. Let

p\colon\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{H}})^{\bullet}\to T
be the relative moduli space with fiber
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{t},h_{t})^{\bullet}
over
t
(to be precise we might need to first do a base change because a priori
\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{t},h_{t})^{\bullet}
is “not unique”). Then there exist an open subset
U\subset T
containing
t_{0}
and an isomorphism

(7.4.6)

\textstyle{f^{-1}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{f_{|\ldots}}
\textstyle{p^{-1}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{|\ldots}}
\textstyle{U}

Thus we have an effective limit cycle
\Gamma
on
W_{t_{1}}\times\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{t_{1}},h_{t_{1}})^{\bullet}
of pure dimension
2a^{2}+2
.
Write
\Gamma=\Gamma_{0}+\sum_{i}m_{i}Z_{i}
where
\Gamma_{0}
is the graph of a birational map
W_{t_{1}}\dashrightarrow\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{t_{1}},h_{t_{1}})^{\bullet}
and each of the
Z_{i}
’s maps to a proper closed subset of the two factors
W_{t_{1}}\times\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{t_{1}},h_{t_{1}})^{\bullet}
. We have that
\Gamma^{*}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(h_{t_{1}})
is an ample class on
W_{t_{1}}
, an hence arguing as in the proof of Proposition
7.6
(i.e. quoting the proof of
[
H03
, Thm. 2.5]
) we get that

\Gamma_{0}
is the graph of an isomorphism.
∎

7.5.
The proof of Theorem
1.7

Lemma 7.9
.

Let hypotheses be as in Theorem
1.1
with
X
projective and
\omega=h
an ample class, so that
\widetilde{M}_{a}\coloneq\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X,h)^{\bullet}
is projective. Then the following hold:

(1)

There exists
Z\in\CH^{4}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(X\times\widetilde{M}_{a})
such that
\cl(Z)\in H^{6}(X)\otimes H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{a})\cong H^{2}(X)^{\vee}\otimes H^{2}(M_{a})
equals the isogeny in (
1.1.12
).

(2)

There exists
P\in\CH^{2a^{2}+2}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(\widetilde{M}_{a}\times X)
such that
\cl(P)\in H^{4a^{2}+2}(\widetilde{M}_{a})\otimes H^{2}(X)\cong H^{2}(\widetilde{M}_{a})^{\vee}\otimes H^{2}(X)
equals the inverse of the isogeny in (
1.1.12
).

Proof.

Since
X
and
\widetilde{M}_{a}
are projective the rational class
32^{-1}\cdot a^{-4}\cdot c_{2}(\mathfrak{g}(\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\bullet}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}))
lifts to a class

Z_{0}\in\CH^{2}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(X\times\widetilde{M}_{a})
whose cohomology class has Künneth components
(4,0)
,
(2,2)
and
(0,4)
(
X
and
\widetilde{M}_{a}
are simply connected). Choose
(x_{0},m_{0})\in X\times\widetilde{M}_{a}
, and let
i_{m_{0}}\colon X\hookrightarrow X\times\widetilde{M}_{a}
,
i_{x_{0}}\colon\widetilde{M}_{a}\hookrightarrow X\times\widetilde{M}_{a}
be given by
i_{m_{0}}(x)\coloneq(x,m_{0})
,
i_{x_{0}}(m)\coloneq(x_{0},m)
.
The algebraic cycle
\overline{Z}_{0}\coloneq Z_{0}-i_{m_{0},*}(i_{m_{0}}^{*}Z_{0})-i_{x_{0},*}(i_{x_{0}}^{*}Z_{0})
has
Künneth
(2,2)
component equal to that of
Z_{0}
and no other Künneth
(2,2)
component. Let

\widetilde{Z}_{0}\coloneq\overline{Z}_{0}\cdot p_{X}^{*}(h_{X}^{2})\in\CH^{4}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(X\times\widetilde{M}_{a})
where

p_{X}\colon X\times\widetilde{M}_{a}\to\widetilde{M}_{a}
is the projection and

h_{X}\in\CH^{1}(X)
is an ample divisor class. The identity
\int_{X}a\cdot b\cdot\cl(h_{X})^{2}=(a,b)_{X}\cdot q_{X}(h_{X})+2(a,h_{X})_{X}\cdot(b,h_{X})_{X}
, valid for any
a,b\in H^{2}(X)
, gives that
the action of
\cl(\widetilde{Z}_{0})
on

c\in H^{2}(X)
is given by

(7.5.1)

\cl(\widetilde{Z}_{0})(c)=q_{X}(h_{X})\cdot\cl(Z_{0})(c)+2(c,h_{X})_{X}\cdot\cl(Z_{0})(h_{X}).

Let
h_{\widetilde{M}_{a}}\in\CH^{1}(\widetilde{M}_{a})
be such that

\cl(h_{\widetilde{M}_{a}})=\cl(Z_{0})(\cl(h_{X}))\in H^{1,1}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(\widetilde{M}_{a})
. Let

(7.5.2)

Z\coloneq q_{X}(h_{X})^{-1}\cdot\left(\widetilde{Z}_{0}-\frac{2}{3}p_{X}^{*}(h_{X}^{3})\cdot p_{\widetilde{M}_{a}}^{*}(h_{\widetilde{M}_{a}})\right),

where
p_{\widetilde{M}_{a}}\colon X\times\widetilde{M}_{a}\to\widetilde{M}_{a}
is the projection. Then Item (1) holds. The proof of Item (2) is analogous.

∎

We are ready to prove Theorem
1.7
. By Theorem
1.3
for
i\in\{1,2\}
there exists a polarized HK manifold

(X_{i},h_{i})
such that
W_{i}\cong\widetilde{M}_{\overline{{\bf w}}_{a}}(X_{i},h_{i})^{\bullet}
. By Lemma
7.9
there exist
Z_{2}\in\CH^{4}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(X_{2}\times W_{2})
such that
\cl(Z_{2})\in H^{6}(X_{2})\otimes H^{2}(W_{2})
equals the isogeny
\phi_{2}\colon H^{2}(X_{2})\to H^{2}(W_{2})
in (
1.1.12
), and

P_{1}\in\CH^{2a_{1}^{2}+2}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(W_{1}\times X_{1})
such that
\cl(P_{1})\in H^{4a_{1}^{2}+2}(W_{1})\otimes H^{2}(X_{1})
equals the inverse
\phi_{1}^{-1}
of the isogeny

\phi_{1}\colon H^{2}(X_{1})\to H^{2}(W_{1})
in (
1.1.12
). By
[
Mar24b
, Thm. 1.1]
there exists an algebraic cycle

V\in\CH^{4}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(X_{1}\times X_{2})
such that the Künneth component
\cl(V)_{6,2}\in H^{6}(X_{1})\otimes H^{2}(X_{2})
equals the Hodge isometry

\phi_{2}\circ f\circ\phi_{1}^{-1}
.
The cycle class
Z\coloneq Z_{2}\circ V\circ P_{1}
(composition of correspondences) satisfies the thesis of Theorem
1.7
.
∎
